## What I learned this week

- Evidence limits: GitHub issues on hahakumquat/calcwise still hold only the daily-report issues (#1-#5), so there is no user feedback. WebSearch returned no Reddit threads for "is there a calculator for" queries (only calculator-request pages and competitor lists), so demand scores for the new items rest on seasonal timing and on which competitor tools exist. Treat demand scores as estimates.
- The finance-ratio vein is exhausted (about 15 shipped this week: ROA, DSCR, P/B and others), so this refresh added 13 items outside finance: Dry January savings, screen time of your life, decimal to fraction, baker's percentage, lean body mass, firewood, pan converter, baseball stats, tile, angle converter, download time, golf handicap, CIDR. None needs a government source; four need a formula or definition citation checked first (ranks 73, 74, 76, 80).
- Time-critical: rank 69 (Dry January) should ship by 2026-12-28 for the January spike; ranks 31 and 40 (IRS 2027 refresh, RMD) are still blocked on irs.gov access and 2026-12-31 deadlines are close. Checked against `src/calculators/` for duplicates: none of the 13 slugs exist. Rows already shipped were moved to Done and the table was renumbered only where rank numbers collided (history ranks 43, 44, 52, 59, 60, 63, 64 are duplicated in Done; harmless).

- 2026-11-24 pass: the buildable queue was down to rank 247, 248 (low-fit filler), 197 and 15 (source checks) after 257-264 shipped, so ranks 265-272 were added (tax-season prep, year-end timing, paycheck cost, winter bills, New Year steps). Best bets are 266 (refund interest forgone, shareable) and 268 (pre-tax contribution cost). Evidence is again seasonal estimates plus one WebSearch confirming the refund-as-loan idea is common in third-party tools; no Reddit, volume or GitHub-issue data.
- 2026-11-26 pass (product-research): ranks 418-426 (specs 393-401) added because the buildable queue was down to ranks 393 and 415. Winter/holiday items found open by grep of `src/calculators/` (about 621 exist; heating, lights, tipping, party drinks, wrapping paper, charity and tax-loss items already exist, so those were skipped). Web sources were not opened this pass, so demand scores are seasonal reasoning only. Ship 423 (advent) by 2026-12-01 and 419 (Hanukkah) and 421 (Santa) in early December.

## Specs for new items

Every spec: copy a neighbour calculator, add `examples` with the hand-checked values below, make FAQ answers match `compute()`, and keep the link to `related` calculators.

1. **Turkey time** (`food`). Inputs: weight (lb, with kg toggle), stuffed yes/no, oven 325 F, fresh or frozen-thawed, serve time. Formula: use the USDA FSIS roasting table at 325 F (unstuffed: 8-12 lb 2.75-3 h, 12-14 lb 3-3.75 h, 14-18 lb 3.75-4.25 h, 18-20 lb 4.25-4.5 h, 20-24 lb 4.5-5 h; copy the stuffed table from the same page, I only saw secondary charts, so verify every row). Output: cook-time range, start time = serve time - time - 30 min rest, and fridge thaw days = ceil(lb/5) to ceil(lb/4) (USDA: 24 h per 4-5 lb, verify). Safe temp 165 F (thigh, breast and stuffing). Example: 16 lb unstuffed -> 3.75-4.25 h; serve 17:00 with a 30-min rest -> out of the oven 16:30, so in the oven 12:15 (4.25 h) to 12:45 (3.75 h). Thaw 16 lb in the fridge -> 4 days (ceil(16/5)=4, ceil(16/4)=4). Sources: USDA FSIS "Roasting turkey" and "Big Bird" thawing pages.
2. **Stacked discounts** (improvement to `finance/discount`). Add: price, discount 1 %, extra discount 2 % (applied after), coupon $ off, sales tax %. Formula: price x (1-d1) x (1-d2) - coupon, then tax. Output: total off % = 1 - (1-d1)(1-d2). Example: $200, 30% then 20% -> $112.00, 44% off, not 50%. Add a social line "30% + 20% is 44%, not 50%".
3. **Gift budget** (`finance`). Inputs: total budget, fixed costs (travel, wrapping), adults, kids, kid weight (default 1.0, optional 1.5, note it is a rule of thumb from one site, not a standard), weeks left, optional already spent. Formula: per adult = (total - fixed) / (adults + kids x weight); per kid = per adult x weight; weekly saving = remaining / weeks. Example: $1,000, $200 fixed, 2 adults, 4 kids at 1.5 -> 800 / 8 = $100 per adult, $150 per kid. Benchmark NRF holiday figures only if linked to NRF itself (sources I saw disagree: $890 vs $628 gifts only).
4. **Savings challenge** (`finance`). Inputs: start amount, weekly increase, weeks (default 52), direction (up/down). Formula: sum of n terms = n/2 x (2a + (n-1)d). Example: $1 start, $1 step, 52 weeks -> $1,378; week 52 deposit $52. Chart: cumulative savings. Optional: add interest-free note.
5. **Rule of 72** (`finance`). Inputs: rate % or target years. Output: 72/rate years (approx) and exact ln 2 / ln(1+r). Example: 8% -> 9.0 years (exact 9.006); 6% -> 12 (exact 11.9). Say 72 is accurate for 6-10%.
6. **Subscription cost** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 10 rows (name, price, monthly/yearly/weekly). Formula: annual = monthly x 12, weekly x 52, yearly x 1. Optional: invest the savings at 7% for 10 years (annuity-immediate, assumption labelled). Example: $10 + $15 + $5 monthly -> $30/month, $360/year; a $120 yearly plan adds $120 -> $480/year, $40/month.
7. **Overtime pay** (`everyday`). Inputs: hourly rate, hours worked, weekly threshold 40, multiplier 1.5, optional holiday hours at 2x. Formula: regular = min(h,40) x rate; OT = max(h-40,0) x rate x mult. Example: $20, 48 h -> 800 + 8 x 30 = $1,040. Note state rules (e.g. daily overtime in CA) and link US DOL FLSA overtime page.
8. **Party food** (`food`). Inputs: guests (adults, kids), meal type. Formula: per-person amounts from a cited table (Butterball suggests about 1-1.5 lb of turkey per person, verify). Example: 10 guests x 1.5 lb -> 15 lb turkey. Add sides in a table; every constant must have a source before merge.
9. **Bill splitter** (`everyday`). Inputs: people with item subtotals, tax $ (or %), tip % (on pre-tax subtotal toggle). Formula: each person's share of tax and tip is proportional to subtotal. Example: A $30, B $20, C $50 (sum $100), tax $8, tip 20% of pre-tax = $20 -> total $128; A $38.40, B $25.60, C $64.00.
10. **Steps to distance** (`health`). Inputs: steps, height, sex. Formula: stride = height x 0.415 (men) or 0.413 (women), a common rule of thumb (cite and label as approximate). Example: 10,000 steps, 175 cm, male -> stride 72.6 cm, 7.26 km, 4.51 mi. Cross-link `calories-burned`.
11. **Plate calculator** (`sports`). Inputs: target weight, bar weight (45 lb or 20 kg), available plates, unit. Formula: per side = (target - bar)/2, greedy from largest plate; flag if not exact. Example: 225 lb, 45 lb bar -> 90 per side = 2 x 45. 135 lb -> 45 per side = one 45.
12. **Tax-loss harvesting** (`finance`). Inputs: realised gains this year, harvestable loss, filing status, ordinary and capital-gains rates. Formula: net = gains - loss; losses offset gains first, then up to $3,000 of ordinary income ($1,500 if married filing separately), rest carries forward (IRC 1211(b); verify on IRS Topic 409). Example: $4,000 gains, $10,000 loss, 24% ordinary, 15% LTCG -> gains tax saved 4,000 x 15% = $600; $3,000 x 24% = $720 deducted; $3,000 carryforward. Add wash-sale warning (30 days).
13. **Heating cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: heater watts, hours per day, $/kWh, furnace gas $/therm, AFUE. Formula: electric heat = kWh x 3,412 BTU; gas cost for the same heat = (BTU / 100,000) / AFUE x $/therm. Example: 1,500 W x 8 h = 12 kWh = 40,944 BTU; at $0.17/kWh cost $2.04; gas at $1.50/therm and AFUE 0.95 -> $0.65 (ratio about 3.2x). Note: a space heater only saves money if the thermostat is lowered. Sources: EIA, DOE Energy Saver. Differs from existing `electricity-cost` and `gas-cost`: compare two heat sources.
14. **Secret Santa** (`other`). Inputs: names (2-30), optional "cannot draw" pairs. Output: a derangement (nobody draws themself), shown one at a time with a reveal so the organiser does not see all. No storage, no sharing. Example check: 3 names -> only the two 3-cycles are valid; 2 names -> A to B, B to A. Test property: no fixed points.
15. **Fuel economy** (`conversion`). Formula: L/100km = 235.215 / mpg (US); km/L = 100 / (L/100km). Example: 30 mpg -> 7.84 L/100km -> 12.75 km/L. Add UK mpg (282.481 / mpg).
16. **Time zone converter** (`everyday`). Inputs: date, time, from zone, to zone (IANA list via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`). Output: converted time, day shift, DST note. Example: 2026-12-24 12:00 America/New_York -> 17:00 Europe/London (GMT, no DST). Check examples in tests without relying on the test machine's locale.
17. **chmod** (`developer`). Inputs: checkbox grid r/w/x for user, group, other, optional setuid/gid/sticky. Output: octal and symbolic. Example: 755 = rwxr-xr-x; 644 = rw-r--r--; 600 = rw-------.
18. **Stair calculator** (`construction`). Inputs: total rise, target riser height, tread depth. Formula: risers = round(rise / target); riser = rise / risers; treads = risers - 1; run = treads x tread. Example: rise 108 in, target 7.5 -> 14 risers (14.4 rounded), 7.71 in each, 13 treads, run at 10 in = 130 in. Check against IRC R311.7 (max riser 7.75 in, min tread 10 in; verify current text).
19. **Latte factor** (`finance`). Inputs: cost per purchase, purchases per week, years, return % (default 7, labelled an assumption), contribution timing. Formula: annual spend = cost x per week x 52; future value = annual x ((1+r)^n - 1) / r (end of year). Example: $5 x 7 x 52 = $1,820 a year (use 365 days -> $1,825); at 7% for 30 years with $1,825 -> $172,391 (1.07^30 = 7.612255). Show spent total $54,750 vs growth. Say plainly it ignores tax, fees and inflation, and that one coffee is not the reason anyone cannot retire. Cross-link `compound-interest`, `subscription-cost`. Sources: standard annuity formula; the "latte factor" is a popularised idea, do not claim a $2 million figure.
20. **Gym cost per visit** (`health`). Inputs: monthly fee, joining fee, months, visits per month, drop-in price. Formula: cost per visit = (fee x months + joining) / (visits x months); break-even visits per month = fee / drop-in. Example: $60 a month, 10 visits -> $6.00 per visit; drop-in $15 -> 60/15 = 4 visits a month. With $40 joining over 12 months and 10 visits a month: (720 + 40) / 120 = $6.33. Share line: "my gym costs $X a visit, i'm cooked". Do not cite unused-membership statistics unless linked to a primary source.
21. **Raise calculator** (`finance`). Inputs: current salary, raise as % or new salary, hours per week (default 40), inflation % (optional), combined marginal tax rate % (optional, user supplied; note it is an estimate). Formula: raise $ = salary x %; monthly = /12; weekly = /52; hourly = salary / (hours x 52); real raise % = (1+raise)/(1+inflation) - 1; after-tax raise = raise $ x (1 - marginal rate). Example: $60,000 at 5% -> $63,000, +$3,000 a year, $250 a month, $57.69 a week; hourly $28.85 -> $30.29 (2,080 h); at 3.3% inflation real raise = 1.05/1.033 - 1 = 1.65%; at 22% federal + 7.65% FICA = 29.65% marginal, after-tax raise = $2,110.50. Link `take-home-pay`, `inflation`, `percentage-change`.
22. **Habit compounding** (`math`). Inputs: daily change % (default 1), days (default 365), direction better/worse. Formula: (1 +/- r)^days. Example: 1.01^365 = 37.78; 0.99^365 = 0.0255 (2.6% left); 1.01^30 = 1.348 (the "only 35% better after a month" point). Chart: curve with a linear-growth line (365% -> 4.65x) for contrast. State that this is a metaphor for effort, not a model of real skill gains.
23. **Year progress** (`everyday`). Inputs: date (default today). Output: day of year, % of year elapsed (day / 365 or 366), days left, weeks left, weekdays left, days to Jan 1. Example: 2026-12-16 -> 16 days to 2027-01-01 (15 left in December plus Jan 1); 2026-12-31 is day 365 of 365 = 100%. Must differ from `date-difference` (presets and the shareable "year is X% done" card). Test with fixed dates, not the machine clock.
24. **NYE party drinks** (`food`). Inputs: guests who drink, hours, drinks per hour model, beer/wine/spirits/bubbly split, non-drinkers. Formula (verify against https://www.omnicalculator.com/food/party-drink before use): first hour 2 drinks, later hours 1 per hour, add 15% buffer; 1 bottle of wine (750 ml) = 5 glasses of 150 ml. Example: 20 drinkers, 5 hours -> 2 + 4 x 1 = 6 drinks each = 120; +15% = 138; if 40% wine = 55.2 drinks -> 11.04 -> 12 bottles. Add a standard-drink note (US 14 g alcohol, NIAAA) and a "no drinking and driving" line. Every constant needs a source.
25. **Christmas lights** (`everyday`). Inputs: strings, watts per string, hours per day, days, $/kWh; second mode: circuit amps (15 or 20), volts 120, other load. Formula: cost = W x h x days / 1,000 x rate; circuit limit = volts x amps x 0.8 (continuous load). Example: 10 strings, 400 W incandescent vs 70 W LED total, 6 h, 45 days, $0.165/kWh -> 108 kWh = $17.82 vs 18.9 kWh = $3.12. Circuit: 120 V x 15 A = 1,800 W, 80% = 1,440 W continuous. Sources: DOE Energy Saver (verify), NEC 80% rule (verify). Note retailer pages disagree on per-string watts, so make watts an input with no hidden defaults beyond labelled examples. Differs from `electricity-cost`: lights-specific presets and circuit check.
26. **Gas-cost extras** (improvement). Add optional tolls, lodging per night x nights, food per day, and show total trip cost and per person. Example: 500 mi round trip at 28 mpg and $3.50 -> 17.857 gal x 3.50 = $62.50 fuel (search page rounded to $63); add $10 tolls -> $72.50; split 2 -> $36.25 each. Keep existing outputs unchanged so `examples` still pass.
27. **Wind chill** (`everyday`). Inputs: temperature, wind speed, unit toggle. Formula (NWS 2001, US units): 35.74 + 0.6215T - 35.75 V^0.16 + 0.4275 T V^0.16, valid only for T at or below 50 F and V above 3 mph; show "not defined" otherwise. Example: 0 F, 15 mph -> -19.4 F (15^0.16 = 1.5423). Convert metric inputs to F and mph first; do not use the Canadian coefficients unless sourced (sources I saw conflict). Source: https://www.weather.gov/ama/WindChill . Note sunshine can raise the effective value.
28. **DTI** (`finance`). Inputs: gross monthly income, housing payment (rent or PITI), other monthly debts (car, student, cards minimums). Formula: front-end = housing / income; back-end = (housing + other debts) / income. Example: income $6,000, housing $1,500, other debts $600 -> front 25.0%, back 35.0% (both inside the 28/36 rule of thumb). State plainly that limits vary by lender and loan type; do not show 43% as a rule until verified at cfpb.gov. Link `home-affordability`, `mortgage`.
29. **Rent split** (`everyday`). Inputs: total rent, share treated as common space (default 0%, or a slider), rooms with name, area (sq ft) and optional $ premium (ensuite, parking). Formula: common = rent x c split equally; private pool = rent - common, split by area; add premiums by moving their cost out of the pool first. Example: $2,000, rooms 150 and 100 sq ft, 40% common -> common $800 = $400 each; private $1,200 -> $720 and $480; totals $1,120 and $880 (sum $2,000). With 0% common -> $1,200 and $800. Say it is a negotiating starting point.
30. **COLA** (`finance`). Inputs: current monthly benefit, COLA % (default 3.6, labelled SSA 2027), optional Medicare Part B premium (user-supplied). Formula: new = old x (1 + COLA). Example: $2,000 -> $2,072 (+$72 a month, +$864 a year); SSA average $2,095 x 1.036 = $2,170.42 (SSA says about $2,170). Note Part B premiums can absorb part of the raise, and the COLA is announced yearly (editable input so it does not go stale).
31. **Commute cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: one-way miles, days per year (default 250), mpg, $/gal, optional parking/tolls per day, one-way minutes, optional per-mile wear cost (user-supplied). Formula: miles = 2 x one-way x days; fuel = miles / mpg x price; hours = 2 x minutes x days / 60. Example: 20 mi one way, 250 days, 25 mpg, $3.20 -> 10,000 mi, 400 gal, $1,280; at 30 min each way, 250 h a year (31 eight-hour days). If citing the IRS mileage rate, read it from irs.gov first.
32. **Dividend** (`finance`). Inputs: starting amount, yield %, dividend growth %, price growth %, years, monthly addition (optional), reinvest yes/no. Simple model (state it): with reinvestment, value grows by (1 + price growth + yield) a year; without, price grows alone and dividends are paid out. Example: $10,000, 4% yield, year-1 income $400; reinvested at 5% price growth + 4% yield for 10 years -> 10,000 x 1.09^10 = $23,673.64 (1.09^10 = 2.367364). Say it ignores tax and yield change.
33. **PTO** (`everyday`). Inputs: days per year, pay periods per year (26, 24, 12), hours per day, days used, days carried over cap, salary (optional). Formula: accrual per period = annual hours / periods; unused value = unused hours x hourly rate. Example: 15 days x 8 h = 120 h / 26 = 4.615 h per paycheck; salary $52,000 / 2,080 = $25 per hour, 5 unused days = 40 h = $1,000. Note payout rules vary by state and employer.
34. **RMD** (`finance`). Inputs: birth year, prior 31 Dec balance, year. Formula: balance / Uniform Lifetime Table factor for age reached in the year; start age 73 (born 1951-1959) or 75 (born 1960+), verify in IRS Pub. 590-B and the IRS RMD page. Factors seen on secondary sites: age 73 = 26.5, 74 = 25.5, 75 = 24.6 (verify every row against Pub. 590-B). Example: $500,000 at age 75 -> 500,000 / 24.6 = $20,325.20. Note the sole-spouse-10-years-younger table is out of scope (show a notice), and the first-RMD April 1 rule.
35. **Charitable deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: filing status, AGI, other itemized deductions, planned gift, gift type. Formula: compare standard deduction ($16,100 single, $32,200 joint for 2026) with itemized total; for itemizers only the gift above 0.5% of AGI counts; non-itemizers may deduct up to $1,000 ($2,000 joint) of cash gifts to public charities (not DAFs), per the 2026 law as reported by CPA blogs: verify at irs.gov before building. Example: single, AGI $100,000, other itemized $14,000, gift $3,000 -> itemized = 14,000 + (3,000 - 500) = $16,500 > $16,100, so itemizing wins by $400. Show tax saved at a user-supplied marginal rate. Park if not verified.
36. **Credit utilization** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 6 cards (balance, limit), target % (default 30, labelled a rule of thumb, not a FICO rule). Formula: utilization = sum(balances) / sum(limits); per card the same; pay-down needed = max(0, balances - target x limits). Example: $1,500 on a $5,000 card and $500 on a $3,000 card -> 2,000 / 8,000 = 25.0%; card 1 is 30%, card 2 16.7%. Target 10% -> allowed 800, pay down $1,200. Say scoring models differ and that this does not predict a score.
37. **Average cost basis** (`finance`). Inputs: lots (shares, price per share), optional current price, optional target average. Formula: average = sum(shares x price) / sum(shares); P/L = (current - average) x shares; shares to buy at price P to reach target T: n = S(A - T) / (T - P), valid only when P < T < A. Example: 100 sh at $50 + 50 sh at $30 = 6,500 / 150 = $43.33. Target $40 from 100 sh at $50 buying at $30: n = 100 x 10 / 10 = 100 shares (check: 8,000 / 200 = $40). Note: average cost is for planning, tax lots (FIFO/specific ID) differ, not tax advice. Link `roi`, `capital-gains-tax`.
38. **BNPL true cost** (`finance`). Inputs: price, down payment, number of payments, payment amount (or fee), period (weekly/biweekly/monthly). Formula: financed = price - down; solve periodic rate r from financed = sum pmt/(1+r)^k (bisection); EAR = (1+r)^periods_per_year - 1; total extra = payments - financed. Hand-checkable examples: 1 payment, financed $100, repay $110, monthly -> r = 10%, EAR = 1.1^12 - 1 = 213.84% (1.1^12 = 3.138428); 4 payments of $100 on $400 -> 0%; 2 monthly payments of $55 on $100 -> r = 6.60% (x = (-55 + sqrt(25025)) / 110 = 0.93812, r = 1/x - 1). Tests use a tolerance for the solved rate. Say plainly that 0% offers are not costless if you would not otherwise buy it, and that late fees are not modelled.
39. **Coffee ratio** (`food`). Inputs: water (g, ml, or oz), ratio 1:N (default 16, labelled a common starting point; range 15-18 is taste, not a standard), strength preset. Formula: coffee g = water g / N; 1 ml water = 1 g; 1 fl oz = 29.5735 ml. Example: 500 ml at 1:16 -> 31.25 g; 12 fl oz (354.88 g) at 1:16 -> 22.18 g. Optional tbsp estimate at about 5 g per level tbsp, labelled approximate. Link `latte-factor` ("a $5 cup is about X per gram at home").
40. **Pizza size comparison** (`everyday`). Inputs: up to 4 pizzas (diameter in or cm, price, count). Formula: area = pi x (d/2)^2; price per sq in = price / (area x count). Example: 12 in = 113.10 sq in, 16 in = 201.06 sq in (1.78x the area: 256/144). $14 for the 12 in = $0.124 per sq in; $22 for the 16 in = $0.109, so the 16 wins. Two 12 in pizzas (226.19 sq in) beat one 16 in. Share line: "a 16 inch is not 4 inches bigger, it is 78% more pizza". Differs from `unit-price` by using area.
41. **Wrapping paper** (`everyday`). Inputs: box length, width, height, overlap (default 1 in), roll width, roll length, number of boxes, unit. Formula (rule of thumb, say so): paper across = 2 x (width + height) + overlap; paper along = length + height; area = across x along; boxes per roll = floor(roll length / along) when the roll width >= across (else try the rotated orientation). Example: 12 x 8 x 4 in -> across 2 x 12 + 1 = 25 in, along 12 + 4 = 16 in, 400 sq in; a 30 in x 15 ft (180 in) roll -> floor(180 / 16) = 11 boxes.
42. **Recipe cost** (`food`). Inputs: rows of ingredient name, package price, package quantity, quantity used (same unit per row, no conversion), servings, optional selling price. Formula: row cost = price x used / package qty; total = sum; per serving = total / servings; margin = (sell - per serving) / sell. Example: flour $4.00 per 80 oz, 32 oz used -> $1.60; eggs $3.60 per 12, 3 used -> $0.90; butter $5.00 per 16 oz, 4 oz -> $1.25; total $3.75, 6 servings -> $0.625 ($0.63) each. Link `recipe-scaler`, `markup`.
43. **Sequence** (`math`). Inputs: type (arithmetic or geometric), first term a1, difference d or ratio r, n. Formulas: arithmetic a_n = a1 + (n-1)d, S_n = n/2 x (a1 + a_n); geometric a_n = a1 r^(n-1), S_n = a1 (r^n - 1)/(r - 1) for r != 1 (S_n = n a1 when r = 1), infinite sum a1 / (1 - r) only when |r| < 1. Example: a1 3, d 4, n 10 -> a_10 = 39, S_10 = 5 x 42 = 210. Geometric a1 2, r 3, n 5 -> a_5 = 162, S_5 = 2 x 242 / 2 = 242. Infinite: a1 8, r 0.5 -> 16. Show the first terms as a table. Link `savings-challenge`, `exponent`.
44. **Dry January** (`everyday`). Inputs: drinks per week, average price per drink, optional calories per drink (user-supplied, default left blank or labelled approximate), days of the challenge (default 31). Formula: weeks = days / 7; saved = drinks x price x weeks; calories avoided = drinks x kcal x weeks. Example: 8 drinks a week at $9, 31 days -> 31/7 = 4.4286 weeks, 35.43 drinks, $318.86 saved; at 150 kcal -> 5,314 kcal. Add "if you kept it up all year: $3,744" (8 x 9 x 52). Do not give medical claims; one neutral line that people dependent on alcohol should talk to a doctor before stopping suddenly. Link `latte-factor`, `subscription-cost`.
45. **Screen time of your life** (`everyday`). Inputs: hours per day, current age, expected age (default 80, labelled an assumption), sleep hours (default 8). Formula: years left = expected - age; screen years = hours/24 x years left; share of waking time = hours / (24 - sleep). Example: 5 h a day, age 25, expected 80 -> 55 years x 5/24 = 11.46 years, 31.25% of waking hours (5/16). Include "swap 1 hour" comparison. Plain, not preachy; say it is arithmetic, not a forecast.
46. **Decimal to fraction** (`math`). Inputs: decimal (terminating or repeating via "bar" toggle), max denominator (default 1000). Terminating: n/10^k reduced by gcd. Repeating: x = 0.(ab) -> ab/99; 0.1(6) = 1/6. Examples: 0.75 -> 3/4; 0.125 -> 1/8; 0.333 repeating -> 1/3; 2.5 -> 5/2 (mixed 2 1/2). For non-terminating input use continued fractions capped by max denominator and label "closest fraction". Reuse the gcd helper from `math/gcf-lcm`.
47. **Baker's percentage** (`food`). Inputs: flour g, water g, salt g, yeast/starter g, optional extras. Formula: each ingredient % = weight / total flour weight x 100; hydration = water / flour. Reverse mode: target hydration and flour -> water. Example: 500 g flour, 350 g water, 10 g salt, 5 g yeast -> 70% hydration, 2% salt, 1% yeast; total dough 865 g. State that starter contributes flour and water (optional 100% hydration starter input: 100 g starter = 50 g flour + 50 g water). Link `recipe-scaler`.
48. **Lean body mass and FFMI** (`health`). Inputs: weight, height, sex, optional body-fat %. Boer (1984): LBM male = 0.407 W(kg) + 0.267 H(cm) - 19.2; female = 0.252 W + 0.473 H - 48.3 (verify coefficients against the paper or a primary source before coding). With body fat %: LBM = W x (1 - bf). FFMI = LBM / H(m)^2; normalised FFMI = FFMI + 6.1 x (1.8 - H(m)). Example: 80 kg, 180 cm, 15% bf -> LBM 68 kg, FFMI 68 / 3.24 = 20.99, normalised 20.99 (height 1.8). Say FFMI "natural limit" claims are debated; show no limit. Link `body-fat`, `tdee`.
49. **Firewood** (`everyday`). Inputs: price per cord (or face cord), stack length, height, depth of log (default 16 in), unit. Formula: full cord = 128 cu ft (4 x 4 x 8 ft); face cord = stack length x height x log depth; face cords per cord = 128 / (stack volume). Example: 8 ft x 4 ft x 16 in (1.333 ft) = 42.67 cu ft = 1/3 cord; at $300 a cord = $100. Compare cost with `heating-cost` (optional $ per MMBtu using a user-supplied BTU per cord). Verify the cord definition on a state or USDA page.
50. **Baking pan converter** (`food`). Inputs: original pan (round diameter, square or rectangular sides, in or cm), new pan, recipe scale. Formula: area ratio = new area / old area (round pi r^2); scale ingredients by that ratio (same depth assumed); note bake time shortens in a wider, shallower pan (no exact formula, say "check early"). Example: 9 in round (63.62 sq in) to 9x13 (117 sq in) -> x1.84; 8 in square (64) to 9 in round (63.62) -> x0.99, same recipe. Link `recipe-scaler`.
51. **Baseball stats** (`sports`). Formulas: AVG = H/AB; OBP = (H+BB+HBP)/(AB+BB+HBP+SF); SLG = TB/AB with TB = 1B + 2 x 2B + 3 x 3B + 4 x HR; OPS = OBP + SLG; ERA = 9 x ER / IP; WHIP = (BB + H) / IP. Example: 150 H, 500 AB, 60 BB, 5 HBP, 5 SF -> AVG .300, OBP 215/570 = .377. Pitcher: 70 ER in 200 IP -> ERA 3.15; (50 BB + 180 H)/200 = 1.15 WHIP. Handle IP in thirds (200.1 = 200 1/3) with a note. Two tabs (batter, pitcher).
52. **Tile** (`construction`). Inputs: room length and width (or area), tile size, grout joint width, waste % (default 10, 15 for diagonal or complex, labelled a rule of thumb), box coverage. Formula: area / (tile + joint)^2 = tiles; add waste; boxes = ceil(tiles / per box). Optional grout estimate left out unless sourced. Example: 10 x 12 ft = 120 sq ft; 12 in tile with 1/8 in joint -> 120 / (12.125/12)^2 = 117.5 -> 118 tiles; +10% = 130 tiles; boxes of 10 -> 13. Link `flooring`, `square-footage`.
53. **Angle converter** (`conversion`). Formulas: rad = deg x pi/180; grad = deg x 10/9; DMS: degrees, minutes = frac x 60, seconds = frac x 3600. Examples: 180 deg = pi rad (3.14159); 90 deg = 100 grad; 45.5 deg = 45 deg 30' 0"; 1 rad = 57.2958 deg. Also turns (deg / 360).
54. **Download time** (`developer`). Inputs: file size (MB/GB/TB), speed (Mbps/MB/s), efficiency % (default 100, optional overhead label). Formula: seconds = size in bits / speed in bits per second; 1 byte = 8 bits; state decimal (1 GB = 10^9 B) vs binary (GiB) choice. Example: 5 GB at 100 Mbps = 40,000 Mb / 100 = 400 s = 6 min 40 s; 1 GiB at 50 Mbps = 8,589.9 Mb / 50 = 171.8 s.
55. **Golf handicap** (`sports`). WHS: score differential = (113 / slope) x (adjusted gross score - course rating - PCC adjustment); index = average of the best 8 of the last 20 differentials (table for fewer rounds). Verify against the USGA/R&A rules text before coding. Example: score 90, rating 71.2, slope 130 -> (113/130) x 18.8 = 16.34. Course handicap = index x slope/113 + (rating - par). Build only the differential and the 20-round index at first.
56. **CIDR** (`developer`). Inputs: IPv4 address and prefix (/0-/32), or mask. Output: netmask, wildcard, network, broadcast, first and last host, usable hosts = 2^(32-p) - 2 (p <= 30; /31 has 2 usable per RFC 3021, /32 has 1). Example: 192.168.1.130/26 -> mask 255.255.255.192, network 192.168.1.128, broadcast 192.168.1.191, hosts .129-.190, 62 usable. Validate octets 0-255; test with fixed inputs. Pure bit math, no network calls.
57. **Body surface area** (`health`). Inputs: height (cm or in), weight (kg or lb). Formula (Mosteller): BSA m^2 = sqrt(height_cm x weight_kg / 3600). Example: 170 cm, 70 kg -> 170 x 70 = 11,900; / 3600 = 3.3056; sqrt = 1.818 m^2 (show 1.82). Convert in x 2.54, lb x 0.45359237 first. Validate positive values; note it is an estimate, not medical advice.
58. **Brine** (`food`). Inputs: water (L or kg; 1 L = 1000 g), salt percent of water weight, optional hours. Formula: salt g = water g x pct / 100; total solution = water + salt; also show salt as percent of solution = salt / (water + salt). Example: 4 L at 5% -> 4000 x 0.05 = 200 g salt; solution 4200 g, 4.76% by solution weight. Label clearly which percent definition is used; no cooking-time claims.
59. **Fence** (`construction`). Inputs: run length (ft or m), section width, closed loop yes/no, price per section, price per post. Formula: sections = ceil(length / width); posts = sections + 1 (open run) or sections (closed loop); cost = sections x section price + posts x post price. Example: 100 ft, 8 ft sections, open -> ceil(12.5) = 13 sections, 14 posts; coverage 104 ft; at $60/section and $15/post = 780 + 210 = $990. Closed loop gives 13 posts, $975.
60. **Coin counter** (`everyday`). Inputs: counts of pennies, nickels, dimes, quarters, half dollars, dollar coins. Formula: total = 0.01p + 0.05n + 0.10d + 0.25q + 0.50h + 1.00c, in integer cents to avoid float error. Example: 100 pennies, 30 nickels, 20 dimes, 10 quarters -> 100 + 150 + 200 + 250 = 700 cents = $7.00 from 160 coins. USD face values only; no weights.
61. **Factorial** (`math`). Inputs: integer n, 0 <= n <= 170 (use BigInt for exact digits; show scientific notation past 20 digits). Outputs: n!, digit count, trailing zeros = floor(n/5) + floor(n/25) + floor(n/125) + ... Examples: 10! = 3,628,800, 7 digits, 2 trailing zeros; 0! = 1; 20! = 2,432,902,008,176,640,000; 100! has 158 digits and 24 trailing zeros. Reject negatives and non-integers.
62. **Quartiles** (`statistics`). Inputs: list of numbers (comma, space or newline separated). Method (state it): sort; Q2 = median; Q1 and Q3 = medians of the lower and upper halves, excluding the median when n is odd. IQR = Q3 - Q1; fences = Q1 - 1.5 IQR and Q3 + 1.5 IQR; points outside are outliers. Example: 2,4,4,5,6,7,8,30 -> Q1 = 4, Q2 = 5.5, Q3 = 7.5, IQR = 3.5, fences -1.25 and 12.75, outlier 30. Needs n >= 4; note other methods give slightly different quartiles.
63. **Dice probability** (`other`). Inputs: number of dice (1-10), sides (2-100), target sum, mode (exactly / at least / at most). Formula: count ways by dynamic-programming convolution of the single-die distribution; probability = ways / sides^dice. Examples: 2d6 sum 7 -> 6/36 = 16.67%; 2d6 at least 10 -> (3+2+1)/36 = 6/36 = 16.67%; 3d6 sum 10 -> 27/216 = 12.5%. Show the distribution as a bar chart. Assumes fair dice.
64. **Word frequency** (`text`). Inputs: pasted text; options: ignore case (default on), strip punctuation, optional minimum word length. Output: total words, unique words, table of top 20 words by count (ties alphabetical). Example: "the cat and the hat and the bat" -> 8 words, 5 unique: the 3, and 2, bat 1, cat 1, hat 1. Runs in the browser only; no stop-word list claims.
65. **Savings rate** (`fire`). Inputs: monthly take-home income (after tax), monthly spending, optional current savings, real return % (default 5, labelled an assumption), withdrawal rate % (default 4, labelled the 4% rule of thumb). Formula: savings rate = (income - spending) / income; FI number = annual spending / withdrawal rate; years to FI = ln(1 + (FI - P) x r / S) / ln(1 + r) with S = annual savings (end-of-year contributions), P = current savings; if r = 0, years = (FI - P) / S. Example: income $5,000, spending $3,500 -> 30.0%; FI = 42,000 x 25 = $1,050,000; S = 18,000; years = ln(3.91667) / ln(1.05) = 1.36524 / 0.04879 = 27.98. Income $5,000, spending $2,500 -> 50%; FI $750,000; years = ln(2.25) / ln(1.05) = 16.62. Chart: years to FI vs savings rate 5-90%. Spending above income -> negative rate, show "spending more than you earn" instead of a curve. Verdict badge for the screenshot. Must differ from `fire/years-to-fi` (lead with the rate; reuse its helper in `helpers/fire.ts`). Link `years-to-fi`, `fire-number`, `budget-50-30-20`.
66. **Sinking fund** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 6 rows (name, amount, months until due), optional annual savings rate % (default 0). Formula: monthly = amount / months; with monthly rate i = APR / 12, monthly = amount x i / ((1 + i)^months - 1) (annuity-immediate). Total per month = sum of rows. Example: Christmas $1,200 in 12 months = $100; car insurance $600 in 6 months = $100; vacation $2,400 in 12 months = $200 -> $400 a month. With 4.8% APR, $1,200 in 12 months -> 1,200 x 0.004 / (1.004^12 - 1) = 4.8 / 0.04907 = $97.82. Say interest is an assumption. Link `savings-goal`, `emergency-fund`, `gift-budget`.
67. **Fasting window** (`health`). Inputs: first meal time, fasting hours (default 16, presets 12, 14, 16, 18, 20), or last meal time (reverse mode). Formula: eating window = 24 - fast hours; last meal = first meal + eating window (mod 24 h); fast starts at last meal and ends at the next first meal. Example: first meal 12:00, 16:8 -> last meal 20:00, fast 20:00 to 12:00 next day; 18:6 -> last meal 18:00. Reverse: last meal 21:00, 16 h fast -> first meal 13:00. Time arithmetic only; no health claims, one neutral line that people who are pregnant, have diabetes or a history of disordered eating should talk to a doctor first. Link `tdee`, `weight-loss-goal`.
68. **Caffeine half-life** (`health`). Inputs: doses (mg and clock time, up to 4; presets for a coffee, espresso, energy drink with labelled approximate mg), half-life in hours (default 5, labelled an average that varies a lot between people), check time. Formula: remaining = sum dose x 0.5^(hours since dose / half-life); time to fall below X mg = half-life x log2(dose / X). Example: 200 mg at 08:00, half-life 5 h, at 22:00 (14 h) -> 200 x 0.5^2.8 = 200 x 0.14359 = 28.7 mg; 95 mg after 5 h -> 47.5 mg; 200 mg falls below 50 mg after 5 x log2(4) = 10 h. Chart: decay curve across the day. Verify the half-life range and the FDA 400 mg a day figure at fda.gov before citing; not a medical claim, doses before the check time only. Link `water-intake`, `sleep`.
69. **Days until holiday** (`everyday`). Inputs: holiday preset (Christmas Dec 25, New Year's Day Jan 1, New Year's Eve Dec 31, Valentine's Day Feb 14, Independence Day Jul 4, Halloween Oct 31, Thanksgiving = 4th Thursday of November) or custom date, "today" (default the browser date, overridable). Formula: whole calendar days between dates using UTC date math (no DST drift); if the date has passed this year, use next year. Output: days, "sleeps", weeks and days. Example: from 2026-11-20 to 2026-12-25 -> 35 days (10 left in November plus 25); from 2026-12-01 -> 24 days; to 2027-01-01 from 2026-12-01 -> 31; Thanksgiving 2026 = 2026-11-26 (Nov 1 is a Sunday, Thursdays 5, 12, 19, 26). Tests pass a fixed "today", never the machine clock. Must differ from `year-progress` and `date-difference` (holiday presets, shareable countdown card). Link `year-progress`, `date-difference`, `gift-budget`.
70. **Reading goal** (`everyday`). Inputs: books goal, average pages per book, days left in the goal (default 365), books already finished, reading speed in pages per hour (user-supplied, default 40, labelled an assumption). Formula: pages left = (goal - finished) x pages; pages per day = pages left / days left; minutes per day = pages per day / speed x 60. Example: 24 books x 300 pages = 7,200 / 365 = 19.73 pages a day; at 40 pages an hour = 29.6 minutes a day. Mid-year: 10 of 24 done, 150 days left -> 14 x 300 = 4,200 / 150 = 28 pages a day. Plain, not preachy. Link `year-progress`, `time-duration`.
71. **Prime factorization** (`math`). Inputs: integer n, 2 <= n <= 10^12 (trial division to sqrt(n); BigInt not required below 2^53). Output: factors as a product with exponents, divisor count = product of (exponent + 1), sum of divisors, list of divisors up to 100 shown. Examples: 360 = 2^3 x 3^2 x 5, 24 divisors; 84 = 2^2 x 3 x 7; 97 is prime; 1001 = 7 x 11 x 13. Reject non-integers and values below 2. Link `prime-number-checker`, `gcf-lcm`.
72. **Distance and midpoint** (`math`). Inputs: two points (x1, y1), (x2, y2), optional z for 3D. Formula: d = sqrt((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2 [+ (z2 - z1)^2]); midpoint = ((x1 + x2)/2, (y1 + y2)/2 [, z]); slope = dy / dx (undefined when dx = 0). Examples: (1, 2) to (4, 6) -> d = 5, midpoint (2.5, 4), slope 4/3; (-3, 4) to (5, -2) -> dx 8, dy -6, d = 10, midpoint (1, 1). Chart: the two points and the segment. Link `slope`, `pythagorean-theorem`.
73. **Retirement countdown** (`fire`). Inputs: birth date, retirement age (default 65, editable), "today" (default browser date, overridable). Formula: retirement date = birth date + age years; days left = UTC date difference (excludes the retirement day); weeks = floor(days / 7); count Mondays and weekend days by iterating dates. Example: born 1990-06-15, retire at 65, today 2026-11-20 -> 2055-06-15, 10,434 days, 1,490 weeks (+4 days), 1,491 Mondays, 2,982 weekend days (7,452 weekdays). If the date has passed show "you made it". Tests pass a fixed today. Differs from `age`. Link `fire-number`, `year-progress`, `years-to-fi`.
74. **Hours of work a purchase costs** (`finance`). Inputs: price, gross hourly wage (or salary / 2,080), tax rate % (user-supplied estimate), hours per workday (default 8). Formula: net hourly = wage x (1 - tax); hours = price / net hourly; workdays = hours / 8. Example: $240, $30 an hour, 20% tax -> net $24 -> 10 hours = 1.25 workdays; $1,200 at $25, 20% -> net $20 -> 60 hours = 7.5 workdays. Plain tone, no scolding. Link `real-hourly-wage`, `cost-per-use`, `salary-to-hourly`.
75. **Annual vs monthly plan** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly price, annual price, optional months you expect to use it. Formula: break-even months = annual / monthly; ceil for whole months; saving over 12 months = 12 x monthly - annual; effective monthly = annual / 12; saving % = saving / (12 x monthly). Example: $15.99 monthly vs $149.99 annual -> 9.38 so pays off from month 10; saving $191.88 - $149.99 = $41.89 (21.8%); effective $12.50 a month. $10 vs $96 -> 9.6 -> month 10, saves $24 (20%). If expected months are below break-even, say monthly is cheaper (e.g. 8 months at $10 = $80 < $96). Link `subscription-cost`, `cost-per-use`.
76. **Cost-of-living salary** (`finance`). Inputs: current salary, cost-of-living index of the current place, index of the new place (both user-supplied from any source they trust, say so), optional offered salary. Formula: equivalent salary = salary x new index / current index; difference % = new / current - 1; if offer given, offer-adjusted = offer x current index / new index. Example: $80,000, index 100 to 135 -> $108,000 (+35%); 100 to 90 -> $72,000 (-10%); offer $100,000 in a 125 city from a 100 city -> $80,000 equivalent. Index is a ratio, not a price; no stored data. Link `inflation`, `take-home-pay`, `rent-affordability`.
77. **Paychecks per year** (`finance`). Inputs: first pay date in the year, frequency (weekly, biweekly, semi-monthly, monthly), year (default 2027), optional annual salary. Formula: biweekly dates = first + 14k within the year; weekly 7k; semi-monthly 24 and monthly 12 fixed. Months with 3 biweekly checks counted from the date list. Example: biweekly starting Fri 2027-01-01 -> 27 paychecks; three-paycheck months Jan (1, 15, 29), Jul (2, 16, 30), Dec (3, 17, 31); weekly from 2027-01-01 -> 53. With $52,000 salary paid over 26 periods ($2,000 each) the 27th is an extra $2,000 gross; if the employer divides by 27, each is $1,925.93 (52,000 / 27). Note employers differ. Tests use fixed dates and UTC math. Link `salary-to-hourly`, `take-home-pay`, `daily-spending-allowance`.
78. **EV vs gas cost per mile** (`everyday`). Inputs: EV efficiency (kWh per 100 mi, default blank, user-supplied), electricity $/kWh, car mpg, gas $/gal, miles per year. Formula: EV cost per mile = kWh per 100 / 100 x rate; gas per mile = price / mpg; annual = per mile x miles; saving = difference. Example: 30 kWh/100 mi at $0.17 -> $0.051 a mile; 28 mpg at $3.50 -> $0.125 a mile; 12,000 mi -> $612 vs $1,500, saving $888. Note home vs public charging price is the user's input and purchase price, insurance and maintenance are excluded. Link `gas-cost`, `electricity-cost`, `commute-cost`.
79. **Time with loved ones** (`everyday`). Inputs: their current age, expected age (default 85, labelled an assumption, not a forecast), visits per year, days per visit, calls per week. Formula: years left = expected - age; visits = visits per year x years; days together = visits x days; calls = calls per week x 52 x years. Example: parent 65, expected 85, 4 visits of 3 days -> 20 years, 80 visits, 240 days (0.66 years); 2 calls a week -> 2,080 calls. Warm, calm tone; add a "if you add one more visit a year" line (+20 visits). Link `screen-time-life`, `year-progress`.
80. **Systems of equations** (`math`). Inputs: a1 x + b1 y = c1 and a2 x + b2 y = c2. Formula (Cramer's rule): D = a1 b2 - a2 b1; x = (c1 b2 - c2 b1) / D; y = (a1 c2 - a2 c1) / D. If D = 0: parallel (no solution) when the equations are inconsistent, else infinitely many. Examples: 2x + y = 7, x - y = 2 -> D = -3, x = 3, y = 1; 3x + 2y = 16, x - y = 2 -> D = -5, x = 4, y = 2; x + y = 2 and 2x + 2y = 5 -> D = 0, no solution; x + y = 2 and 2x + 2y = 4 -> infinitely many. Chart: the two lines and the intersection point. Link `slope`, `quadratic`.
81. **No-spend challenge** (`finance`). Inputs: challenge days (default 30), no-spend days you aim for per week or in total, average daily discretionary spend, days already done. Formula: no-spend days = days x target share; saved = no-spend days x daily spend; per year = saved / days x 365. Example: 30 days, 3 a week (30/7 x 3 = 12.86 days), $25 a day -> $321.43; 21 no-spend days at $20 -> $420. Define "no-spend" for the user (essentials still paid). Link `daily-spending-allowance`, `dry-january`, `latte-factor`.
82. **Zero-based budget** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly income, up to 10 category rows (name, amount). Formula: unassigned = income - sum(rows); percent of income per row = amount / income. Example: income $4,000; rent 1,400, food 500, transport 300, debt 400, savings 600, fun 300 -> total 3,500, unassigned $500 (12.5%). Over-assigned shows a red "over by" figure. Link `budget-50-30-20`, `sinking-fund`.
83. **Sleep debt** (`health`). Inputs: target hours per night (default 8, labelled personal), hours slept for each of 7 nights. Formula: debt = sum(max(0, target - slept)); surplus nights tracked separately; catch-up per night over N nights = debt / N. Example: target 8; slept 6, 6.5, 7, 5, 6, 9, 8 -> deficits 2 + 1.5 + 1 + 3 + 2 = 9.5 h, surplus 1 h, net debt 8.5 h. Calm tone, no medical claims. Link `sleep`.
84. **Food burn-off** (`health`). Inputs: food kcal (presets labelled approximate), weight, activity (walk, run, cycle). Formula: kcal/min = MET x 3.5 x kg / 200; minutes = food kcal / (kcal/min). Example: 250 kcal, 70 kg, walking at MET 3.5 -> 4.288 kcal/min -> 58.3 min. MET values must come from the Compendium of Physical Activities; verify before coding. Say food is not a debt to be "worked off". Link `calories-burned`, `steps-to-distance`.
85. **Study time planner** (`other`). Inputs: days until exam, topics or chapters, hours per day available, chapters done, review share % (default 20, labelled assumption). Formula: chapters left / days = per day; hours per chapter = hours x (1 - review) x days / chapters left. Example: 10 days, 12 chapters, 3 h a day, 2 done, 20% review -> 10 left, 1 a day, 3 x 0.8 x 10 / 10 = 2.4 h per chapter. Link `final-grade`, `gpa`.
86. **Grade curve** (`other`). Inputs: list of scores, max points, method (flat add, scale top score to max, square root). Formulas: flat = score + k, capped at max; scale = score x max / top; sqrt = 10 x sqrt(score) when max is 100. Examples: scores 62, 75, 88 with +5 -> 67, 80, 93; scale with top 88 to 100 -> 70.45, 85.23, 100; sqrt of 64 -> 80. Show before and after; say teachers choose methods differently. Link `weighted-grade`.
87. **Mileage build plan** (`sports`). Inputs: current weekly miles or km, weekly increase % (default 10, labelled a rule of thumb, not medical advice), weeks, optional cutback every 4th week at 80%. Formula: week n = start x (1 + g)^(n-1). Example: 20 mi, 10%, 8 weeks -> 20, 22, 24.2, 26.62, 29.28, 32.21, 35.43, 38.97 (1.1^7 = 1.948717). Chart: weekly volume. Link `pace`, `race-time-predictor`.
88. **Linear equation** (`math`). Inputs: a, b, c for a x + b = c, or a x + b = c x + d. Formula: x = (c - b) / a; two-sided x = (d - b) / (a - c). a = 0: no solution or infinitely many. Examples: 3x + 5 = 20 -> 5; 2x + 3 = 5x - 9 -> x = (-9 - 3) / (2 - 5) = 4; 0x + 2 = 2 -> infinitely many. Show steps. Link `system-of-equations`, `quadratic`.
89. **Cold tire pressure** (`everyday`). Inputs: set pressure (psi, gauge) at a known temperature, that temperature, new temperature, unit toggle (F/C, psi/kPa/bar). Formula (ideal gas, constant volume): P_abs2 = P_abs1 x T2 / T1 with T in Rankine (F + 459.67) and P_abs = gauge + 14.7; show also the 1 psi per 10 F rule of thumb. Example: 32 psi at 70 F, now 20 F -> P_abs1 = 46.7, T1 = 529.67, T2 = 479.67, P_abs2 = 46.7 x 0.905596 = 42.29, gauge 27.59 psi (drop 4.41); rule of thumb says 27.0. Say tires should be inflated to the door-jamb placard pressure when cold; check the real gauge. Link `pressure-converter`.
90. **Pet food duration** (`everyday`). Inputs: bag size (lb or kg), bag price, daily amount in cups, weight of one cup (oz or g, from the package, no default claim), pets optional multiplier. Formula: bag oz = lb x 16; days = bag oz / (cups x oz per cup); cost per day = price / days; per month = per day x 30.4375. Example: 30 lb bag = 480 oz, 2 cups a day at 4 oz a cup = 8 oz -> 60 days; $54 / 60 = $0.90 a day, $27.39 a month, $328.50 a year. Show "reorder by" date from a start date (test with a fixed date). Say feeding amounts come from the vet or the package.
91. **Foreign transaction fee** (`finance`). Inputs: trip spend in home currency, card fee % (default 3, labelled an example; 0 for no-fee cards), number of ATM withdrawals, flat ATM fee each, ATM percent fee, optional local-currency price and exchange rate. Formula: card cost = spend x fee; ATM cost = n x flat + withdrawn x pct; total = card + ATM; effective % = total / spend. Example: $2,000 spend at 3% = $60; 4 ATM withdrawals at $5 flat = $20; total $80 = 4.0% of spend. Rate mode: 500 EUR at 1.08 USD per EUR = $540.00; with 3% fee = $556.20. Compare against a 0% card. Say fees vary by card and bank; read your own terms. Link `tip`, `discount`.
92. **FSA spend-down** (`finance`). Inputs: remaining FSA balance, today (default today), plan deadline (default 12-31), optional carryover amount your plan allows (user-supplied). Formula: days left = deadline - today; weeks left = days / 7; per week = (balance - carryover) / weeks; per day likewise. Example: balance $600, 2026-12-01, deadline 2026-12-31 -> 30 days (4.29 weeks), $140.00 a week, $20 a day; with $200 carryover allowed -> $400 / 4.2857 = $93.33 a week. Warn that deadlines, grace periods and carryover are employer choices; "check your plan documents". Test with fixed dates. Link `hsa-fsa-savings`.
93. **Thermostat setback** (`everyday`). Inputs: current set temperature, setback degrees (default 8, F or C), hours per day at setback (default 8), annual heating bill. Formula (rule of thumb, label it): savings % = min(setback x 1% x hours / 8, 10%) per DOE (about 1% per degree for 8 hours, up to 10% a year); savings $ = bill x %. Example: bill $1,200, setback 8 F for 8 h -> 8% = $96; setback 12 F -> capped 10% = $120. 9 h at 7 F -> 7 x 9/8 = 7.9% = $94.50. Heat-pump warning: needs a thermostat designed for heat pumps. Source: DOE Energy Saver; verify wording on the live page. Link `heating-cost`.
94. **Road trip time** (`everyday`). Inputs: distance (mi or km), average speed, number of stops, minutes per stop, departure time. Formula: drive hours = distance / speed; total = drive + stops x minutes / 60; arrival = departure + total (show day shift). Example: 600 mi at 65 mph = 9.231 h (9 h 14 min); 4 stops x 15 min = 1 h; total 10 h 14 min; depart 07:00 -> arrive 17:14. Add "stop every 2 h" suggestion as a planning default, not a safety rule. Link `gas-cost`, `time-zone-converter`.
95. **School break childcare cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: weekdays off, days covered by PTO or family, kids, mode A hourly sitter (hours per day, rate, kids share one sitter) or mode B camp or care (price per child per day). Formula: paid days = weekdays off - covered; A = paid days x hours x rate; B = paid days x kids x price. Example: 9 weekdays off, 3 covered -> 6 paid days; A: 6 x 8 x $18 = $864; B: 6 x 2 x $60 = $720. Show cost per day and a share line. Rates are inputs only. Link `hours-of-work-cost`, `sinking-fund`.
96. **Dog age in human years** (`everyday`). Inputs: dog age in years (min 0.5, max 20), optional breed size note. Formula: human age = 16 x ln(dog age) + 31 (Wang et al., Cell Systems 2020; mostly Labrador retrievers, say so); also show the old "x 7" rule for contrast. Examples: 1 -> 31.0; 3 -> 16 x 1.098612 + 31 = 48.58; 7 -> 16 x 1.945910 + 31 = 62.13; 12 -> 16 x 2.484907 + 31 = 70.76. Chart: curve of human age vs dog age. Calm tone: a fun estimate, not a health tool. Link `age`.
97. **Christmas tree cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: real tree price, yearly extras (stand, delivery, disposal; default 0), artificial tree price, years you will use it. Formula: artificial per year = price / years; break-even years = ceil(artificial price / (real price + extras)). Example: real $80, artificial $150 over 8 years -> $18.75 a year vs $80; break-even 150 / 80 = 1.875, so year 2; over 10 years real $800 vs artificial $150. State prices and lifespan are the user's; no environmental or safety claims. Link `holiday-lights`, `wrapping-paper`.
98. **Holiday tipping budget** (`everyday`). Inputs: up to 10 rows (who, how many, amount each), optional weeks left, optional monthly income. Formula: total = sum(count x amount); weekly saving = total / weeks; % of income = total / income. Example: cleaner 1 x $100, babysitter 1 x $50, teacher gifts 3 x $25, stylist 1 x $60 -> $285; 6 weeks -> $47.50 a week. Amounts are inputs; do not state what is customary. Link `gift-budget`, `tip`.
99. **Holiday spending total** (`finance`). Inputs: gifts, travel, food, decor, events (and one custom row), last year total, amount put on a card, card APR, monthly payment. Formula: total = sum; change vs last year = total / last - 1; payoff months with r = APR / 12: n = -ln(1 - r x B / P) / ln(1 + r), payments = ceil(n); interest = simulated balance (balance x (1+r), minus min(P, balance)) summed. Example: $600 + $400 + $250 + $80 + $120 = $1,450; last year $1,300 -> +11.5%; card $1,000 at 24% APR, $250 a month -> n = 4.21, 5 payments, total paid $1,053.07, interest $53.07. If P <= r x B say it never clears. Link `gift-budget`, `credit-card-payoff`, `sinking-fund`.
100. **Dinner timeline** (`food`). Inputs: serve time, rows of dish name and minutes of prep plus cook plus rest, optional buffer minutes. Formula: start = serve - minutes - buffer; sort by start. Example: serve 17:00; turkey 285 min (255 cook + 30 rest) -> 12:15; casserole 60 -> 16:00; rolls 25 -> 16:35; earliest start 12:15. Test with fixed times, handle crossing midnight. Note oven space clashes are not checked. Link `turkey-cooking-time`, `party-food-quantity`.
101. **Birthday paradox** (`statistics`). Inputs: group size n (2-100). Formula: P(any match) = 1 - prod_{i=0}^{n-1} (365 - i)/365 (ignores 29 Feb and uneven birthdays; say so); P(someone shares your birthday) = 1 - (364/365)^(n-1). Examples: n = 23 -> 50.73%; n = 30 -> 70.63%; n = 57 -> 99.01%; your birthday with 23 people -> 1 - (364/365)^22 = 5.86%. Chart: P vs n. Link `probability`, `permutation-combination`.
102. **Season pass break-even** (`sports`). Inputs: pass price, day ticket price, days you expect to go, optional extra per-day cost for pass holders (default 0). Formula: break-even days = ceil(pass / (day - extra)); savings = days x day - pass. Example: pass $699, day $130 -> 699 / 130 = 5.38, so 6 days; 8 days -> 1,040 - 699 = $341 saved. Prices are inputs. Link `break-even`, `cost-per-use`.
103. **Round-up savings** (`finance`). Inputs: card purchases per week, average round-up per purchase (default $0.50, labelled an assumption: random cents average about 50 cents), multiplier (1x, 2x, 5x), optional return % and years. Formula: yearly = purchases x round-up x multiplier x 52; future value = yearly x ((1+r)^n - 1) / r. Example: 20 x $0.50 x 52 = $520 a year; at 7% for 10 years: 520 x 13.8164 = $7,184.55 vs $5,200 contributed (1.07^10 = 1.967151). Say it ignores tax and fees. Link `savings-challenge`, `latte-factor`.
104. **Drive vs fly cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: round-trip miles, mpg, gas price, tolls and parking, travelers, fare per person, bag fees per person, airport transport, optional wear cost per mile (user-supplied, default 0), optional door-to-door hours each way. Formula: drive = miles / mpg x gas + tolls + miles x wear; fly = travelers x (fare + bags) + transport. Example: 1,000 mi, 28 mpg, $3.20 -> 35.714 gal = $114.29, + $20 tolls = $134.29; fly 4 x ($250 + $60) = $1,240 + $80 transport = $1,320; difference $1,185.71, drive $33.57 a person. Do not quote fares or the IRS mileage rate. Link `gas-cost`, `road-trip-time`.
105. **Car lease vs buy** (`finance`). Inputs: price, down payment, loan APR, loan months, resale value at end; lease payment, lease months, due at signing. Formula: loan payment = P r / (1 - (1+r)^-n), r = APR/12; buy net cost = down + payment x n - resale; per month = net / n; lease per month = (signing + payment x months) / months. Example: $35,000, $5,000 down, 6%, 60 months, resale $18,000 -> payment $579.98 (1.005^60 = 1.348850), paid $5,000 + $34,798.80 = $39,798.80, net $21,798.80 = $363.31 a month; lease $400 x 36 + $2,000 = $16,400 = $455.56 a month; difference $92.25. Say insurance, fees, mileage limits and tax are excluded. Link `auto-loan`, `loan-comparison`.
106. **Credit card annual fee break-even** (`finance`). Inputs: annual fee, yearly spend on the card, rewards rate % (value, not points), comparison no-fee card rate %, optional first-year bonus value (default 0). Formula: net = spend x rate - fee; advantage = net - spend x comparison; break-even spend = fee / (rate - comparison). Example: fee $95, spend $12,000, 2% vs 1% -> rewards $240, net $145, no-fee $120, advantage +$25; break-even $9,500. If rate <= comparison, say it never breaks even. Rates are the user's; no card names. Link `credit-card-payoff`, `cost-per-use`.
107. **Trip budget** (`everyday`). Inputs: total budget, already-paid costs (flights, lodging), days, buffer % (default 10, labelled a choice). Formula: remaining = total - paid; usable = remaining x (1 - buffer); per day = usable / days. Example: $2,000 - $1,200 = $800; buffer 10% -> $720; 7 days -> $102.86 a day (no buffer $114.29). Negative remaining shows over budget. Link `sinking-fund`, `foreign-transaction-fee`.
108. **Airport leave time** (`everyday`). Inputs: departure time, minutes at the airport before departure (user choice, no default claim beyond an editable 120), drive or transit minutes, parking or shuttle minutes, extra buffer. Formula: leave = departure - (airport + travel + parking + buffer). Example: 14:30, 120 + 35 + 20 + 15 = 190 min (3 h 10 min) -> leave 11:20. Handle crossing midnight (departure 01:00 -> previous day 21:50). Say check your airline for its own check-in cut-offs. Test with fixed times. Link `road-trip-time`, `time-zone-converter`.
109. **Party seating** (`everyday`). Inputs: guests, seats per table, spare chairs % (default 10, a choice), floor space per guest (sq ft, input). Formula: tables = ceil(guests / seats); seats = tables x seats; spare seats = seats - guests; chairs = ceil(guests x (1 + spare)); floor = guests x space. Example: 26 guests, 6 per table -> 5 tables, 30 seats, 4 spare; 8 per table -> 4 tables, 32 seats, 6 spare; 10% spare -> ceil(28.6) = 29 chairs; 10 sq ft each -> 260 sq ft. Link `party-food-quantity`, `party-drinks`.
110. **Daily interest** (`finance`). Inputs: balance, APR %, day-count basis (365 default or 360). Formula: daily rate = APR / basis; per day = balance x daily rate; per 30.4167-day month = per day x 365 / 12 (basis 365); simple year = balance x APR. Example: $5,000 at 24% -> 0.065753% a day = $3.29 (3.28767); month $100.00; year $1,200; on a 360 basis $3.33 a day. Say compounding and daily balances change the real figure and the card agreement is the authority. Link `credit-card-payoff`, `apr`.
111. **Pet lifetime cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: yearly food, vet, insurance or meds, grooming, supplies; one-time setup or adoption; years; optional yearly cost growth % (default 0). Formula: yearly = sum; total = setup + yearly x years, or with growth g: yearly x ((1+g)^years - 1) / g; per month = total / (12 x years). Example: 600 + 400 + 300 + 150 + 100 = $1,550; setup $300; 12 years -> $18,900 = $131.25 a month; with 3% growth: 1.03^12 = 1.425761, 1,550 x 14.19203 = $21,997.65 + $300 = $22,297.65. Do not quote average pet costs. Link `pet-food-duration`, `sinking-fund`.
112. **Year in review return (Modified Dietz)** (`finance`). Inputs: start balance, end balance, net contributions during the year, average timing (default mid-year, weight 0.5). Formula: gain = end - start - contributions; return = gain / (start + weight x contributions); contributions' share of growth shown separately. Example: 20,000 -> 27,000 with 4,800 added -> gain $2,200; denominator 20,000 + 2,400 = 22,400; return 9.82% (naive (27,000 - 20,000) / 20,000 = 35% overstates). Say it is an approximation of money-weighted return, not tax or fee advice. Link `cagr-calculator`, `net-worth`.
113. **Windfall split** (`finance`). Inputs: amount after tax, percent to each of: emergency fund, debt, invest, fun, gifts (must total 100, show remainder). Output: dollars per bucket, and what the invest share grows to in 10 and 30 years at an input return (compound formula). Example: $3,000 at 40/30/20/10 -> $1,200/$900/$600/$300; $600 at 7% for 10 years = $1,180.29. Not financial advice note; link `bonus-tax`, `compound-interest`.
114. **Severance estimate** (`finance`). Inputs: weekly or annual pay, years of service, weeks per year (default 2, labelled a common rule of thumb, not a legal minimum), optional cap in weeks, unused PTO days. Formula: weeks = min(years x rate, cap); pay = weeks x weekly pay + PTO days x daily pay. Example: $78,000/yr (=$1,500/wk), 6 years x 2 = 12 weeks = $18,000. Output also shows how many months of expenses it covers (input). Say taxes and agreements vary; link `savings-runway`.
115. **Quit smoking savings** (`health`). Inputs: packs (or pods) per day, price each, optional return rate. Output: per day, week, month, year, 5 and 10 years, and invested value. Example: 1 pack/day at $9 = $9/day = $3,285/yr (365 days); $3,285 yearly at 7% for 10 years via annuity = $45,387. No health claims.
116. **Balance transfer break-even** (`finance`). Inputs: balance, current APR, transfer fee %, promo months, monthly payment, APR after promo. Compare two month-by-month amortisations over promo months; saving = interest avoided - fee. Example: $5,000 at 24% vs 0% for 18 months, 3% fee ($150), pay $300/mo: hand-check interest over 18 months in the test. Show break-even month and warn when payment is too low to clear the balance. Link `debt-payoff-planner`, `credit-card-payoff`.
117. **Job offer comparison** (`finance`). Inputs per offer (A/B): base salary, bonus %, 401k match %, equity per year, PTO days, commute cost per year, other benefits value. Total = base + bonus + match + equity - commute; also value per working day after PTO. Example: A $100,000 + 10% + 4% match ($4,000) = $114,000; B $108,000 + 0 + 3% ($3,240) = $111,240; difference $2,760. State all values are the user's estimates.
118. **Annual goal pace** (`everyday`). Inputs: goal amount, unit label, start date (default Jan 1), end date (default Dec 31), progress so far. Output: per day, per week, per month needed; on-track marker vs elapsed share; projected finish. Example: 1,000 miles in 365 days = 2.74/day = 19.18/week; after 90 days at 200 done -> expected 246.6, behind by 46.6.
119. **Points value** (`finance`). Inputs: cash price, taxes/fees paid in points booking, points needed, optional baseline cents per point. Formula: cpp = (cash - fees) / points x 100. Example: $450 flight, $11 fees, 25,000 points -> (439/25,000) x 100 = 1.756 cents per point. Verdict versus the user's baseline, not a program valuation.
120. **Cookie exchange planner** (`food`). Inputs: guests, dozens each person brings or takes home, cookies per batch, dough ingredient quantities per batch (butter, flour, sugar), batches per baking sheet. Output: total cookies, batches (rounded up), sheets, ingredient totals. Example: 12 guests x 2 dozen take-home = 288 cookies; 36 per batch = 8 batches; butter 1 cup per batch = 8 cups. Link `recipe-scaler`, `party-food-quantity`.
121. **Home office deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: office sq ft, home sq ft, annual home expenses (rent or mortgage interest, utilities, insurance, repairs), marginal tax rate. Formula: simplified = min(office, 300) x $5; actual = office / home x expenses; better = max; tax saved = better x rate. Example: 150 sq ft office, 1,500 sq ft home, $24,000 expenses -> simplified 150 x 5 = $750; actual 10% x 24,000 = $2,400; actual wins by $1,650; at 22% saves $528. Cap check: 400 sq ft -> simplified uses 300 = $1,500. Warn: exclusive and regular use; employees cannot deduct; not tax advice.
122. **Business mileage deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: business miles, rate per mile (default = IRS standard rate, labelled with its year, editable), marginal rate. Formula: deduction = miles x rate; tax saved = deduction x rate. Example: 8,000 miles x $0.70 = $5,600; at 22% saves $1,232. Also show per 1,000 miles = $700. Warn: commuting is not business mileage; keep a log.
123. **Charitable giving tax value** (`finance`). Inputs: cash gifts per year, other itemized deductions per year, standard deduction (input), marginal rate. Formula: itemized = gifts + other; extra deduction = max(0, itemized - standard); tax saved = extra x rate. Bunching: year A itemized with two years of gifts, year B standard; compare total extra deduction. Example: gifts $5,000, other $12,000, standard $15,000, 24% -> itemized 17,000; extra 2,000; saves $480 a year. Bunching: A = 10,000 + 12,000 = 22,000 -> extra 7,000; B = standard -> 0; total 7,000 vs 2 x 2,000 = 4,000; bunching adds 3,000 x 24% = $720. Gifts $3,000 -> itemized 15,000, extra 0, saves $0.
124. **Estimated tax safe harbor** (`finance`). Inputs: last year total tax, last year AGI, expected tax this year, tax withheld to date, estimated payments made. Formula: prior-year target = last tax x (1.10 if AGI > 150,000 else 1.00); required = min(0.90 x expected, prior-year target); Q4 due = max(0, required - withheld - paid). Example: last tax 20,000, AGI 180,000 -> 22,000; expected 30,000 -> 90% = 27,000; required 22,000; withheld 15,000 + paid 3 x 2,000 = 21,000; Q4 = $1,000. Note: simplified (withholding is treated as paid on time; no per-quarter penalty test; MFS threshold is $75,000).
125. **Mortgage interest paid this year** (`finance`). Inputs: current balance, annual rate, remaining months, months of payments in the year (default 12). Formula: r = rate / 12; payment = B r / (1 - (1+r)^-n); loop months: interest = balance x r; principal = payment - interest. Example: $300,000, 6.5%, 360 months -> payment $1,896.20; interest over 12 months $19,401.27; principal $3,353.18 (12 x 1,896.20 = 22,754.45 = 19,401.27 + 3,353.18); ending balance $296,646.82. Warn: your lender's 1098 is authoritative; $750,000 debt cap; must itemize.
126. **Credits to graduate** (`other`). Inputs: credits required, completed, in progress, credits per term, terms per year (default 2), optional tuition per credit. Formula: remaining = required - completed - in progress; terms = ceil(remaining / per term); years = terms / terms per year; cost = remaining x tuition. Example: 120 required, 45 done, 15 in progress, 15 per term -> 60 left, 4 terms, 2 years; at $400 per credit $24,000. At 12 per term: 60 / 12 = 5 terms, 2.5 years.
127. **Dimensional weight** (`everyday`). Inputs: length, width, height (in), actual weight (lb), divisor (default 139, editable and labelled), optional rate per lb. Formula: dim weight = L x W x H / divisor; billable = ceil(max(actual, dim)). Example: 20 x 14 x 10 = 2,800 / 139 = 20.14 lb; actual 12 -> billable 21 lb (dimensional). 12 x 10 x 8 = 960 / 139 = 6.91 lb; actual 9 -> billable 9 lb (actual). Tip: a smaller box can cut the bill.
128. **Fuel tank runtime** (`everyday`). Inputs: tank size (gal), gauge level %, energy per gallon (BTU, default 91,500 for propane, labelled and editable; a heating oil option about 138,500 to be verified), appliance BTU per hour, hours running per day, optional price per gallon. Formula: gallons = size x level; days = gallons x BTU per gal / (BTU per hr x hours); cost per day = BTU per hr x hours / BTU per gal x price. Example: 500 gal at 40% = 200 gal x 91,500 = 18,300,000 BTU; furnace 60,000 BTU/h x 8 h = 480,000 per day -> 38.1 days. Warn: gauges are approximate; order before 20%.
129. **Rent increase impact** (`finance`). Inputs: current rent, new rent (or increase %), months in lease, take-home pay. Formula: rise = new - old; pct = rise / old; yearly = rise x 12; share of take-home = new / pay. Example: $1,500 to $1,620 -> +$120/month, 8.0%, $1,440 a year.
130. **Stock option exercise** (`finance`). Inputs: shares, strike, current FMV. Formula: cost = shares x strike; value = shares x FMV; spread = value - cost. Example: 1,000 shares, $5 strike, $12 FMV -> cost $5,000, value $12,000, spread $7,000. State: no tax computed.
131. **Eating out cost** (`finance`). Inputs: meals out per week, average price, cooked cost per meal. Formula: yearly out = n x 52 x price; saved = n x 52 x (price - cooked). Example: 4 x $21 -> $4,368 a year; cooked at $6 -> save $3,120.
132. **Car affordability** (`finance`). Inputs: gross monthly income, down payment %, term, APR. Formula: payment cap = 10% of gross monthly; max loan = PV of payment cap over term; price = loan / (1 - down %). Example: $5,000 gross, 20% down, 48 months, 0% APR -> cap $500, loan $24,000, price $30,000.
133. **Side hustle goal** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly target, price per unit, cost per unit, fees %. Formula: profit per unit = price x (1 - fee) - cost; units = ceil(target / profit). Example: $500 target, $25 price, $8 cost, 10% fee -> $14.50 profit, 35 units a month.
134. **Early withdrawal penalty** (`finance`). Inputs: amount, marginal tax %, state tax %. Formula: penalty = 10% x amount; tax = amount x rate; net = amount - penalty - tax. Example: $10,000, 22% -> $1,000 penalty, $2,200 tax, $6,800 net. Verify rule on the IRS page first.
135. **Cash-back rewards** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly spend and rate per category, annual fee. Formula: yearly = 12 x sum(spend x rate) - fee. Example: $600 at 3%, $400 at 1% -> $22/month, $264 a year, less $95 fee = $169.
136. **Yield on cost** (`finance`). Inputs: shares, cost basis per share, annual dividend per share, dividend growth %, years. Formula: YOC = dividend / cost; after n years dividend x (1+g)^n / cost. Example: $50 cost, $2 dividend, 6% growth, 10 years -> 4.0% now, 7.2% in 10 years (3.58 / 50 = 7.16%).
137. **Lifestyle creep** (`finance`). Inputs: annual raise after tax, share absorbed by spending %, return %, years. Formula: saved = raise x (1 - share); future value = saved x ((1+r)^n - 1) / r (end-of-year deposits, labelled). Example: $4,800 raise, 60% absorbed, 7%, 10 years -> $2,880 spent, $1,920 saved, $26,528 later; saving all of it would be $66,319.
138. **Salary needed** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly spending, savings % of take-home, effective tax rate %. Formula: net = spending / (1 - save%); gross monthly = net / (1 - tax); annual = 12 x gross. Example: $4,000, 20%, 22% -> net $5,000, gross $6,410.26 a month, $76,923 a year. Say tax is a flat estimate.
139. **Black Friday deal check** (`finance`). Inputs: sale price, "was" price, typical price you saw. Formula: claimed % = (was - sale) / was; real % = (typical - sale) / typical. Example: was $200, sale $140, typical $160 -> claimed 30%, real 12.5%, real saving $20.
140. **Free shipping threshold** (`finance`). Inputs: cart total, threshold, shipping fee, filler item price. Formula: gap = threshold - cart; net filler cost = gap - fee. Example: $38 cart, $50 threshold, $6.99 fee -> gap $12.00, net cost of a $12 filler $5.01 (pay $44.99 vs $50.00); worth it only if you want the item.
141. **Cost per class** (`other`). Inputs: course cost, meetings per week, weeks, minutes per meeting. Formula: meetings = per week x weeks; per class = cost / meetings; per hour = per class / (minutes / 60). Example: $1,800, 2 a week, 15 weeks, 50 min -> 30 meetings, $60 per class, $72 per hour.
142. **Paycheck bill planner** (`finance`). Inputs: pay dates (day of month), take-home per check, bill list (name, amount, due day). Rule: each bill comes from the latest paycheck on or before its due day (wrap to the last check of the previous month if none). Example: pay days 1 and 15, $1,600 each; bills $1,200 (day 1), $150 (5), $100 (10), $80 (20), $60 (28) -> check 1 carries $1,450, leaves $150; check 15 carries $140, leaves $1,460.
143. **Workout volume** (`health`). Inputs: exercises (sets, reps, weight), unit lb or kg, sessions per week. Formula: volume = sets x reps x weight; kg = lb x 0.45359237. Example: bench 4 x 8 at 135 = 4,320; squat 5 x 5 at 185 = 4,625; total 8,945 lb (4,057 kg); 3 sessions -> 26,835 lb a week.
144. **Sourdough starter feeding** (`food`). Inputs: grams needed for the recipe, grams to keep back, feed ratio starter:flour:water. Formula: total = needed + keep; seed = total / (1 + f + w); flour = seed x f; water = seed x w. Example: 200 + 20 = 220 g at 1:2:2 -> 44 g starter, 88 g flour, 88 g water, 100% hydration.
145. **Employer 401(k) match** (`finance`). Inputs: salary, your contribution %, match rate % (e.g. 50 or 100), match cap (% of salary). Formula: matched contribution = min(your %, cap %) x salary; match = matched contribution x rate; missed = (cap% x salary x rate) - match; to-capture % = cap. Example: $80,000, you 3%, 100% match up to 6% -> match $2,400, missed $2,400, raise contribution to 6%.
146. **Resale profit** (`finance`). Inputs: sale price, platform fee %, fixed fee $, shipping paid by seller, item cost, hours spent. Formula: fees = price x pct + fixed; profit = price - fees - shipping - cost; margin = profit / price; per hour = profit / hours. Example: $60, 12%, $0.30, $7 ship, $15 cost, 1.5 h -> fees $7.50, profit $30.50, margin 50.8%, $20.33 an hour.
147. **Gig driver profit per hour** (`finance`). Inputs: gross earnings, tips, hours (including waiting), miles, mpg, fuel price, other per-mile cost (wear, default 0, user-set). Formula: fuel = miles / mpg x price; costs = fuel + miles x other; net = gross + tips - costs; per hour = net / hours. Example: $240 + $40 tips, 8 h, 150 mi, 25 mpg, $3.50, $0.10 -> fuel $21.00, wear $15.00, net $244.00, $30.50 an hour.
148. **Weight change rate** (`health`). Inputs: start weight, current weight, days between (unit lb or kg). Formula: change = current - start; per week = change / days x 7; percent per week = per week / start x 100. Example: 200 lb to 194 lb in 21 days -> -6 lb, -2.0 lb a week, -1.0% a week. Neutral wording, no health thresholds.
149. **Event cost per guest** (`everyday`). Inputs: guests, cost lines (food, drinks, decor, venue, other), alternate guest count. Formula: total = sum of lines; per guest = total / guests; fixed lines (venue, decor) do not scale, variable lines (food, drinks) do, flagged per line. Example: food $300 and drinks $150 variable, venue $200 fixed, 25 guests -> $650, $26.00 each; at 40 guests $920, $23.00 each.
150. **Group gift split** (`everyday`). Inputs: gift price, tax %, shipping, card cost, contributors. Formula: total = (price x (1 + tax) + shipping + card); each = ceil(total / n x 100) / 100; overage = each x n - total. Example: $90, 8%, $6 ship, $4 card, 7 people -> total $107.20, each $15.32, overage $0.04.
151. **Delivery app true cost** (`finance`). Inputs: menu price in store, app markup %, delivery fee, service fee, tip % (on in-app food), tax % (on in-app food), orders per week. Formula: food = menu x (1 + markup); tax = food x tax%; tip = food x tip%; total = food + tax + tip + delivery + service; pickup = menu x (1 + tax%); premium = total - pickup; yearly = premium x orders x 52. Example: $20, 15%, 8%, 18%, $3.99, $2.50 -> food $23.00, tax $1.84, tip $4.14, total $35.47; pickup $21.60; premium $13.87 (64.2%); 2 a week -> $1,442.48 a year. Label tax and tip bases as assumptions.
152. **Remote work savings** (`finance`). Inputs: office days replaced per week, round-trip miles, cost per mile, daily parking and tolls, extra daily lunch and coffee, round-trip commute minutes, work weeks (default 48), extra home cost per month. Formula: per day = miles x rate + parking + lunch; yearly = per day x days/week x weeks - home cost x 12; hours = minutes x days x weeks / 60. Example: 3 days, 20 mi at $0.30, $8, $12, 60 min, $40 home -> $26 a day, $3,744 gross, $3,264 net, 144 hours. Cost per mile is the user's number (link `gas-cost`).
153. **DIY vs hire** (`finance`). Inputs: hire quote, materials, tools to buy, DIY hours, your hourly value. Formula: DIY cost = materials + tools + hours x rate; savings = quote - DIY cost; break-even rate = (quote - materials - tools) / hours. Example: $600, $120, $80, 8 h, $25 -> DIY $400, saves $200, break-even $50 an hour. If break-even <= 0, say DIY is dearer even at free time.
154. **Rebate vs low APR** (`finance`). Inputs: price, down payment, term months, cash rebate, dealer APR %, your bank APR %. Formula: A = finance (price - down) at dealer APR; B = finance (price - down - rebate) at bank APR; payment = P x i / (1 - (1+i)^-n), i = APR/12 (n/APR 0 handled); total = payment x n + down. Example: $30,000, $3,000 down, 60 months, $2,000 rebate, 0% vs 6% -> A $450.00/mo, loan total $27,000; B $483.32/mo, loan total $28,999.20; A wins by about $1,999. Neutral wording; ignore trade-in and tax.
155. **Lease break cost** (`finance`). Inputs: months remaining, monthly rent, fee clause in months of rent (0 if none), months until re-let, re-letting fee. Formula: fee route = fee months x rent; re-let route = months until re-let x rent + re-letting fee; stay = remaining x rent (reference). Example: 8 months, $1,500, 2 months fee, 2 months to re-let, $750 fee -> fee route $3,000; re-let route $3,750; staying costs $12,000. Say terms vary by lease and local law; not legal advice.
156. **Energy upgrade payback** (`everyday`). Inputs: units, old watts, new watts, hours per day, price per kWh, total upgrade cost. Formula: kWh a year = (old - new) / 1000 x units x hours x 365; saved = kWh x price; payback months = cost / saved x 12. Example: 10 bulbs, 60 W to 9 W, 3 h, $0.16, $30 -> 558.45 kWh, $89.35 a year, payback 4.0 months. Link `electricity-cost`.
157. **Mortgage recast** (`finance`). Inputs: balance, rate %, months remaining, lump sum. Formula: payment = B x i / (1 - (1+i)^-n); recast payment uses B - lump over the same n; saving per month = difference; total interest = payment x n - balance. Example: $300,000, 6.5%, 300 months, $50,000 -> $2,025.62 down to $1,688.02 (-$337.60 a month); interest $307,686 vs $256,405 (-$51,281). Say lenders set fees and minimums; compare with `extra-mortgage-payment`.
158. **Home sale net proceeds** (`finance`). Inputs: sale price, mortgage payoff, agent commission %, other seller closing cost %, repairs and credits $; optional target net. Formula: net = price x (1 - commission% - closing%) - payoff - credits; price for target = (target + payoff + credits) / (1 - commission% - closing%). Example: $400,000, $250,000, 5%, 1.5%, $3,000 -> costs $26,000, net $121,000; target $121,000 -> $400,000. Capital gains tax excluded and stated.
159. **Rental cash-on-cash return** (`finance`). Inputs: price, down payment %, closing costs, rehab, monthly rent, vacancy %, monthly operating costs, loan rate %, term years. Formula: cash in = down + closing + rehab; NOI = rent x 12 x (1 - vacancy) - opex x 12; debt service = 12 x P x i / (1 - (1+i)^-n); CoC = (NOI - debt service) / cash in. Example: $250,000, 20%, $7,500, $5,000, $2,200, 5%, $700, 6.5%, 30 years -> cash in $62,500; NOI $16,680; debt service $15,169.63; cash flow $1,510.37; 2.42%.
160. **Protein cost per gram** (`health`). Inputs: for products A and B: price, servings or units, protein per serving (g); daily protein target (g). Formula: g = servings x protein; $/g = price / g; per 100 g = $/g x 100; daily cost = $/g x target. Example: A $24.99, 30 servings, 24 g -> 720 g, $0.0347/g, $3.47 per 100 g, $4.86 a day at 140 g. B $12.00 for 12 eggs at 6 g -> $0.1667/g. Protein values are the user's; no health claims. Link `protein`.
161. **GPA needed this term** (`other`). Inputs: current cumulative GPA, credits earned, credits this term, target cumulative GPA, scale max (4.0). Formula: needed = (target x (earned + term) - current x earned) / term; max reachable = (current x earned + max x term) / (earned + term). Example: 3.20 over 60 credits, 15 credits, target 3.30 -> 3.70 needed; max reachable 3.36; target 3.40 -> 4.20, say not reachable this term. Schools weight differently; note that.
162. **Textbook rent vs buy vs resell** (`other`). Inputs: per option (new, used, rent, digital): price, shipping, expected resale, resale chance %. Formula: net = price + shipping - resale x chance. Example: new $150, resale $60 -> $90; used $100, resale $40 -> $60; rent $55 + $8 return shipping -> $63; digital $70 -> $70; used wins by $3 over rent. Warn about late and damage fees (not modelled).
163. **Shower cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: flow gpm (2.1), minutes, showers per week, cold and hot temp F (55, 115), heater efficiency % (100 electric), $/kWh, water+sewer $/1,000 gal. Formula: gal = gpm x min; kWh = gal x 8.34 x dT / 3,412 / eff; cost = kWh x price + gal / 1,000 x water price. Example: 2.1 gpm, 8 min, 55 to 115, 100%, $0.16, $6 -> 16.8 gal, 2.46 kWh, $0.39 heat + $0.10 water = $0.50 a shower; 7 a week -> $180.19 a year (364 showers). Gas heaters: user enters efficiency and gas price per kWh-equivalent (29.3 kWh per therm, label it).
164. **Meal kit vs groceries** (`food`). Inputs: kit price, shipping, servings per box, grocery cost of the same recipes, food waste %, minutes saved per box, boxes per year (52).
