Formula: kit/serving = (price + ship)/servings; grocery/serving = cost x (1 + waste) / servings; premium = kit - groceries per box; per hour = premium / (minutes / 60). Example: $69.99, $0, 6 servings, $38 groceries, 15% waste, 60 min -> $11.67 vs $7.28 a serving; premium $26.29 a box; $1,367.08 a year; $26.29 per hour saved.
165. **Warehouse membership break-even** (`finance`). Inputs: annual fee, yearly spend there, savings % vs usual store, unplanned extra spend. Formula: saved = spend x pct; net = saved - fee - extra; break-even spend = fee / pct. Example: $65, $1,800, 12%, $80 extra -> saved $216, net $71; break-even $541.67 a year ($45.14 a month). Savings % is the user's estimate.
166. **Keep or replace the car** (`finance`). Inputs: annual repairs, annual maintenance (old), new monthly payment, extra insurance per year, maintenance (new). Formula: keep = repairs + maintenance; replace = 12 x payment + extra insurance + new maintenance; difference = replace - keep; break-even repairs = replace - old maintenance. Example: $2,400 + $600 = $3,000; $5,400 + $600 + $300 = $6,300; keep saves $3,300 ($275 a month); repairs would need to hit $5,700 a year. Cash view only: ignores down payment, trade-in and reliability; say so. Link `car-affordability`.
167. **401(k) loan true cost** (`finance`). Inputs: loan amount, loan rate %, term years, assumed market return %. Formula: payment = P x i / (1 - (1+i)^-n), monthly; stay invested = P x (1+m)^n; repay path = payment x ((1+m)^n - 1)/m; cost = difference. Example: $20,000, 8.5%, 5 years, 10% -> $410.33 a month, interest $4,619.84 (paid to yourself); stay invested $32,906 vs repay path $31,775 -> $1,131 lost growth. If return < rate the loan comes out ahead; say so. State that plan limits and leaving-job repayment rules vary; give no numbers.
168. **Laundry cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: loads per week, washer kWh per load, dryer kW, dryer minutes, $/kWh, water gal per load, water+sewer $/1,000 gal, detergent $ per load. Formula: energy = washer kWh + dryer kW x min / 60; per load = energy x price + gal / 1,000 x water price + detergent; year = per load x loads x 52. Example: 4 loads, 0.5 kWh, 5 kW, 45 min, $0.16, 20 gal, $6, $0.25 -> 4.25 kWh = $0.68 + $0.12 + $0.25 = $1.05 a load; $218.40 a year (208 loads); dryer share $0.60 of the $1.05. All inputs are user estimates; give no appliance claims. Link `electricity-cost`, `shower-cost`.
169. **Ice melt coverage** (`everyday`). Inputs: length ft, width ft, application rate lb per 1,000 sq ft (from the product label; no default stated as a standard, placeholder 10 shown as an example), bag size lb, bag price. Formula: area = L x W; lb per application = area x rate / 1,000; applications per bag = bag / lb; cost per application = price / applications. Example: 40 x 4 ft = 160 sq ft at 10 lb per 1,000 -> 1.6 lb; a 50 lb bag gives 31.25 applications; $15 bag -> $0.48 each. Note surfaces and pets vary; defer to the label.
170. **Candle burn time** (`everyday`). Inputs: label burn hours, price, hours lit per evening, evenings (31). Formula: cost per hour = price / burn hours; evenings per candle = burn hours / hours per evening; candles needed = ceil(evenings x hours / burn hours). Example: $24, 50 h, 3 h a night -> $0.48 an hour, $1.44 an evening, 16.67 evenings per candle; 31 evenings x 3 h = 93 h -> 2 candles = $48. Label hours are the maker's estimate; say real burn varies. Safety line: never leave a lit candle unattended.
171. **Battery runtime** (`everyday`). Inputs: capacity mAh, cell voltage (3.7 default for Li-ion), device draw W, conversion efficiency % (85 default, label as an assumption), phone battery Wh (15 example). Formula: Wh = mAh x V / 1,000; usable = Wh x eff; hours = usable / W; phone charges = usable / phone Wh. Example: 10,000 mAh x 3.7 V = 37 Wh; x 85% = 31.45 Wh; a 5 W device runs 6.29 h; 15 Wh phone -> 2.10 charges. Airline Wh limits not stated (needs source).
172. **Waist-to-hip ratio** (`health`). Inputs: waist, hip (cm or in, same unit), sex. Formula: WHR = waist / hip. Example: waist 34, hip 40 -> 0.85; WHO 2008 cutoffs for abdominal obesity are >= 0.90 men, >= 0.85 women (verify against the WHO report before shipping), so a woman at 0.85 is flagged, a man is not. Say it is a screening ratio, not a diagnosis; no medical claims beyond the cited cutoff. Link `waist-to-height-ratio`, `bmi`.
173. **Long division** (`math`). Inputs: dividend, divisor (nonzero integers), decimal places (0-10). Output: quotient, remainder, decimal expansion, and the step table (bring down, multiply, subtract). Example: 1,234 / 7 = 176 remainder 2 (7 x 176 = 1,232); decimal 176.285714 to 6 places. Check: dividend = divisor x quotient + remainder. Reject divisor 0; handle negatives by dividing absolute values and stating the sign rule. Test with fixed inputs.
174. **Distance, rate and time** (`math`). Inputs: any two of distance, speed, time, plus units. Formula: d = r x t; r = d / t; t = d / r. Example: 150 mi at 60 mph -> 2.5 h = 2 h 30 min; 2.5 h at 55 mph -> 137.5 mi; 150 mi in 3 h -> 50 mph. Require exactly two filled fields; reject zero time or speed. Link `road-trip-time`, `pace`.
175. **Oven cost per bake** (`everyday`). Inputs: electric oven watts (3,000 example), duty cycle % (50 example, an estimate: the element cycles), minutes, $/kWh; or gas BTU per hour (18,000 example), duty %, therm price. Formula: kWh = W x duty x hours / 1,000; gas therms = BTU x duty x hours / 100,000. Example: 3,000 W x 50% x 1.5 h = 2.25 kWh x $0.16 = $0.36; gas 18,000 x 50% x 1.5 = 13,500 BTU = 0.135 therm x $1.50 = $0.20. Preheat and door-opening not modelled. Link `electricity-cost`, `cookie-exchange`.
176. **Investment fee drag** (`finance`). Inputs: starting balance, monthly contribution, gross annual return %, years, fund fee A % and fee B %. Formula: net rate = gross - fee; balance = monthly compounding at net/12 with contributions at month end: B = B x (1 + r/12) + contribution, repeated 12 x years. Example: $10,000 start, $500/month, 7% gross, 30 years, fee 0.05% (net 6.95%) vs 1.00% (net 6.00%) -> $683,965 vs $562,483, difference $121,482 on $190,000 contributed. Label "assumes a constant return, which real markets never give". Link `investment`, `compound-interest`.
177. **Debt vs invest** (`finance`). Inputs: extra amount, debt APR %, expected investment return %, years, optional tax rate on gains. Formula (annual compounding, simplification stated): pay-debt value = amount x (1 + d)^n; invest value = amount x (1 + i)^n; show the difference and the break-even return (= debt rate). Example: $5,000, debt 8%, invest 6%, 10 years -> $10,794.62 vs $8,954.24, paying debt wins by $1,840.38. State that debt payoff is guaranteed and the return is not, and that employer match comes first (link `employer-match-calculator`). Link `windfall-split`, `debt-payoff-planner`.
178. **Sabbatical fund** (`finance`). Inputs: months off, monthly spending, monthly income while off (0 default), buffer % (10 default), current savings, months until start, optional annual return % (0 default). Formula: target = months x max(0, spending - income) x (1 + buffer); gap = max(0, target - savings); monthly saving = gap / months until start (at 0% return; with return r use the annuity formula gap x i / ((1 + i)^n - 1), i = r/12). Example: 6 months x ($3,000 - $500) x 1.10 = $16,500; savings $4,000 -> gap $12,500; 12 months -> $1,041.67 a month. Link `savings-runway`, `sinking-fund`.
179. **Income-based bill split** (`finance`). Inputs: two to four people with monthly income (state: use net, after-tax, consistently), total bill. Formula: share_i = income_i / sum(incomes); amount_i = bill x share_i; compare with equal split. Example: $6,000 and $4,000 incomes, $2,000 bill -> 60% / 40% = $1,200 / $800 vs $1,000 each; the higher earner pays $200 more. Reject all-zero incomes; round to cents and give the remainder cent to the largest share. Link `rent-split`, `bill-splitter`.
180. **Savings account switch** (`finance`). Inputs: balance, current APY %, new APY %, years (1, 5, 10 shown). Formula: interest = balance x ((1 + APY)^years - 1), no deposits; gain = new - old. Example: $20,000 at 0.50% vs 4.00% -> 1 year $100 vs $800 (+$700); 5 years $505.03 vs $4,333.06 (+$3,828.03). Rates are inputs, never quoted as current; note that variable rates change and that deposit insurance limits are not modelled. Link `apy-calculator`, `emergency-fund`.
181. **Bill negotiation savings** (`finance`). Inputs: current monthly price, negotiated price, months the deal lasts, price after the deal ends, horizon months, optional return % for investing the saving. Formula: saving = (current - deal) x months in deal + (current - after) x remaining months; monthly saving invested at r/12 as an ordinary annuity. Example: $80 to $65 for 12 months, then back to $80, over 36 months -> $180 saved; if the $65 price stuck for all 36 months -> $540, and $15 a month invested at 6% -> $590.04. Link `subscription-cost`, `price-hike-impact`.
182. **Health plan comparison** (`finance`). Inputs per plan: monthly premium, deductible, coinsurance %, out-of-pocket max, optional employer HSA contribution; expected yearly medical spending (allowed charges). Formula: yearly cost = 12 x premium - HSA + min(OOP max, min(spend, deductible) + coinsurance x max(0, spend - deductible)). Also a table at spend $0, $2,000, $5,000, $10,000, $20,000 and the spend where the plans cross. Example: Plan A $200 premium, $500 deductible, 20%, OOP max $4,000; Plan B $120 premium, $3,000 deductible, 30%, OOP max $7,000; spend $5,000 -> A = 2,400 + min(4,000, 500 + 0.2 x 4,500) = 2,400 + 1,400 = $3,800; B = 1,440 + min(7,000, 3,000 + 0.3 x 2,000) = 1,440 + 3,600 = $5,040; A cheaper by $1,240. Simplified (copays, networks, prescriptions not modelled; check plan documents). Link `hsa-fsa-savings`, `fsa-spend-down`.
183. **Moving cost estimator** (`everyday`). Inputs: movers or helpers cost, truck rental, fuel, boxes and supplies, cleaning or deposit loss, overlap days x monthly rent / 30, utilities setup. Formula: sum of lines; show each line's share and the total as months of rent. Example: labour $480 + truck $120 + fuel $60 + supplies $75 + overlap 10 days x $1,800 / 30 = $600 + cleaning $150 = $1,485. All costs are user inputs. Link `prorated-rent`, `lease-break-cost`.
184. **One more year** (`fire`). Inputs: portfolio today, yearly savings if you keep working, real return %, withdrawal rate % (4 default, label as a rule of thumb), annual spending. Formula: after = portfolio x (1 + r) + savings; income now = portfolio x w; income after = after x w; gain = income after - income now. Example: $850,000, $30,000, 5%, 4% -> $922,500; income $34,000 -> $36,900; +$2,900 a year (+$241.67 a month, +8.53%). Also show the gap to spending (e.g. $40,000 -> $6,000 short now, $3,100 short after). Say it ignores sequence risk and health and that the 4% rate is not a guarantee. Link `safe-withdrawal-rate`, `years-to-fi`.
185. **Portfolio income per day** (`fire`). Inputs: portfolio, withdrawal rate % (4 default), hourly wage, work hours per week (40). Formula: year = portfolio x w; day = year / 365; hour = year / 8,760; month = year / 12; hours of wage covered = year / wage; weeks = hours / hours per week. Example: $500,000 at 4% -> $20,000 a year; $1,666.67 a month; $54.79 a day; $2.28 an hour; at $30 an hour that is 666.7 hours, 16.7 work weeks. Withdrawal is not income from yield alone; say so. Link `safe-withdrawal-rate`, `fire-number`.
186. **Net worth milestones** (`fire`). Inputs: net worth today, monthly contribution, annual return % (7 example), milestones list (default 100k, 250k, 500k, 1M). Formula: monthly loop B = B x (1 + r/12) + c until B >= milestone; months, years, and date. Example: $40,000, $1,500, 7% -> $100k in 32 months, $250k in 92, $500k in 161, $1M in 248 (20.7 years). Flag milestones already passed. Constant return assumed, label it. Chart: balance with milestone markers. Link `net-worth`, `fire-calculator`.
187. **Loss recovery** (`finance`). Inputs: loss %, optional assumed annual return % for time to recover. Formula: gain needed = 1 / (1 - loss) - 1; years = ln(1 / (1 - loss)) / ln(1 + r). Example: 30% loss -> +42.86%; 50% -> +100%; 20% -> +25%; 10% -> +11.11%; 30% at 7% a year -> 5.27 years. Reject loss >= 100%. Show a table 10% to 90%. Link `percentage-change`, `cagr-calculator`.
188. **Pay cut for a four-day week** (`finance`). Inputs: salary, hours per week (40), new hours (32), pay cut % (0 for same pay example), weeks per year (52). Formula: old hourly = salary / (h x weeks); new pay = salary x (1 - cut); new hourly = new pay / (new h x weeks); hours gained = (h - new h) x weeks; cost per free hour = (salary - new pay) / hours gained. Example: $80,000, 40 to 32 hours, 10% cut -> new pay $72,000; hourly $38.46 -> $43.27 (+12.5%); lose $8,000 a year ($666.67 a month gross) for 416 hours -> $19.23 per free hour. At 20% cut hourly stays $38.46 and cost per free hour is $38.46. Gross figures; benefits and tax not modelled. Link `salary-to-hourly`, `real-hourly-wage`.
189. **Family plan split** (`finance`). Inputs: family plan monthly price, members, solo plan price, optional shared-with-yourself months. Formula: per person = plan / members; saving per person per month = solo - per person; group saving per year = 12 x (members x solo - plan); break-even members = ceil(plan / solo). Example: $22.99, 5 members, $11.99 solo -> $4.60 each; saves $7.39 a month each ($88.70 a year); group saves $443.52 a year; break-even 2 members. Check the provider's household rules (not stated). Link `subscription-cost`, `bill-splitter`.
190. **Life in weeks** (`everyday`). Inputs: age, life expectancy assumption (80 default, editable, labelled an assumption not a forecast). Formula (52 weeks a year, simplification stated): total = 52 x expectancy; lived = 52 x age; left = total - lived; share = lived / total; Saturdays left = weeks left; summers and Christmases left = expectancy - age. Example: age 30, 80 -> 4,160 weeks; 1,560 lived (37.5%); 2,600 left; 50 summers. Grid of dots, lived filled; sensitive topic, calm copy. Link `time-with-loved-ones`, `retirement-countdown`.
191. **Dividend income goal** (`finance`). Inputs: target monthly income, dividend yield %, current invested, monthly contribution, annual total return % (reinvested). Formula: needed = target x 12 / yield; gap = needed - current; months = loop B = B x (1 + r/12) + c until B >= needed. Example: $1,000 a month at 4% -> $300,000; at 3% -> $400,000; at 2% -> $600,000; from $20,000 + $1,000 a month at 6% -> 165 months (13.75 years). Yields vary and dividends can be cut; say so, no stock picks, tax not modelled. Link `dividend-calculator`, `yield-on-cost`.
192. **Bonus vs raise** (`finance`). Inputs: salary, raise % (option A), one-time bonus $ (option B), years, optional future annual raise % on the base. Formula: extra pay in year t = salary x raise x (1+g)^(t-1); raise total = sum over years; break-even years = bonus / (salary x raise) when g = 0. Example: $80,000, 3% raise = $2,400 a year vs $5,000 bonus: break-even 2.08 years; over 5 years raise = $12,000 vs bonus $5,000; with g = 3%, 5-year raise total = 2,400 x 5.30913581 = $12,741.93. Pre-tax both sides; note bonus withholding differs, link `bonus-tax`, `raise-calculator`. Mention raises also lift later raises and some benefits.
193. **Salary growth projection** (`finance`). Inputs: current salary, annual raise %, working years. Formula: year k salary = S x (1+g)^(k-1); final-year salary = S x (1+g)^(n-1); lifetime total = S x ((1+g)^n - 1) / g (g = 0 gives S x n). Example: $60,000, 3%, 30 years: 1.03^30 = 2.42726247, lifetime total = 60,000 x 47.5754157 = $2,854,524.94, final-year salary = 60,000 x 2.35656551 = $141,393.93. Optional inflation input shows the real raise (1+g)/(1+i) - 1. Link `raise-calculator`, `inflation`. Not a forecast.
194. **W-2 to contractor rate** (`finance`). Inputs: salary, benefits value $ a year (health, match), extra tax % on salary paid as contractor (user input, default 7.65), PTO days, paid holidays, hours per week. Formula: billable hours = 52 x hours - (PTO + holidays) x hours/5; rate = (salary + benefits + salary x extra%) / billable hours. Example: $90,000, $12,000 benefits, 7.65% extra = $6,885, 25 days off = 200 h of 2,080 -> 1,880 h; rate = 108,885 / 1,880 = $57.92 an hour vs $43.27 salary-equivalent (+33.9%). Say tax is user-supplied and not advice; link `freelance-rate`, `self-employment-tax`.
195. **Savings rate escalator** (`finance`). Inputs: income, starting savings rate %, yearly step (points), cap %, years, optional income growth %, optional annual return %. Formula: rate_y = min(cap, start + step x (y-1)); saved_y = income_y x rate_y; with return r, balance = balance x (1+r) + saved_y. Example (0% growth, 0% return): $60,000, start 5%, +1 point, cap 15%, 10 years: rates 5..14, total = 60,000 x 0.95 = $57,000 vs flat 5% = $30,000; 15 years: rates 5..15 then 15 for 4 more years = 60,000 x 1.70 = $102,000. Hand-check any return case with a loop before adding it to `examples`. Link `savings-goal`, `employer-match-calculator`.
196. **Year-to-date income projection** (`finance`). Inputs: YTD gross, pay periods completed, pay periods per year (12/24/26/52), one-time bonuses already in YTD (optional). Formula: projected = (YTD - bonuses) / completed x periods + bonuses. Example: $62,500 after 20 of 26 biweekly periods, no bonus: 62,500 / 20 x 26 = $81,250; with $5,000 of the YTD being a bonus: 57,500 / 20 x 26 + 5,000 = $79,750. Gross only, not tax; link `paychecks-per-year`, `take-home-pay`.
197. **Engagement ring budget** (`finance`). Inputs: ring cost, gross annual income, savings so far, monthly amount you can save. Outputs: months of gross income = cost / (income / 12); % of annual income; hours of work = cost / (income / 2080); months to save = max(0, cost - saved) / monthly. Also show 1, 2 and 3 months of income as comparison lines, labelled a popular guideline, not a standard. Example: $5,000, $72,000, $1,000 saved, $400 a month: 0.833 months, 6.94%, 144.4 hours (hourly 34.615), 10 months; 3 months of income = $18,000. Link `hours-of-work-cost`, `savings-goal`. No financing assumed.
198. **Cost of living alone** (`finance`). Inputs: solo rent, solo utilities, solo internet, shared-place total rent, total utilities, total internet, number of people sharing. Formula: solo = rent + utilities + internet; shared = (rent + utilities + internet) / people; premium = solo - shared per month, x12 per year, / 365 per day. Example: solo 1,800 + 150 + 60 = $2,010; shared (2,700 + 240 + 60) / 3 = $1,000; premium $1,010 a month = $12,120 a year = $33.21 a day. Link `rent-split`, `rent-affordability`. Say deposits, furniture and commute differences are not included.
199. **Shares to sell for cash** (`finance`). Inputs: cash needed after tax, share price, cost basis per share, tax rate on gains % (user input), optional fee per sale. Formula: gain = max(0, price - basis); net per share = price - gain x rate; shares = ceil((cash + fee) / net per share); gross proceeds = shares x price. Example: need $10,000, price $50, basis $30, 15%: gain $20, tax $3, net $47 per share; 10,000 / 47 = 212.77 -> 213 shares, $10,650 gross, tax $639, net $10,011. Tax rate is an input, no IRS table; not tax advice; link `capital-gains-tax`, `stock-profit-calculator`.
200. **Net to gross** (`finance`). Inputs: current gross salary, extra take-home wanted per year, combined marginal tax rate % (income tax + payroll, user input), pay periods per year (26 default). Formula: gross raise = extra / (1 - t); raise % = gross raise / salary; per paycheck gross = raise / periods; per paycheck net = extra / periods. Example: $60,000, $3,000 take-home, 30% -> $4,285.71 gross raise (7.14% of salary); per biweekly check $164.84 gross, $115.38 net. Reject t >= 100%. FAQ: a raise is taxed at your marginal rate, not your average rate; state the rate is your own estimate. Related: `raise-calculator`, `take-home-pay`, `bonus-vs-raise`.
201. **Degree payback** (`finance`). Inputs: tuition and fees total, years in school, salary given up per year while studying (0 if you keep working), yearly salary uplift after the degree (pre-tax), tax rate % on the uplift, working years left. Formula: cost = tuition + years x forgone pay; net uplift = uplift x (1 - tax); payback years after graduating = cost / net uplift; lifetime gain = net uplift x working years - cost. Example: $40,000 + 2 x $50,000 = $140,000; uplift $20,000 at 25% tax -> $15,000 net; payback 9.33 years; over 30 working years +$310,000. Label as an illustration of your own inputs, not an expected outcome. Related: `student-loan-payoff`, `job-offer-comparison`, `credits-to-graduate`.
202. **Student loan interest while in school** (`finance`). Inputs: loan balance, APR %, months until repayment, optional monthly interest-only payment. Formula (two views side by side): simple = balance x APR x months/12; balance at repayment = balance + simple; yearly capitalised = balance x (1 + APR)^(months/12). Monthly interest at repayment = new balance x APR / 12. Example: $20,000, 6%, 48 months -> simple interest $4,800, balance $24,800; yearly-capitalised $25,249.54 (1.06^4 = 1.26247696); monthly interest $124 vs $100 at the start (+$24); paying $100 a month while in school avoids the $4,800. Do not say which method applies: tell the user to check their servicer. Related: `student-loan-payoff`, `loan`, `degree-payback`.
203. **Cost per mile** (`everyday`). Inputs: miles per year, mpg, fuel price, insurance per year, maintenance and tires per year, registration and fees per year, depreciation per year (or purchase price, resale value, years), loan interest per year. Formula: fuel = miles / mpg x price; total = sum of lines; per mile = total / miles; per month = total / 12; per day = total / 365. Example: 12,000 miles, 30 mpg, $3.50 -> fuel $1,400; insurance $1,800; maintenance $900; registration $250; depreciation ($28,000 - $16,000) / 4 = $3,000; total $7,350; $0.6125 per mile (show $0.61), $612.50 a month, $20.14 a day. Optional compare-to-rate input (user supplied, no default). Related: `gas-cost`, `keep-vs-replace-car`, `business-mileage-deduction`.
204. **Wedding budget** (`finance`). Inputs: total budget, guests, percent per category (venue and catering, photo and video, attire, music, flowers and decor, other and buffer; must total 100, show the remainder), months left, saved so far. Formula: category = budget x %; per guest = budget / guests; monthly saving = (budget - saved) / months. Example: $30,000, 100 guests, 50/12/8/8/10/12 -> $15,000, $3,600, $2,400, $2,400, $3,000, $3,600; $300 per guest; $6,000 saved with 18 months left -> $1,333.33 a month. Percentages are the user's, not benchmarks. Related: `engagement-ring-budget`, `event-cost-per-guest`, `sinking-fund`.
205. **Portfolio rebalance** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 5 holdings (name, current value, target %), optional new cash to add (default 0). Formula: total = sum + cash; target value = total x target %; trade = target value - current value (positive buy, negative sell); drift = current % - target %. Targets must total 100. Example: stocks $70,000 (60%), bonds $20,000 (30%), cash $10,000 (10%): drift +10, -10, 0 points; sell $10,000 stocks, buy $10,000 bonds. With $10,000 new cash: total $110,000, trades -$4,000, +$13,000, +$1,000 (sum = cash added). Show a no-sell mode only if it stays simple. No tax advice; mention taxable-account sales can create tax. Related: `investment`, `net-worth`, `investment-fee-drag`.
206. **Prorated salary** (`finance`). Inputs: annual salary, month length (28-31), start day, weekdays in the month, weekdays worked from the start day (or derive from a date). Formula: monthly = salary / 12; calendar method = monthly x days worked / days in month (days worked = days - start + 1); working-day method = monthly x weekdays worked / weekdays in month. Example: $72,000, start 12 Jan 2027 (31 days, 21 weekdays, 14 weekdays from the 12th to the 29th): monthly $6,000; calendar $6,000 x 20/31 = $3,870.97; working-day $6,000 x 14/21 = $4,000.00. Say employers differ and gross pay is shown before tax. Related: `prorated-rent`, `paychecks-per-year`, `ytd-income-projection`.
207. **Home gym payback** (`health`). Inputs: equipment cost, monthly membership fee, monthly upkeep (optional, default 0), months to compare (36). Formula: monthly saving = membership - upkeep; break-even months = cost / saving; over N months home = cost + N x upkeep vs gym = N x membership. Example: $1,200, $45, $0 -> 26.67 months; with $5 upkeep -> 30 months; over 36 months gym $1,620 vs home $1,380, so home saves $240. If upkeep >= membership, say it never pays back. Related: `gym-cost-per-visit`, `cost-per-use`, `workout-volume`.
208. **Childcare vs working** (`finance`). Inputs: gross monthly pay, tax rate % (user estimate), childcare per month, commute and work costs per month, hours worked per month. Formula: net pay = gross x (1 - tax); gain = net pay - childcare - costs; per hour = gain / hours; share of net pay eaten = (childcare + costs) / net pay. Example: $4,000, 25%, $1,500 childcare, $300 costs, 160 h -> net $3,000, gain $1,200 a month ($14,400 a year), $7.50 an hour; 40% of net pay goes to childcare and costs. Say career growth and pension are not counted; link `real-hourly-wage`, `take-home-pay`.
209. **Tax-equivalent yield** (`finance`). Inputs: tax-free yield % and marginal tax rate % (user estimate); or a taxable yield. Formula: taxable equivalent = tax-free / (1 - t); after-tax of taxable = taxable x (1 - t). Example: 3.5% tax-free at 24% -> 3.5 / 0.76 = 4.605%; a 4.2% taxable yield at 24% -> 3.192% after tax, so the tax-free 3.5% wins. Ignore state tax unless included in the rate; not advice; link `bond-yield-calculator`.
210. **Equity dilution** (`finance`). Inputs: your ownership %, up to 4 rounds each with new money raised and pre-money valuation (or % sold), optional option-pool top-up %. Formula: new investors own raised / (pre + raised); your stake x (1 - that share). Example: 1.0%; round 1 raises $5M at $20M pre -> investors 20% -> you 0.80%; round 2 raises $20M at $80M pre -> 20% -> 0.64%. Show what it is worth at an exit value you type. Illustration only; link `stock-option-exercise`.
211. **Progressive overload plan** (`health`). Inputs: start weight, goal weight, increase per step (lb or kg), steps per week (1-3), optional deload every N weeks at x% of the last weight. Formula: weight_w = start + inc x w; weeks to goal = ceil((goal - start) / (inc x steps)). Example: 135 lb to 185 lb adding 5 lb once a week -> 10 weeks; adding 5 lb twice a week -> 5 weeks. Say real progress slows and this is a plan, not a prescription; link `one-rep-max`, `plate-calculator`.
212. **Weekly calorie budget** (`health`). Inputs: daily target, planned big-meal calories, which day, days it is spread over. Formula: weekly = target x 7; remaining = weekly - big meal; other days = remaining / 6 (or over the chosen days). Example: 2,000 target, 3,500 feast -> weekly 14,000; other six days = 10,500 / 6 = 1,750 each (250 under). Warn against going under a safe minimum; link `tdee`, `food-burn-off`.
213. **ARM payment after reset** (`finance`). Inputs: loan amount, term years, initial rate %, fixed years, new rate % after reset. Formula: payment = P r / (1 - (1+r)^-n) with r monthly; reset payment uses the remaining balance and remaining months at the new rate. Example: $300,000, 30 years, 5% fixed 5 years -> $1,610.46; balance after 60 payments $275,486.20; at 7% for the remaining 300 months the payment is $1,947.08 (script-checked, re-verify with hand math). Rate caps are inputs; link `mortgage`, `refinance`.
214. **House hacking offset** (`finance`). Inputs: mortgage payment plus tax and insurance per month, rent received, vacancy %, extra costs (utilities, repairs) per month. Formula: net rent = rent x (1 - vacancy) - costs; net housing cost = payment - net rent; covered % = net rent / payment. Example: $2,400 payment, $900 rent, 5% vacancy, $150 costs -> net rent $705; net cost $1,695 a month ($20,340 a year); 29.4% covered. Landlord duties and tax on rent are not modelled; link `cash-on-cash-return`, `rent-split`.
215. **Allocation by age** (`finance`). Inputs: age, portfolio value, rule (100, 110, 120 minus age). Formula: stocks % = rule - age (cap 0-100); bonds = 100 - stocks; dollars = value x %. Example: age 30, $50,000 -> 70/30 under 100, 80/20 under 110, 90/10 under 120; $40,000 stocks under 110. State plainly these are popular rules of thumb with no standard, not advice; link `investment`, `portfolio-rebalance`.
216. **Roth conversion** (`finance`). Inputs: amount converted, marginal rate now %, expected rate later %, years until withdrawal, annual growth %. Formula: tax now = amount x rate now (paid from outside funds); balance = amount x (1+g)^n; tax avoided = balance x rate later; cost of paying outside = tax now x (1+g)^n; net = tax avoided - cost. Example: $20,000, 22% now, 24% later, 20 years, 7% -> tax now $4,400; balance $77,393.69; avoided $18,574.49; cost $17,026.61; net +$1,547.87 (script-checked, re-verify by hand). Ignores the 5-year rule, Medicare and aid effects; not advice; link `roth-vs-traditional`, `roth-ira`.
217. **Standard vs itemized** (`finance`). Inputs: standard deduction (user enters current figure), mortgage interest, state and local taxes paid (user enters capped amount), charity, other, marginal rate %. Formula: itemized = sum; better = max; extra = |itemized - standard|; tax saved by choosing the larger over the smaller = extra x rate. Example: standard $15,000; $8,000 + $6,000 + $2,500 = $16,500 itemized; itemize wins by $1,500; at 22% that is $330. Add a note that bunching two years of charity can tip the result; link `income-tax`, `tax-refund-estimator`.
218. **0% gains headroom** (`finance`). Inputs: top of the 0% bracket (user enters current figure), taxable income before gains, gain to realise, long-term rate above the bracket %. Formula: headroom = max(0, top - income); tax-free part = min(gain, headroom); taxed = gain - tax-free; tax = taxed x rate. Example: top $96,700, income $70,000 -> headroom $26,700; a $30,000 gain has $3,300 taxed; at 15% tax $495. Ignores state tax and the extra tax on high income; link `capital-gains-tax`, `tax-loss-harvesting`.
219. **Sign-up bonus pace** (`finance`). Inputs: minimum spend, months allowed, spent so far, months left, bonus points or cash, cents per point, annual fee. Formula: still needed = min spend - spent; per month = still needed / months left; bonus value = points x cpp / 100; net = value - fee. Example: $4,000 min, $600 spent, 2 months left -> $3,400 still needed, $1,700 a month; 60,000 points at 1.5c = $900; less $95 fee = $805. Warn not to overspend or carry a balance; link `credit-card-annual-fee`, `cash-back-rewards`.
220. **CD ladder** (`finance`). Inputs: total, rungs, term and rate per rung. Formula: each rung = total / rungs; interest = rung x rate x term in years (simple, terms up to 1 year); maturity = rung + interest. Example: $12,000, 4 rungs of $3,000 at 3 mo 4.0%, 6 mo 4.3%, 9 mo 4.5%, 12 mo 4.7% -> interest $30.00 + $64.50 + $101.25 + $141.00 = $336.75. Note that rates are examples and rungs roll into new terms; link `cd`, `apy-calculator`.
221. **Social Security breakeven** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly benefit at the early age, at the later age (from the user's statement), the two ages. Formula: forgone = early monthly x 12 x (later - early); extra per month = later monthly - early monthly; breakeven months = forgone / extra; breakeven age = later age + months / 12. Example: $2,000 at 67 vs $2,480 at 70 -> forgone $72,000; extra $480; 150 months; breakeven age 82.5. Ignores COLA, taxes and investment return of early payments; link `social-security-cola`, `fire-number`.
222. **Snow removal payback** (`everyday`). Inputs: blower price, yearly fuel and upkeep, plow-service price per visit, visits per winter, years of life. Formula: service per winter = price x visits; saving = service - upkeep; payback winters = price / saving; lifetime net = saving x years - price. Example: $900, $40 upkeep, $60 x 14 visits = $840 -> saving $800; payback 1.125 winters; over 10 years net $7,100. Excludes your own time; link `ice-melt-coverage`, `energy-upgrade-payback`.
223. **Antifreeze mix** (`everyday`). Inputs: system capacity, current antifreeze %, target %. Formula: drain x of mixture and refill with pure antifreeze: c(V - x) + x = tV, so x = V(t - c) / (1 - c). Example: 12 qt, 30% now, 50% target -> x = 12 x 0.20 / 0.70 = 3.43 qt. Say to follow the vehicle maker and use a tester; link `heating-cost`.
224. **ESPP return** (`finance`). Inputs: contribution $, price at offering start, price at purchase, discount % (user, commonly up to 15), lookback yes/no. Formula: purchase price = (lookback ? min(start, end) : end) x (1 - d); shares = contribution / purchase price; value = shares x end price; gain = value - contribution; return = gain / contribution. Example: $8,500, $40 start, $50 end, 15%: lookback price $34.00, 250 shares, value $12,500, gain $4,000 (47.1%); no lookback price $42.50, 200 shares, value $10,000, gain $1,500 (17.6%). Say plans differ and taxes are not computed; link `rsu-tax`, `stock-option-exercise`.
225. **Purchase opportunity cost** (`finance`). Inputs: price, years (default 30), annual return % (default 7, labelled an assumption), optional inflation %. Formula: FV = price x (1 + r)^years; real value = FV / (1 + i)^years. Example: $1,200, 7%, 30 years -> 1.07^30 = 7.6123, FV $9,134.71. Show 10, 20, 30-year rows; wording is a what-if, not advice. Link `compound-interest`, `latte-factor`, `hours-of-work-cost`.
226. **Donate stock vs cash** (`finance`). Inputs: market value of shares, cost basis, long-term capital gains rate %, marginal rate for the deduction %. Formula: gain = value - basis; tax avoided = gain x gains rate; deduction value = value x marginal rate (same either way if itemizing). Example: $10,000 value, $4,000 basis, 15%, 24% -> gain $6,000, tax avoided $900, deduction value $2,400, so gifting the stock leaves $900 more than selling and gifting the cash. State plainly that deduction limits, itemizing and holding-period rules are not computed. Link `capital-gains-tax`, `gains-harvest-headroom`.
227. **Tax drag** (`finance`). Inputs: starting balance, yearly contribution (default 0), years, pre-tax return %, yearly tax drag in points (default 0.5, user), tax rate on gains at the end %. Formula: tax-advantaged (no tax on withdrawal, Roth-style) = FV at r; taxable = FV at (r - drag), then end tax = rate x (balance - contributions), a simplification that overstates tax because reinvested distributions add basis (say so). Example: $10,000, 30 years, 7%, drag 0.5, 15% -> advantaged $76,122.55; taxable $66,143.66 before tax, tax $8,421.55, after tax $57,722.11; gap $18,400.44. Link `roth-vs-traditional`, `investment-fee-drag`.
228. **Sign-on bonus clawback** (`finance`). Inputs: gross bonus, repayment period months, months worked, rule (prorated or all-or-nothing), withholding rate % on the bonus. Formula: owed = prorated ? bonus x (period - worked) / period : (worked < period ? bonus : 0); after-tax received = bonus x (1 - rate); kept = received - owed when repaid out of pocket at gross (label: tax refund or deduction on repayment depends on the year and is not computed). Example: $10,000, 12 months, left after 8, prorated, 30% -> owed $3,333.33, received $7,000, kept $3,666.67. Link `bonus-tax`, `job-offer-comparison`.
229. **Winter tire cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: winter set price (tires and wheels), years of use, swap fee per change (two a year), all-season set price, all-season life in miles, miles a year, share of miles in winter %. Formula: yearly cost = set / years + 2 x swap; wear saved = all-season set x (miles x share) / life; net extra = yearly cost - wear saved. Example: $800, 5 years, $60 swap, $600 all-season, 40,000 mi life, 12,000 mi a year, 30% -> 160 + 120 = $280; saved 600 x 3,600 / 40,000 = $54; net $226 a year. No safety claims. Link `tire-pressure-cold`, `cost-per-mile`.
230. **Rent on a credit card** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly rent, processing fee % (user, e.g. 2.9), reward points per dollar, cents per point, optional sign-up bonus value and months of spend it unlocks. Formula: fee = rent x f; reward = rent x points x cpp / 100; net = reward - fee; yearly = 12 x net. Example: $2,000, 2.9%, 1 point per $ at 1.5 cents -> fee $58.00, reward $30.00, net -$28.00 a month, -$336 a year. Add the bonus to show when one-off value beats the fee. Warn that a carried balance costs far more than any reward. Link `points-value`, `signup-bonus-pace`.
231. **Deductible break-even** (`finance`). Inputs: annual premium at low and high deductible, the two deductibles, optional claim chance % a year. Formula: saved = premium_low - premium_high; extra exposure = ded_high - ded_low; break-even years without a claim = extra / saved; break-even claim chance = saved / extra. Example: $1,400 vs $1,100, deductibles $500 vs $1,500 -> saved $300, extra $1,000, 3.33 years, 30% chance. Note claims can raise future premiums and are not modelled. Link `health-plan-comparison`, `emergency-fund`.
232. **Backdoor Roth pro-rata** (`finance`). Inputs: pre-tax traditional, SEP and SIMPLE IRA balance on Dec 31 (after the conversion), after-tax basis (from Form 8606), amount converted, marginal tax rate %. Formula: total = pre-tax balance + converted amount; non-taxable share = basis / total; non-taxable = converted x share; taxable = converted - non-taxable; tax = taxable x rate. Example: $45,000 pre-tax, $7,000 basis, $7,000 converted, 24% -> share 7,000 / 52,000 = 13.46%; non-taxable $942.31; taxable $6,057.69; tax $1,453.85 (with the $45,000 rolled into a 401(k) first the taxable part is $0). Does not replace Form 8606; income limits and the Dec 31 rule are stated, not computed. Link `roth-ira`, `roth-conversion`.
233. **Mega backdoor Roth room** (`finance`). Inputs: total annual plan limit (user, from the plan year's IRS figure), your pre-tax and Roth deferrals, employer match and other employer money, paychecks left. Formula: room = limit - deferrals - employer; per paycheck = room / paychecks. Example (limit is an example, not a current figure): $70,000 limit, $23,500 deferrals, $6,000 employer -> room $40,500; over 26 paychecks $1,557.69. Say catch-up contributions are outside the limit, and that the plan must allow after-tax contributions and in-plan or in-service conversion. Link `401k-max-out`, `roth-ira`.
234. **HSA invest vs spend** (`finance`). Inputs: expense today, years, annual return %, tax rate on taxable gains %. Formula: HSA = expense x (1 + r)^n (invest HSA, pay cash); taxable = expense x (1 + r)^n less rate x gain (spend HSA, invest the cash); advantage = HSA - taxable. Example: $1,000, 20 years, 7%, 15% -> HSA $3,869.68; taxable after tax $3,439.23; advantage $430.45. Say to keep receipts, that the cash must really be affordable, and that some states tax HSA growth. Link `hsa-fsa-savings`, `compound-interest`.
235. **COBRA vs marketplace** (`finance`). Inputs: months of coverage, COBRA monthly premium, marketplace monthly premium after subsidy, deductible left on the current plan, deductible on the new plan, expected medical spend. Formula: plan cost = premium x months + min(expected spend, deductible); difference = marketplace - COBRA. Example: 6 months, $720 vs $410, deductible left $200 vs $3,000, spend $3,000 -> COBRA 4,320 + 200 = $4,520; marketplace 2,460 + 3,000 = $5,460; COBRA cheaper by $940. With $1,000 spend: $4,520 vs $3,460, marketplace cheaper by $1,060. Ignores coinsurance and out-of-pocket maxes; say to check the election deadline on the notice. Link `health-plan-comparison`, `severance-pay`, `savings-runway`.
236. **Write-off value** (`finance`). Inputs: expense, federal rate %, state rate %, self-employment tax rate % (blank if employee). Formula: saved = expense x (federal + state + SE); real cost = expense - saved. Example: $1,000, 22%, 5%, 14.13% -> saved $411.30; real cost $588.70. Say only deductible business expenses qualify and that it is not tax advice. Link `home-office-deduction`, `self-employment-tax`, `estimated-tax-safe-harbor`.
237. **Dental plan worth it** (`finance`). Inputs: annual premium, deductible, plan pays %, annual maximum, expected care at cash price. Formula: plan pays = min(max, (care - deductible) x %); you pay = care - plan pays; with plan = premium + you pay; difference = care - with plan. Example: $480, $50, 80%, $1,500 max, $1,500 care -> plan pays $1,160; you pay $340; with plan $820; saves $680. With $300 care: plan pays $200; with plan $580 vs $300, costs $280 more. Link `health-plan-comparison`, `hsa-fsa-savings`.
238. **Bracket headroom** (`finance`). Inputs: taxable income so far, top of the current bracket, current rate %, next rate %, extra income to test. Formula: headroom = top - income; tax on extra = min(extra, headroom) x current + max(0, extra - headroom) x next; average rate = tax / extra. Example: $80,000, top $100,000, 22% then 24%, extra $30,000 -> headroom $20,000; tax 4,400 + 2,400 = $6,800 (22.67%). Federal ordinary only; no credits, phase-outs, FICA or state. Link `roth-conversion`, `gains-harvest-headroom`, `standard-vs-itemized`.
239. **Car idling cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: idle minutes per day, days a year, fuel burned per hour (gal/h), fuel price. Formula: hours = minutes x days / 60; gallons = hours x burn; cost = gallons x price. Example: 10 min, 120 days, 0.3 gal/h, $3.50 -> 20 h; 6 gal; $21.00. No warm-up or engine advice. Link `gas-cost`, `cost-per-mile`.
240. **Tax prep DIY vs pro** (`finance`). Inputs: software price, DIY hours, pro fee, hours you still spend with a pro, value of an hour of your time, extra deductions or credits a pro might find (user guess, $), marginal rate %. Formula: DIY cost = software + DIY hours x hourly value; pro cost = fee + pro hours x hourly value - extra x rate; difference = pro cost - DIY cost. Example: $60, 4 h, $250, 1 h, $30 an hour, $400 extra, 22% -> DIY 60 + 120 = $180; pro 250 + 30 - 88 = $192; DIY cheaper by $12. Say the extra-deduction figure is a guess and that complex returns can change the answer; not tax advice. Link `tax-refund-estimator`, `write-off-value`.
241. **Refund interest forgone** (`finance`). Inputs: refund received, annual savings rate % (user, e.g. a savings APY), months the money was effectively lent (default 6, since over-withholding builds up through the year). Formula: interest forgone = refund x rate x months / 12; per paycheck to adjust = refund / paychecks per year. Example: $3,000, 4.5%, 6 months -> $67.50 (about $5.63 a month); $3,000 / 26 = $115.38 more take-home per biweekly paycheck. State that a refund is not a loss of the principal, that W-4 changes are the user's call (no advice), and that the 6-month average is an assumption. Link `tax-refund-estimator`, `apy-calculator`, `take-home-pay`.
242. **Income timing tax shift** (`finance`). Inputs: income amount, marginal rate this year %, marginal rate next year %, annual return on cash % (default 4, labelled). Formula: tax now = amount x r1; tax later = amount x r2; rate benefit = tax now - tax later; time value = tax now x return; total benefit of deferring = rate benefit + time value (negative means take it this year). Example: $20,000, 24% vs 22%, 4% -> tax now $4,800; later $4,400; rate benefit $400; time value $192; total $592. Say rates are the user's own estimate, that deferral is only possible when the payer or accounting method allows it, and that it is not tax advice. Link `bracket-headroom`, `bonus-tax`, `estimated-tax-safe-harbor`.
243. **Pre-tax contribution cost** (`finance`). Inputs: contribution per paycheck, paychecks per year, federal marginal rate %, state rate %, employer match $ per paycheck (default 0). Formula: tax saved per paycheck = contribution x (federal + state); take-home cost = contribution - tax saved; annual cost = take-home cost x paychecks; into the account = (contribution + match) x paychecks; cost per dollar invested = annual cost / into account. Example: $200, 26, 22%, 5%, match $100 -> saved $54; cost $146 per paycheck; annual $3,796 for $5,200 contributed (tax saved $1,404); into account $7,800; $0.487 per dollar. Say Social Security and Medicare tax still apply, a Roth saves no tax now, and plan limits are not computed. Link `401k-max-out`, `employer-match-calculator`, `take-home-pay`.
244. **Holiday card cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: number of cards, price per card and envelope, extras per card (photo print, stickers), postage per card (user enters current rate), cards you could drop. Formula: per card = price + extras + postage; total = cards x per card; saved by trimming = dropped x per card. Example: 60 cards, $1.50, $0.40, $0.78 -> $2.68 each; $160.80 total; dropping 10 saves $26.80. No claim about current stamp prices. Link `gift-budget`, `holiday-spending-total`, `wrapping-paper`.
245. **Step goal ramp** (`health`). Inputs: current daily steps, goal daily steps, steps added per week, start date. Formula: weeks = ceil((goal - current) / increase); week k target = min(goal, current + k x increase); extra steps over the ramp = 7 x sum of (target_k - current). Example: 4,000 to 8,000 adding 500 a week -> 8 weeks; targets 4,500, 5,000, ... 8,000; extra steps = 7 x 500 x (1+...+8 = 36) = 126,000; goal date = start + 56 days. Goals are the user's own; no medical claims or recommended counts. Link `steps-to-distance`, `goal-pace`, `calories-burned`.
246. **Budget billing true-up** (`finance`). Inputs: 12 monthly bill amounts (Jan to Dec, editable), optional start month. Formula: level payment = sum / 12; running balance after month k = k x level - sum of first k bills; worst shortfall = most negative balance; best buffer = most positive. Example: 220, 200, 150, 100, 70, 60, 80, 90, 70, 90, 130, 190 -> sum $1,450; level $120.83; balance after Jan -$99.17, Feb -$178.33, Mar -$207.50 (worst), Apr -$186.67, May -$135.83, Jun -$75.00, Jul -$34.17, Aug -$3.33, Sep +$47.50, Oct +$78.33 (best), Nov +$69.17, Dec $0. Say utility plans re-estimate and settle differently; check the utility. Link `heating-cost`, `thermostat-setback`, `sinking-fund`.
247. **Renew vs move** (`finance`). Inputs: renewal rent, new place rent, one-off moving cost (movers, deposits not refunded, overlap rent), months you expect to stay. Formula: saving per month = renewal - new; break-even months = moving cost / saving (if saving <= 0, moving never pays); net over stay = saving x months - moving cost. Example: $1,950 vs $1,700, $2,400 cost, 24 months -> $250 a month; 9.6 months; net $3,600. Ignores commute, utilities and lease-break fees; link `rent-increase-impact`, `moving-cost`, `lease-break-cost`.
248. **Overtime deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: regular hourly rate, overtime hours in the year, overtime multiplier (default 1.5), MAGI, filing status, cap and phase-out threshold (editable, defaults $12,500 / $25,000 joint and $150,000 / $300,000 joint, labelled "check the Schedule 1-A instructions"), phase-out per $1,000 (default $100), marginal rate %. Formula: premium = hours x rate x (multiplier - 1); max = cap - phase-out x (MAGI - threshold)/1,000 when over, floor 0; deduction = min(premium, max); tax saved = deduction x rate. Example: $30, 200 h, 1.5 -> premium $3,000; deduction $3,000; at 22% saves $660. Second: premium $14,000, single, MAGI $160,000, 24% -> max 12,500 - 1,000 = $11,500; deduction $11,500; saves $2,760. Say only the FLSA premium counts, the W-2 box 12 code TT figure wins when present, payroll tax still applies, not tax advice. Link `overtime-pay`, `bracket-headroom`, `take-home-pay`.
249. **Car loan interest deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: interest paid in the year, MAGI, filing status, marginal rate %, cap and threshold (editable, defaults $10,000 and $100,000 / $200,000 joint), phase-out per $1,000 (default $200). Formula: max = cap - phase-out x (MAGI - threshold)/1,000 when over, floor 0; deduction = min(interest, max); tax saved = deduction x rate. Example: $1,800, MAGI $110,000 single, 22% -> max $8,000; deduction $1,800; saves $396. Second: $4,000 interest, MAGI $145,000 -> max 10,000 - 9,000 = $1,000; deduction $1,000. Checklist text only: new vehicle, final assembly in the US, personal use, loan taken after 2024, VIN on the return; used cars and leases do not qualify; check the IRS page. Link `auto-loan`, `car-affordability`, `bracket-headroom`.
250. **Tips deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: qualified tips for the year, MAGI, filing status, marginal rate %, cap and threshold (editable, defaults $25,000 and $150,000 / $300,000 joint), phase-out per $1,000 (default $100). Formula: max = cap - phase-out x (MAGI - threshold)/1,000 when over, floor 0; deduction = min(tips, max); tax saved = deduction x rate; payroll tax on tips unchanged = tips x 7.65% shown as a note. Example: $18,000 tips, MAGI $60,000, 12% -> deduction $18,000; saves $2,160. Second: $30,000 tips, MAGI $170,000, 24% -> max 25,000 - 2,000 = $23,000; saves $5,520. Say the occupation must be on the IRS list, self-employed deduction cannot exceed net income from that business, not tax advice. Link `tip`, `bracket-headroom`, `take-home-pay`.
251. **Solo 401(k) and SEP room** (`finance`). Inputs: net self-employment profit, Social Security wage base, employee deferral limit, total plan limit (all three editable, labelled "enter the current IRS and SSA figures"). Formula: SE earnings = profit x 0.9235; SE tax = min(SE earnings, wage base) x 12.4% + SE earnings x 2.9%; compensation = profit - SE tax / 2; employer share = 20% x compensation; SEP = min(employer share, total limit); solo 401(k) = min(min(deferral limit, compensation) + employer share, total limit). Example: $100,000, base $184,500, deferral $24,500, total $72,000 -> SE earnings $92,350; SE tax $11,451.40 + $2,678.15 = $14,129.55; compensation $92,935.22; employer $18,587.05; SEP $18,587.05; solo 401(k) $43,087.05. Say catch-up amounts and the Roth option are not modelled, limits are illustrative until checked, not tax advice. Link `self-employment-tax`, `401k-max-out`, `roth-vs-traditional`.
252. **Capital loss carryover schedule** (`finance`). Inputs: net capital loss, annual limit against ordinary income (default 3,000; 1,500 if married filing separately), capital gains you expect each year (default 0), ordinary marginal rate %, capital gains rate %, years to show (max 15). Formula per year: against gains = min(remaining, gains); against ordinary = min(remaining - against gains, limit); tax saved = gains used x gains rate + ordinary used x ordinary rate; remaining -= both. Example: $20,000, limit 3,000, no gains, 24% -> 6 years at $3,000 then $2,000 in year 7; saved $720 x 6 + $480 = $4,800. Second: $20,000, gains $5,000 a year, 15% / 24% -> years 1 and 2 use $8,000 each (saved $750 + $720 = $1,470 each), year 3 uses $4,000 on gains ($600); total $3,540, 3 years. Say wash sale rules and carryforward character are not modelled. Link `tax-loss-harvesting`, `capital-gains-tax`, `average-cost-basis`.
253. **Heating fuel cost per million BTU** (`everyday`). Inputs for each fuel: price and efficiency %: electricity $/kWh (COP option for heat pumps, default 1), natural gas $/therm, propane $/gal, heating oil $/gal. Editable energy content defaults: 3,412 BTU/kWh, 100,000 BTU/therm, 91,500 BTU/gal propane, 138,500 BTU/gal oil. Formula: cost per MMBtu delivered = price / (BTU per unit x efficiency) x 1,000,000; optional annual heat need in MMBtu x cost = yearly bill. Example: electric $0.17 at COP 1 -> $49.82; gas $1.50 at 95% -> $15.79; propane $3.00 at 90% -> $36.43; oil $4.00 at 85% -> $33.98; heat pump COP 3 -> $16.61. Cite EIA for energy contents after checking. Link `heating-cost`, `fuel-tank-runtime`, `thermostat-setback`.
254. **Marketplace subsidy and premium** (`finance`). Inputs: household income, federal poverty level for the household size (user, from the current HHS table), applicable percentage % (user, from healthcare.gov or the current IRS table), benchmark (second-lowest silver) annual premium, chosen plan annual premium. Formula: income as % of FPL = income / FPL; expected contribution = income x percentage; subsidy = max(0, benchmark - contribution); net premium = max(0, chosen - subsidy); monthly = net / 12. Example: $50,000, 4%, benchmark $9,000, plan $8,400 -> contribution $2,000; subsidy $7,000; net $1,400; $116.67 a month. Flag when income / FPL is above 400% that eligibility depends on current law (check healthcare.gov); estimates only. Link `cobra-vs-marketplace`, `health-plan-comparison`, `hsa-fsa-savings`.
255. **Senior deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: MAGI, filing status, number of people 65 or older (1 or 2), marginal rate %, amount per person, threshold and phase-out rate (editable, defaults $6,000, $75,000 / $150,000 joint, 6%). Formula: deduction = max(0, amount x people - rate x max(0, MAGI - threshold)); tax saved = deduction x marginal rate. Example: single, 1 person, MAGI $95,000, 22% -> 6,000 - 1,200 = $4,800; saves $1,056. Second: joint, 2 people, MAGI $200,000 -> 12,000 - 3,000 = $9,000; saves $1,980. Say it applies with the standard or itemized deduction, runs 2025 to 2028, and the SSN rule applies; check the IRS page. Link `bracket-headroom`, `standard-vs-itemized`, `social-security-cola`.
256. **Moving to a lower-tax state** (`finance`). Inputs: annual income, current effective state (and local) income tax rate %, new state effective rate %, monthly rent or housing change (new minus old, can be negative), one-off moving cost. Formula: tax saved per year = income x (current - new); housing change per year = 12 x monthly change; net per year = tax saved - housing change; net per month = net / 12; payback months = moving cost / net per month (if net <= 0, never). Example: $120,000, 6.0% vs 0%, rent +$300, moving $4,000 -> tax saved $7,200; housing +$3,600; net $3,600 a year ($300 a month); payback 13.3 months. Use the effective rate from last year's state return; no state table, brackets, sales or property tax are modelled; not tax advice. Link `net-to-gross`, `cost-of-living-salary`, `moving-cost`.
257. **Property tax appeal** (`finance`). Inputs: current assessed value, your evidence of market value (comparable sales or appraisal), assessment ratio % (default 100; some counties assess a fraction), tax rate % (or mills / 10), appeal cost (fee, appraisal), years the new value applies (default 3, user), chance of success % (default 100). Formula: target = evidence x ratio; reduction = max(0, assessed - target); saving per year = reduction x rate; net = saving x years - cost; expected net = saving x years x chance - cost. Example: $400,000, $370,000, 100%, 1.8%, $150, 3 years -> reduction $30,000; saves $540 a year; net $1,470; at 50% chance expected $810 - $150 = $660. Say deadlines, forms, and whether rates reset differ by county; the tax rate also changes after reassessment; not legal advice. Link `mortgage`, `rent-vs-buy`, `mortgage-interest-year`.
258. **Phone upgrade vs keep** (`finance`). Inputs: new phone price, trade-in or promo credit, expected resale value of the new phone at the end, years you will keep it, cost to keep the old phone going (battery, screen), extra years you would keep the old one. Formula: new net cost = price - credit - resale; new per month = net / (years x 12); keep per month = old cost / (extra years x 12); difference = new per month - keep per month; extra per year = difference x 12. Example: $999, $300, $150, 3 years; $89 battery, 1 year -> net $549; $15.25 a month; keep $7.42 a month; upgrade costs $7.83 more a month ($94 a year). Say carrier plan pricing and credits over 36 months are not modelled beyond the one credit input. Link `cost-per-use`, `subscription-cost`, `bill-negotiation-savings`.
259. **Standby power cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: up to 8 rows of device name, watts while idle (user's own plug-meter reading), hours a day idle (default 24), quantity; price per kWh. Formula: kWh a year = watts x hours x 365 x quantity / 1000; cost = kWh x price; total of rows. Example: cable box 15 W x 24 h -> 131.4 kWh; console 10 W x 20 h -> 73 kWh; PC 5 W x 16 h -> 29.2 kWh; total 233.6 kWh at $0.17 = $39.71 (22.34 + 12.41 + 4.96). No typical device wattages claimed; ask the user to measure. Link `electricity-cost`, `thermostat-setback`, `energy-upgrade-payback`.
260. **Rideshare vs owning a car** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly fixed costs of the car (payment, insurance, parking, registration / 12), variable cost per mile (fuel, maintenance, tires), miles a month, rides a month, average fare per ride including tip. Formula: own = fixed + miles x per mile; rideshare = rides x fare; saving by riding = own - rideshare (per month and x 12); break-even rides = own / fare. Example: fixed $620, $0.18/mi, 600 mi -> own $728; 30 rides at $14.50 = $435; saves $293 a month ($3,516 a year); break-even 50.2 rides a month. Ignores car sale proceeds, rentals and trips rideshare cannot cover. Link `cost-per-mile`, `keep-vs-replace-car`, `commute-cost`.
261. **Cost per hour of entertainment** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 6 rows of service name, monthly price, hours used a month. Formula: cost per hour = price / hours (no hours = flag as unused); total monthly = sum of prices; cancel saving per year = 12 x price of the highest cost-per-hour row. Example: $17.99 at 20 h -> $0.90 an hour; $11.99 at 4 h -> $3.00; $9.99 at 0.5 h -> $19.98; total $39.97 a month; cancelling the worst saves $119.88 a year. Hours are the user's estimate; no claim about what any service charges. Link `subscription-cost`, `cost-per-use`, `annual-vs-monthly-plan`.
262. **Repair or replace an appliance** (`finance`). Inputs: repair quote, extra years the repair should buy (user guess), new price, years the new one should last (user guess), yearly running-cost saving from the new one (default 0). Formula: repair per year = repair / extra years; new per year = price / years - saving; repair as % of new price = repair / price; difference = repair per year - new per year. Example: $350, 3 years, $900, 10 years, $30 a year saved -> repair $116.67 a year; new $60 a year; repair is 38.9% of the new price; new is $56.67 a year cheaper but needs $900 up front. Label the common "50% of new price" test a rule of thumb; lifespans are guesses; warranty and install costs are not modelled. Link `keep-vs-replace-car`, `energy-upgrade-payback`, `diy-vs-hire`.
263. **EV charging time and cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: battery size kWh, start charge %, end charge %, charger power kW, charging loss % (default 10, labelled an assumption), price per kWh, optional miles per kWh. Formula: battery energy = size x (end - start) / 100; from the wall = battery energy / (1 - loss); hours = wall energy / kW; cost = wall energy x price; miles added = battery energy x mi per kWh. Example: 75 kWh, 20% to 80%, 7.2 kW, 10%, $0.16, 3.5 mi/kWh -> 45 kWh into the battery; 50 kWh from the wall; 6.94 h (6 h 57 min); $8.00; 157.5 miles. Say DC fast charging tapers and is not modelled, and cold weather changes losses. Link `ev-vs-gas-cost`, `gas-cost`, `electricity-cost`.
264. **IRA contribution pace** (`finance`). Inputs: yearly limit (user, from IRS), already contributed, paychecks left before the deadline, optional gross pay per paycheck. Formula: room = max(0, limit - contributed); per paycheck = room / paychecks; percent of pay = per paycheck / gross. Example: limit $7,500, contributed $1,200, 10 paychecks, $2,500 gross -> room $6,300; $630 a paycheck; 25.2% of pay. Say the deadline for prior-year contributions is the filing deadline and income limits apply; the user checks irs.gov. Link `roth-ira`, `401k-max-out`, `savings-goal`.
265. **Free trials you forgot** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 5 rows of name, price after trial, months it ran before you noticed (or will). Formula: cost = price x months; total; annual cost if never cancelled = 12 x price. Example: $14.99 x 5 = $74.95; $9.99 x 8 = $79.92; total $154.87; if kept all year $179.88 and $119.88. Prices are the user's own. Link `subscription-cost`, `annual-vs-monthly-plan`, `cost-per-hour-entertainment`.
266. **Subscription rotation** (`finance`). Inputs: up to 5 rows of name, monthly price, months a year you actually use it. Formula: kept all year = 12 x price; rotating = months x price; saving = difference; total. Example: $17.99, 4 months -> $215.88 vs $71.96, saves $143.92; $11.99, 3 months -> $143.88 vs $35.97, saves $107.91; total saves $251.83. Ignores lost promo prices, price rises and lost watch history. Link `cost-per-hour-entertainment`, `subscription-cost`, `free-trial-forgot-cost`.
267. **Dependent care FSA** (`finance`). Inputs: childcare expenses for the year, FSA election, FSA limit (user, from IRS), federal marginal rate %, FICA % (default 7.65), state rate %. Formula: contribution = min(election, expenses, limit); saved = contribution x (federal + FICA + state). Example: $12,000, election $5,000, limit $5,000, 22%, 7.65%, 5% -> rate 34.65%; saves $1,732.50. Say the child and dependent care credit is reduced by FSA dollars and is not computed; use-it-or-lose-it. Link `childcare-vs-work`, `hsa-fsa-savings`, `fsa-spend-down`.
268. **Trade-in vs private sale** (`finance`). Inputs: trade-in offer, private sale price, selling costs (detailing, repairs, listing), sales tax %, tax credit on trade-in (yes/no), hours spent selling, value of an hour. Formula: trade-in value = offer + (offer x tax % if credit); private net = price - costs; difference = private net - trade-in value; after time = difference - hours x rate. Example: $14,000 offer, $15,500 private, $200 costs, 6% with credit, 8 h at $20 -> trade-in value $14,840; private net $15,300; private ahead $460; $300 after time. Link `auto-loan`, `keep-vs-replace-car`, `car-affordability`.
269. **Late payment cost** (`finance`). Inputs: bill amount, flat late fee, APR % charged on the balance, days late. Formula: interest = bill x APR x days / 365; cost = fee + interest; as % of bill = cost / bill; annualized = cost / bill x 365 / days. Example: $850, $35, 29.99%, 10 days -> interest $6.98; cost $41.98; 4.94% of the bill; 180.3% annualized. Say credit reports and penalty APR terms are not modelled. Link `daily-interest`, `credit-card-payoff`, `bill-negotiation-savings`.
270. **Transit pass break-even** (`everyday`). Inputs: pass price a month, single fare, rides a month. Formula: break-even rides = pass / fare, rounded up; pay-per-ride cost = rides x fare; saving = pay-per-ride - pass (negative means the pass loses); yearly = 12 x. Example: $127, $2.75 -> break-even 46.18, so 47 rides; at 44 rides $121.00, pass costs $6 more; at 60 rides $165.00, pass saves $38 a month ($456 a year). Link `commute-cost`, `real-hourly-wage`, `annual-vs-monthly-plan`.
271. **529 state deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: your contribution this year, state deduction cap (user, per state rules), state tax rate %. Formula: deductible = min(contribution, cap); saved = deductible x rate; room left = max(0, cap - contribution). Example: $6,000, cap $4,000, 5% -> deductible $4,000; saves $200; $0 room left; if $2,500 given, saves $125 and $1,500 room left. Rules, carryforwards and recapture vary by state; not tax advice. Link `charitable-giving-tax-value`, `savings-goal`, `degree-payback`.
272. **Bonus gross-up** (`finance`). Inputs: net bonus you want, federal supplemental rate % (user), FICA % (default 7.65), state rate %. Formula: combined rate = federal + FICA + state; gross = net / (1 - combined); withheld = gross - net. Example: $5,000 net, 22%, 7.65%, 5% -> rate 34.65%; gross $7,651.11; withheld $2,651.11. Withholding is not final tax; true tax settles at filing. Link `bonus-tax`, `bonus-vs-raise`, `net-to-gross`.
273. **Return restocking cost** (`finance`). Inputs: item price, restocking fee %, return shipping, what the item is worth to you if you keep it. Formula: fee = price x %; refund = price - fee - shipping; lost = price - refund; return if refund > worth, gain = refund - worth. Example: $129.99, 15%, $12.00 shipping, worth $80 -> fee $19.50; refund $98.49; lost $31.50; returning gains $18.49. Policies differ by store; check yours. Link `free-shipping-threshold`, `cost-per-use`, `black-friday-deal-check`.
274. **Price adjustment refund** (`finance`). Inputs: price paid, current lower price, quantity, minutes to claim, value of an hour, policy window days, days since purchase. Formula: refund = (paid - now) x qty; time cost = minutes / 60 x rate; net = refund - time cost; days left = window - days since; drop % = (paid - now) / paid. Example: $249.99, $199.99, 2, 15 min, $20, 14-day window, 6 days -> refund $100.00; time cost $5.00; net $95.00; drop 20.0%; 8 days left. Policy and exclusions are the store's; enter yours. Link `black-friday-deal-check`, `discount`, `real-hourly-wage`.
275. **House guest cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: guests, nights, extra food per guest per day, extra household cost per night (heat, water, laundry), other costs (outings, bedding). Formula: food = guests x nights x food rate; household = nights x rate; total = food + household + other; per guest night = total / (guests x nights). Example: 2, 5, $18, $6, $40 -> food $180; household $30; total $250; $25.00 per guest night. All rates are the user's own estimates. Link `event-cost-per-guest`, `holiday-spending-total`, `heating-cost`.
276. **Winter mpg penalty** (`everyday`). Inputs: miles a month, mpg in mild weather, winter drop % (user's own estimate), fuel price per gallon, winter months. Formula: winter mpg = mpg x (1 - drop); extra gallons = miles / winter mpg - miles / mpg; extra cost = gallons x price; season = x months. Example: 1,000 mi, 30 mpg, 15%, $3.40, 4 months -> winter mpg 25.5; extra 5.88 gal; $20.00 a month; $80.00 a season. Say the drop varies by car and trip length; not a measured figure. Link `gas-cost`, `cost-per-mile`, `winter-tire-cost`.
277. **Ship vs store gift** (`everyday`). Inputs: online price, shipping, store price, round-trip miles, cost per mile (user), minutes in store, value of an hour. Formula: online = price + shipping; store = price + miles x rate; time cost = minutes / 60 x rate; store with time = store + time; difference = store with time - online. Example: $59.99, $7.95, $64.99, 12 mi, $0.67, 40 min, $20 -> online $67.94; store $73.03; time $13.33; store with time $86.36; online cheaper by $18.42 (by $5.09 before time). Delivery date risk is not modelled. Link `free-shipping-threshold`, `cost-per-mile`, `real-hourly-wage`.
278. **Gift tax annual exclusion** (`finance`). Inputs: annual exclusion per recipient (user, from IRS), up to 4 recipients with gifts this year, gift splitting with a spouse yes/no. Formula: limit = exclusion x (2 if split); room = max(0, limit - gift); excess = max(0, gift - limit); total excess. Example: exclusion $19,000, gifts $15,000, $19,000, $25,000, no split -> room $4,000, $0, $0; excess $0, $0, $6,000; total $6,000. With split (limit $38,000) -> room $23,000, $19,000, $13,000; excess $0. Excess means a Form 709 filing, not necessarily tax due; not tax advice. Link `charitable-giving-tax-value`, `gift-budget`, `donate-stock-vs-cash`.
279. **HSA prorated limit** (`finance`). Inputs: annual HSA limit (user, from IRS), months eligible this year, contributed so far, pay periods left. Formula: prorated limit = limit x months / 12; room = max(0, prorated - contributed); per pay period = room / periods. Example: $4,300, 8 months, $1,500, 10 periods -> prorated $2,866.67; room $1,366.67; $136.67 per period. The last-month rule and its testing period can allow the full limit; not computed. Link `hsa-fsa-savings`, `hsa-invest-vs-spend`, `pretax-contribution-cost`.
280. **Social Security wage base paycheck bump** (`finance`). Inputs: annual gross pay, pay periods a year, Social Security wage base (user, from SSA), employee rate % (default 6.2). Formula: per period = gross / periods; taxable periods = wage base / per period (the period that crosses the cap pays tax only on the remainder); bump per paycheck = per period x rate. Example: $240,000, 26 periods, base $170,000 (illustrative) -> $9,230.77 per period; cap reached in period 19 (18 full periods = $166,153.85, period 19 taxed on $3,846.15 = $238.46); bump $572.31 from period 20; 7 untaxed paychecks add $4,006.15; total SS tax $10,540.00 = 6.2% of the base. Employee share only; Medicare unaffected; not for self-employed. Link `take-home-pay`, `paychecks-per-year`, `self-employment-tax`.
281. **Child tax credit** (`finance`). Inputs: children, credit per child (user), MAGI, filing status threshold (user, default $200,000 / $400,000 joint), reduction per $1,000 or part (default $50). Formula: reduction = 50 x ceil(max(0, MAGI - threshold)/1000); credit = max(0, children x amount - reduction). Example: 2 children, $2,000, MAGI $420,000 joint, threshold $400,000 -> reduction 20 x $50 = $1,000; credit $3,000. MAGI $500,000 -> reduction $5,000; credit $0. Refundable part, SSN rules and other-dependent credit not computed. Link `income-tax`, `take-home-pay`, `dependent-care-fsa`.
282. **QCD tax value** (`finance`). Inputs: RMD for the year, planned QCD, QCD cap (user, from IRS), federal marginal rate %, state rate %, whether you would itemize (yes/no). Formula: qcd = min(planned, cap); counts toward RMD = min(qcd, RMD); taxable RMD left = RMD - counts; AGI cut = qcd; tax saved = qcd x (federal + state) when you would not itemize (a cash gift would be no deduction). Example: RMD $20,000, QCD $8,000, 24%, 5% -> taxable RMD left $12,000; tax saved $2,320. Say the QCD must go straight from the IRA to a qualifying charity and be done by Dec 31; not tax advice. Link `charitable-giving-tax-value`, `donate-stock-vs-cash`.
283. **Social Security earnings test** (`finance`). Inputs: earnings limit (user, from SSA), expected yearly earnings, monthly benefit, withholding ratio (default $1 per $2). Formula: withheld = max(0, earnings - limit) x ratio; months withheld = withheld / monthly benefit; benefits paid = 12 x benefit - withheld (floor 0). Example: limit $24,480 (illustrative), earnings $40,000, $2,000 a month -> withheld $7,760; 3.88 months (4 full months); paid $16,240. Say withheld months are credited back after full retirement age and the test ends then; the higher first-year-of-retirement limit is not computed. Link `social-security-breakeven`, `retirement-countdown`.
284. **Home sale exclusion** (`finance`). Inputs: sale price, selling costs %, purchase price, improvements, exclusion (user: $250,000 single, $500,000 joint), long-term gain rate % (user). Formula: gain = price x (1 - costs%) - purchase - improvements; taxable = max(0, gain - exclusion); tax = taxable x rate. Example: $650,000, 6%, $300,000, $50,000, $250,000, 15% -> gain $261,000; taxable $11,000; tax $1,650. Joint $500,000 -> $0. Say the two-of-five-years ownership and use test and depreciation recapture are not computed. Link `home-sale-net-proceeds`, `capital-gains-tax`.
285. **Saver's credit** (`finance`). Inputs: contributions, per-person cap (user, default $2,000), AGI, three tiers of AGI ceiling and rate (user, from the IRS table for the filing status), tax liability. Formula: rate = first tier with AGI at or below the ceiling, else 0; credit = min(rate x min(contribution, cap), tax liability). Example: $1,500, AGI $30,000 single with tiers 50% to $23,000, 20% to $25,000, 10% to $38,250 (illustrative) -> 10%; $150. AGI $22,000, liability $100 -> 50% x $1,500 = $750, capped to $100. Student and dependent exclusions not computed. Link `roth-ira`, `ira-contribution-pace`.
286. **Taxable Social Security** (`finance`). Inputs: annual benefits, other income, tax-exempt interest, base thresholds (user, default $25,000 and $34,000 single; $32,000 and $44,000 joint), marginal rate %. Formula: provisional = other + exempt + 0.5 x benefits; tier 1 = min(0.5 x (base2 - base1), 0.5 x benefits); taxable = min(0.85 x benefits, tier 1 + 0.85 x max(0, provisional - base2)) when provisional is over base2, else min(0.5 x benefits, 0.5 x (provisional - base1)); tax = taxable x rate. Example: $24,000, $30,000 other, single, 12% -> provisional $42,000; tier 1 $4,500; 0.85 x $8,000 = $6,800; taxable $11,300 (47.1%); tax $1,356. Link `roth-conversion`, `income-tax`.
287. **American Opportunity credit** (`finance`). Inputs: qualified expenses, first-tier amount (default $2,000 at 100%), second tier (default next $2,000 at 25%), MAGI, phase-out start and end (user, default $80,000 to $90,000 single), refundable share (default 40%). Formula: full = first + 25% x min(second, expenses - first); factor = clamp((end - MAGI)/(end - start), 0, 1); credit = full x factor; refundable = credit x 40%. Example: $5,000 expenses, MAGI $85,000 -> full $2,500; factor 0.5; credit $1,250; refundable $500. Four-year limit and eligibility not computed. Link `credits-to-graduate`, `degree-payback`.
288. **Roast cooking time** (`food`). Inputs: weight (lb, kg toggle), minutes per pound (user), resting minutes (default 20), serve time. Formula: cook = weight x min per lb; start = serve - cook - rest. Example: 12 lb, 15 min per lb, 20 min rest, serve 17:30 -> cook 180 min; start 14:10. Say the user enters the minutes per lb from their recipe and checks internal temperature with a thermometer against the USDA chart; no time is promised. Link `turkey-cooking-time`, `dinner-timeline`.
289. **College net price** (`finance`). Inputs: tuition and fees, room and board, books and other, grants and scholarships per year, years (default 4). Formula: cost = tuition + room + books; net = cost - grants; total = net x years; covered = grants / cost. Example: $28,000, $14,000, $2,000, grants $18,000 -> cost $44,000; net $26,000 per year; $104,000 over 4 years; 40.9% covered. Say figures come from the award letter and costs usually rise each year (not modelled). Link `credits-to-graduate`, `degree-payback`.
290. **Tax refund splitter** (`finance`). Inputs: refund, share to debt % and its APR, share to savings % and its APY, remainder is spending or investing (no return assumed). Formula: debt = refund x share; savings = refund x share; year-one gain = debt x APR + savings x APY. Example: $2,400, 50% to debt at 22%, 30% to savings at 4% -> $1,200 and $720, remainder $480; gain $264 + $28.80 = $292.80. Say simple annual interest, no compounding, rates are the user's. Link `refund-interest-forgone`, `debt-vs-invest`.
291. **PTO payout value** (`finance`). Inputs: unused hours, hourly rate, federal withholding % (user), FICA % (default 7.65), state % (user). Formula: gross = hours x rate; net = gross x (1 - fed - FICA - state). Example: 80 h, $30, 22%, 7.65%, 4% -> gross $2,400; withholding 33.65% = $807.60; net $1,592.40. Say payout rules vary by state and employer and the final tax bill can differ from withholding. Link `pto-calculator`, `bonus-tax`.
292. **Use tax on online purchases** (`finance`). Inputs: untaxed purchases total, state rate %, local rate %, tax already paid. Formula: use tax = total x (state + local) - already paid, floor 0. Example: $1,200, 6% + 1.5% = 7.5% -> $90; with $30 already paid -> $60. Say rules and thresholds vary by state and the user enters the rates. Link `sales-tax`, `tax-refund-estimator`.
293. **Calorie surplus for a lean bulk** (`health`). Inputs: maintenance kcal (user, from `tdee`), daily surplus (default 300), kcal per lb (default 3,500, editable), weeks. Formula: intake = maintenance + surplus; weekly gain = surplus x 7 / 3,500; total = weekly x weeks. Example: 2,600 maintenance, 300 surplus -> intake 2,900; 0.6 lb per week; 7.2 lb in 12 weeks. Say gain is not all muscle and the 3,500 rule is an approximation. Link `tdee`, `weight-change-rate`.
294. **Cycling power to weight** (`sports`). Inputs: FTP watts or 20-minute average, test factor % (user, default 95), body weight (kg, lb toggle). Formula: FTP = 20-min avg x factor; W/kg = FTP / kg. Example: 260 W average, 95%, 75 kg -> FTP 247 W; 3.29 W/kg. Direct FTP 250 W -> 3.33 W/kg. No fitness category is claimed. Link `heart-rate-zones`, `race-time-predictor`.
295. **Swim pace per 100** (`sports`). Inputs: distance, unit (m or yd), time, pool length (default 25), optional target time. Formula: pace per 100 = time / distance x 100; lengths = distance / pool; per length = time / lengths. Example: 1,500 m in 30:00 -> 1,800 s / 15 = 120 s = 2:00 per 100 m; 60 lengths; 30 s per length. Link `pace`, `race-time-predictor`.
296. **Humidifier runtime** (`everyday`). Inputs: tank size (L, gal toggle), output per hour (mL), hours per day, watts, $/kWh, days (default 30). Formula: run on one tank = tank / output; water per day = output x hours; refills per day = water per day / tank; cost = watts x hours / 1000 x rate x days. Example: 4.5 L, 300 mL/h, 12 h, 25 W, $0.16 -> 15 h per tank; 3.6 L per day; 0.8 refills per day; $0.048 per day; $1.44 over 30 days. Say output varies with settings and the user reads it off the label. Link `electricity-cost`, `standby-power-cost`.
297. **Withholding adjuster** (`finance`). Inputs: projected balance due at filing (enter a refund as a negative number), refund you want (default 0), paychecks left this year. Formula: change needed = balance due + wanted refund (positive = withhold more, negative = withhold less); per paycheck = change / paychecks. Example: owe $1,800, want $0, 12 paychecks -> $1,800 / 12 = $150.00 extra per paycheck. Second: refund $2,400 expected (-2,400), want $400 -> -2,400 + 400 = -$2,000; 26 paychecks -> $76.92 less withheld per paycheck. Say the projection comes from the user's own estimate or `tax-refund-estimator`, the Form W-4 line is the user's to complete, and this is not tax advice. Link `tax-refund-estimator`, `refund-interest-forgone`, `take-home-pay`.
298. **Student loan interest deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: interest paid (Form 1098-E), cap (user, illustrative $2,500), MAGI, phase-out start and end (user, illustrative $85,000 to $100,000 single), marginal rate %. Formula: base = min(interest, cap); factor = clamp((end - MAGI) / (end - start), 0, 1); deduction = base x factor; tax saved = deduction x rate. Example: $2,100, cap $2,500, MAGI $90,000, range $85,000 to $100,000, 22% -> base $2,100; factor 10,000 / 15,000 = 0.6667; deduction $1,400.00; saves $308.00. Second: $3,000, MAGI $70,000, 12% -> base $2,500; factor 1; saves $300.00. Say limits and filing status rules are the user's to check on irs.gov; not tax advice. Link `student-loan-payoff`, `bracket-headroom`, `aotc-credit`.
299. **College meal plan value** (`finance`). Inputs: plan price for the term, meals included, meals you actually use, what a comparable meal costs you otherwise. Formula: per meal paid = price / included; per meal used = price / used; unused value = (included - used) x per meal paid; alternative cost = used x other cost; plan minus alternative = price - alternative cost. Example: $2,400, 150 meals, 105 used, $9 otherwise -> $16.00 per meal paid; $22.86 per meal used; 45 unused = $720.00; alternative $945.00; plan costs $1,455.00 more. Second: $1,800, 180 meals, 170 used, $11 -> $10.00; $10.59; unused $100.00; alternative $1,870.00; plan saves $70.00. Say convenience, dining dollars and rollover rules are not modelled. Link `college-net-price`, `eating-out-cost`, `meal-kit-vs-groceries`.
300. **Student work hours to cover a gap** (`finance`). Inputs: money gap, hourly wage, % withheld from pay (user), weeks available.
