Formula: net per hour = wage x (1 - withheld); hours = gap / net per hour; per week = hours / weeks. Example: $5,200, $15, 10%, 15 weeks -> net $13.50; 385.19 hours; 25.68 hours a week. Second: $1,200, $18, 8%, 12 weeks -> $16.56; 72.46 hours; 6.04 hours a week. Say work-study earnings rules and class load are not modelled. Link `college-net-price`, `hours-of-work-cost`, `salary-to-hourly`.
301. **Carpool and road trip cost share** (`everyday`). Inputs: round-trip miles, mpg, fuel price, tolls and parking, wear per mile (user, default 0), passengers besides the driver. Formula: fuel = miles / mpg x price; wear = miles x rate; total = fuel + tolls + wear; per person = total / (passengers + 1). Example: 600 mi, 28 mpg, $3.20, $24 tolls, $0.10 wear, 3 passengers -> fuel $68.57; wear $60.00; total $152.57; $38.14 each. Second: 250 mi, 25 mpg, $3.40, no tolls, $0.12 wear, 1 passenger -> fuel $34.00; wear $30.00; total $64.00; $32.00 each. Say whether the driver pays a share is the group's choice (the toggle is the default). Link `rideshare-vs-own-car`, `cost-per-mile`, `gas-cost`.
302. **Per diem vs actual spend** (`finance`). Inputs: daily meals and incidentals rate (user, from the employer or travel policy), trip days, first and last day share (default 75%, editable), actual spend. Formula: allowed days = (days - 2) + 2 x share; allowance = rate x allowed days; difference = allowance - actual. Example: $68 a day, 4 days, 75% -> 2 + 1.5 = 3.5 days; $238.00; actual $190 -> $48.00 kept. Second: $59, 3 days -> 1 + 1.5 = 2.5 days; $147.50; actual $201 -> $53.50 short. Say no government rate is built in and the employer policy decides. Link `business-mileage-deduction`, `write-off-value`, `commute-cost`.
303. **Tip pool split** (`everyday`). Inputs: pooled tips, up to 6 rows of name and hours worked, optional tip-out % and the sales it applies to. Formula: rate = pool / total hours; share = hours x rate; tip-out = sales x %; kept = share - tip-out. Example: $480, hours 8, 6, 4 -> 18 h; $26.67 an hour; $213.33, $160.00, $106.67. Second: $300 tips, $1,200 sales, 3% tip-out -> $36.00 out; $264.00 kept. Say the venue's pooling policy decides who is eligible. Link `tip`, `bill-splitter`, `holiday-tipping-budget`.
304. **Heat cable cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: watts per foot (from the label), length in feet, % of time the thermostat has it on, hours a day it is plugged in (default 24), days, $/kWh. Formula: watts = per ft x length; kWh a day = watts x hours x % / 1000; cost = kWh a day x days x price. Example: 5 W/ft, 30 ft, 50%, 24 h, 90 days, $0.17 -> 150 W; 1.8 kWh a day; 162 kWh; $27.54. Second: 3 W/ft, 50 ft, 100%, 24 h, 30 days, $0.15 -> 150 W; 3.6 kWh a day; 108 kWh; $16.20. Say duty cycle depends on weather and the label wattage applies. Link `electricity-cost`, `standby-power-cost`, `heating-cost`.
305. **Medical expense deduction floor** (`finance`). Inputs: AGI, floor % (user, illustrative 7.5), medical expenses, other itemized deductions, standard deduction (user), marginal rate %. Formula: floor = AGI x %; deductible = max(0, medical - floor); benefit = max(other + deductible, standard) - max(other, standard); tax saved = benefit x rate; gap to floor = max(0, floor - medical). Example: AGI $80,000, 7.5% -> floor $6,000; medical $9,500 -> deductible $3,500; other $12,000, standard $14,600 -> 15,500 - 14,600 = $900; at 22% saves $198.00. Second: AGI $60,000 -> floor $4,500; medical $4,000 -> deductible $0; saves $0; $500.00 short of the floor. Say the floor, standard deduction and rate are the user's to check on irs.gov, qualifying expense rules are not computed; not tax advice. Link `standard-vs-itemized`, `hsa-invest-vs-spend`, `write-off-value`.
306. **Furnace filter cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: price per filter, filters per change, days between changes, optional option B price and days. Formula: changes a year = 365 / days; annual = changes x filters x price; per month = annual / 12; difference = A - B. Example: A $8, 1 filter, 60 days -> 6.0833 changes; $48.67 a year; $4.06 a month. B $22, 180 days -> 2.0278 changes; $44.61; A costs $4.06 more a year. Second: $15, 2 filters, 90 days -> 4.0556 changes; $121.67; $10.14 a month. Say the change interval comes from the filter label or HVAC advice, none is built in. Link `heating-cost`, `energy-upgrade-payback`.
307. **Dishwasher vs hand washing** (`everyday`). Inputs: water per dishwasher load (gal, L toggle), kWh per load, water per load of hand washing, kWh of hot water per equivalent load, water price per unit, $/kWh, loads per week. Formula: cost per load = water x water price + kWh x rate; yearly difference = (hand - dishwasher) x loads x 52. Example: dishwasher 4 gal, 0.9 kWh; hand 15 gal, 1.5 kWh; $0.005 per gal, $0.16 -> $0.164 vs $0.315; $0.151 saved per load; 5 loads a week = 260 -> $39.26 a year. Second: 3 gal, 1.2 kWh vs 8 gal, 1.0 kWh; $0.004, $0.20 -> $0.252 vs $0.232; hand washing cheaper by $0.020; 7 loads = 364 -> $7.28 a year. Say all usage figures are the user's estimates and detergent and time are not counted. Link `laundry-cost`, `shower-cost`, `electricity-cost`.
308. **Semester refund budget** (`finance`). Inputs: refund received, weeks in the term, fixed costs for the term (rent, food plan), planned one-offs. Formula: free = refund - fixed - one-offs; per week = free / weeks; per day = per week / 7 (negative = short). Example: $3,200, 15 weeks, fixed $1,800, one-offs $350 -> free $1,050; $70.00 a week; $10.00 a day. Second: $1,500, 16 weeks, $1,200, $400 -> short $100; -$6.25 a week. Say a refund of borrowed aid is not free money (interest and repayment not modelled) and the school's terms apply. Link `college-net-price`, `student-work-hours-gap`, `paycheck-bill-planner`.
309. **Treadmill incline gain** (`sports`). Inputs: belt distance (mi or km), incline %, optional minutes. Formula: climb = distance x incline % (treadmill distance treated as the slope length, a small-angle approximation); feet per mile equivalent = 52.8 x incline %; climb rate per hour = climb / minutes x 60. Example: 3 mi at 8% -> 15,840 ft x 0.08 = 1,267.2 ft (386.2 m); 422.4 ft per mile; in 45 min = 1,689.6 ft per hour. Second: 5 km at 3% -> 150 m = 492.1 ft; 158.4 ft per mile. Say no calories are claimed and the console's own incline calibration may differ. Link `slope`, `pace`, `calories-burned`.
310. **Rowing split** (`sports`). Inputs: distance (m), time (mm:ss), optional target split. Formula: split per 500 m = time / distance x 500; time for a target = target / 500 x distance. Example: 2,000 m in 7:30 (450 s) -> 112.5 s = 1:52.5 per 500 m. Second: 5,000 m in 21:40 (1,300 s) -> 130 s = 2:10.0; at a 2:00 target over 2,000 m -> 480 s = 8:00. Say watts are not computed. Link `swim-pace-per-100`, `pace`, `race-time-predictor`.
311. **Utility split by days present** (`everyday`). Inputs: bill total, up to 5 rows of name and days present. Formula: person-days = sum of days; rate = bill / person-days; share = days x rate. Example: $150, A 30 days, B 30, C 10 -> 70 person-days; $2.1429 each; $64.29, $64.29, $21.43 (sums to $150.00 before rounding). Second: $90, A 28, B 14 -> 42; $2.1429; $60.00 and $30.00. Say fixed charges and heavy-use differences are the housemates' to agree. Link `bill-splitter`, `prorated-rent`, `rent-split`.
312. **Oven vs small appliance cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: appliance A name, average watts, minutes (include preheat); appliance B the same; $/kWh; meals per week. Formula: kWh = watts x minutes / 60 / 1000; cost = kWh x rate; yearly saving = (A - B) x meals x 52. Example: oven 1,500 W, 50 min -> 1.25 kWh; air fryer 1,400 W, 20 min -> 0.4667 kWh; $0.16 -> $0.2000 vs $0.0747; $0.1253 saved per meal; 4 a week = 208 -> $26.07 a year. Second: slow cooker 200 W, 8 h (1.6 kWh) vs oven 1,500 W, 1.5 h (2.25 kWh), $0.20 -> $0.32 vs $0.45; $0.13 saved; 2 a week = 104 -> $13.52 a year. Say watts are average draw read off the label or a plug meter. Link `oven-cost`, `electricity-cost`, `eating-out-cost`.
313. **QBI deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: qualified business income, taxable income before QBI, net capital gain, deduction rate % (user, illustrative 20), marginal rate %. Formula: deduction = min(rate x QBI, rate x max(0, taxable income - net capital gain)); tax saved = deduction x marginal rate. Example: QBI $60,000, taxable $70,000, no gains, 20% -> min(12,000, 14,000) = $12,000; at 22% saves $2,640.00. Second: taxable $40,000 with $5,000 gains -> min(12,000, 0.2 x 35,000 = 7,000) = $7,000; at 12% saves $840.00. Say income thresholds, wage and property limits and excluded trades are not computed; not tax advice. Link `self-employment-tax`, `estimated-tax-safe-harbor`.
314. **Income-driven student loan payment** (`finance`). Inputs: AGI, poverty guideline for household size (user), protected income % (user, illustrative 150), plan % of discretionary income (user, illustrative 10). Formula: discretionary = max(0, AGI - protected% x guideline); yearly = plan% x discretionary; monthly = yearly / 12. Example: AGI $50,000, guideline $15,650, 150% -> protected $23,475, discretionary $26,525, yearly $2,652.50, monthly $221.04. Second: AGI $30,000 -> discretionary $6,525 -> $54.38 a month. Say plan names, percentages and guidelines change; check studentaid.gov; forgiveness and interest are not modelled. Link `student-loan-payoff`, `student-loan-interest-deduction`.
315. **Inherited stock basis** (`finance`). Inputs: shares, original cost per share, value per share at date of death (user), sale price per share, capital gains rate % (user). Formula: gain with step-up = (sale - value at death) x shares; gain on carryover basis = (sale - original) x shares; tax = max(0, gain) x rate; tax avoided = carryover tax - step-up tax. Example: 100 shares, cost $20, value at death $80, sale $85, 15% -> step-up gain $500, tax $75.00; carryover gain $6,500, tax $975.00; avoided $900.00. Say the step-up applies to most inherited assets but not all (retirement accounts, some trusts); estate rules not computed; not tax advice. Link `average-cost-basis`, `gains-harvest-headroom`.
316. **72(t) SEPP payment** (`finance`). Inputs: account balance, interest rate % (user; check the IRS ceiling), payout years (user, from an IRS life expectancy table), penalty rate % (user, illustrative 10). Formula (fixed amortization): payment = B x i / (1 - (1+i)^-n); penalty avoided per year = payment x penalty%. Example: $500,000, 5%, 35 years -> $30,535.85 a year, penalty avoided $3,053.59 a year. Second: $300,000, 4.5%, 30 years -> $18,417.46. Say the payments must continue the longer of five years or until 59.5 and a change can trigger back penalties; other methods are not computed; not tax advice. Link `early-withdrawal-penalty`, `fire-calculator`.
317. **Prepay property tax** (`finance`). Inputs: state and local taxes already paid this year, next installment prepaid, SALT cap (user), other itemized deductions, standard deduction (user), marginal rate %. Formula: SALT deductible = min(cap, paid + prepay); itemized = SALT + other; with = max(itemized, standard); without = max(min(cap, paid) + other, standard); gain = with - without; saved = gain x rate. Example: paid $7,000, prepay $4,000, cap $10,000, other $6,000, standard $14,600, 24% -> without max(13,000, 14,600) = $14,600; with max(16,000, 14,600) = $16,000; gain $1,400, saves $336.00. Second: paid $10,000 already at the cap -> gain $0. Say a bill must be assessed to be deductible in some places and the cap is the user's to check. Link `standard-vs-itemized`, `property-tax-appeal`.
318. **Net investment income tax** (`finance`). Inputs: MAGI, threshold for filing status (user), net investment income, rate % (user, illustrative 3.8). Formula: tax = rate x min(NII, max(0, MAGI - threshold)). Example: MAGI $260,000, threshold $200,000, NII $40,000 -> min(40,000, 60,000) = $40,000 -> $1,520.00. Second: MAGI $230,000, NII $80,000 -> min(80,000, 30,000) = $30,000 -> $1,140.00. Show the MAGI cut that would remove the tax. Say what counts as investment income is not computed; not tax advice. Link `capital-gains-tax`, `gains-harvest-headroom`.
319. **Home energy credit payback** (`finance`). Inputs: project cost, credit % (user), annual credit cap (user, 0 = none), tax liability available this year, savings per year. Formula: credit = min(cost x %, cap if cap > 0); usable = min(credit, liability); net cost = cost - usable; payback years = net / savings; unused credit = credit - usable. Example: $12,000, 30%, cap $2,000, liability $6,000, saves $600 -> credit $2,000, net $10,000, payback 16.7 years vs 20.0 without. Second: $20,000, 30%, no cap, liability $4,000, saves $1,000 -> credit $6,000, usable $4,000, unused $2,000, net $16,000, 16.0 vs 20.0 years. Say carryforward and eligibility rules are not computed. Link `energy-upgrade-payback`, `heating-cost`.
320. **Running shoe cost** (`sports`). Inputs: shoe price, expected life in miles (user), miles per week. Formula: cost per mile = price / life; weeks per pair = life / weekly miles; pairs a year = 52 x weekly / life; yearly cost = pairs x price. Example: $140, 400 miles, 20 a week -> $0.35 a mile, 20 weeks a pair, 2.6 pairs, $364.00 a year. Say shoe life varies and the user should enter their own. Link `pace`, `cost-per-use`.
321. **Wash sale basis** (`finance`). Inputs: shares sold, sale price per share, original cost per share, shares bought back inside the window, repurchase price per share. Formula: loss = (cost - sale) x sold; washed shares = min(sold, rebought); disallowed = loss x washed / sold; allowed = loss - disallowed; new basis per share = repurchase price + disallowed / washed. Example: sell 100 at $40 from $50, rebuy 60 at $42 -> loss $1,000, disallowed $600, allowed $400, new basis $52.00 a share. State the 30-day-before-and-after rule as something to verify. Link `tax-loss-harvesting`, `capital-loss-carryover`.
322. **Tuition payment plan cost** (`finance`). Inputs: tuition bill, enrollment fee, instalments, annual rate you could otherwise earn or pay (%). Formula: instalment = (bill) / n; extra cost = fee; interest benefit of delaying = sum over k of instalment_k x rate/12 x months delayed (first due now); net cost = fee - benefit. Example: $6,000, $50 fee, 5 equal payments due at months 0-4, 0% -> plan costs $50 more than paying in full. Link `college-net-price`.
323. **Overdraft fee cost** (`finance`). Inputs: fee per overdraft, overdrafts per year, average amount overdrawn, days until repaid. Formula: yearly cost = fee x count; APR equivalent = (fee / amount) x (365 / days) x 100. Example: $35 fee, $12 overdrawn, 3 days -> yearly cost at 3 a year $105; APR = 35/12 x 365/3 = 354.9 as a multiple, i.e. about 35,486% APR. Say fees and grace rules vary by bank. Link `late-payment-cost`.
324. **Underpayment penalty** (`finance`). Inputs: required payment per quarter (4 values), paid per quarter, days late per quarter, annual penalty rate (user, from irs.gov). Formula: shortfall_q = max(0, required - paid); penalty_q = shortfall_q x rate x days late / 365; total = sum. Example: shortfall $2,500, 90 days late, 8% -> $49.32. Estimate only; Form 2210 rules decide. Link `estimated-tax-safe-harbor`.
325. **Pace splits** (`sports`). Inputs: distance, goal time, split style (even / negative / positive), split percent (default 2), split unit km or mile. Formula: even pace = time / distance; negative split: first half pace = even x (1 + p), second half = even x (1 - p); positive is the reverse; equal halves so the total equals goal. Example: 42.195 km in 4:00:00, 2% negative -> even 341.3 s/km (5:41); first half 348.1 s/km (5:48), second half 334.5 s/km (5:34); halves sum to 4:00:00. Link `pace`, `race-time-predictor`.
326. **VO2 max estimate** (`health`). Inputs: method (resting heart rate or 12-minute run), max heart rate, resting heart rate, or metres run in 12 minutes. Formula: Uth = 15.3 x HRmax / HRrest; Cooper = (distance m - 504.9) / 44.73. Example: HRmax 190, HRrest 60 -> 48.5; 2,800 m -> 51.3. Note estimates carry error and are not medical advice. Link `heart-rate-zones`.
327. **CD early break** (`finance`). Inputs: balance, current CD rate (%), new rate (%), months left, penalty in months of interest, term of new account equals months left. Formula: penalty = balance x rate/12 x penalty months; gain = balance x new/12 x months left - balance x rate/12 x months left; net = gain - penalty (simple interest). Example: $10,000, 4%, 5%, 12 months left, 3-month penalty -> penalty $100, gain $100, net $0.00 (break-even). Link `cd-ladder`, `savings-account-switch`.
328. **Tax set-aside** (`finance`). Inputs: invoice amount, self-employment tax % (user), federal marginal %, state %, deductible expenses %. Formula: profit = invoice x (1 - expenses%); set aside = profit x (SE x 0.9235 + fed + state) with SE and rates user-entered; keep = invoice - expenses - set aside. Example: $1,000, 0% expenses, SE 15.3, fed 22, state 5 -> set aside 1000 x (0.153 x 0.9235 + 0.22 + 0.05) = $411.30, keep $588.70. Not tax advice. Link `self-employment-tax`, `estimated-tax-safe-harbor`.
329. **Average daily balance interest** (`finance`). Inputs: APR %, cycle days, list of (balance, days). Formula: ADB = sum(balance x days) / cycle days; interest = ADB x APR/365 x cycle days. Example: $1,000 for 10 days, $1,500 for 20 days, 24% -> ADB 1,333.33, interest 26.30. Link `credit-card-payoff`.
330. **Security deposit return** (`finance`). Inputs: deposit, deductions list, months held, annual interest % (user, 0 allowed). Formula: refund = deposit + deposit x rate x years - sum(deductions). Example: $1,500, deductions $200 + $150, 1% for 1 year -> refund 1,500 + 15 - 350 = $1,165. Say local law varies; no state rule coded. Link `renew-vs-move`, `moving-cost`.
331. **EV winter range** (`everyday`). Inputs: rated range, cold loss %, rated kWh per 100 mi, price per kWh. Formula: cold range = rated x (1 - loss); cold kWh/100 mi = rated / (1 - loss); extra cost per 100 mi = (cold - rated) x price. Example: 300 mi, 30%, 30 kWh, $0.15 -> 210 mi, 42.86 kWh, extra $1.93 per 100 mi. Link `ev-charging-time`, `winter-mpg-penalty`.
332. **Bulk buy break-even** (`finance`). Inputs: bulk pack units and price, regular unit price, use per week, shelf life weeks. Formula: used = min(units, use x shelf life); effective unit price = bulk price / used; break-even used = bulk price / regular unit price. Example: 48 units $24, regular $0.75, 2 per week, 16 weeks -> used 32, effective $0.75 = break-even 32. Link `unit-price`, `warehouse-membership-break-even`.
333. **Water heater cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: gallons per day, inlet F, set F, efficiency, price per kWh. Formula: kWh/day = gal x 8.34 x (set - inlet) / 3412 / efficiency; yearly = x price x 365. Example: 40 gal, 55 to 120 F, 0.92, $0.16 -> 6.91 kWh/day, $1.11/day, $403.4/year. Link `shower-cost`, `electricity-cost`.
334. **Holiday weight burn-off** (`health`). Inputs: pounds gained, kcal per lb (default 3,500, editable, say it is a rough rule), daily deficit. Formula: days = lb x kcal / deficit. Example: 4 lb, 3,500, 500 -> 28 days. Not medical advice. Link `weight-loss-goal`, `calories-burned`.
335. **Minimum payment trap** (`finance`). Inputs: balance, APR %, minimum = max(floor, percent of balance + optional interest), fixed-payment comparison. Month loop until balance 0 (cap 1,200 months). Example first month: $3,000, 24% APR, 1% + interest, floor $25 -> interest $60, payment $90. Engineer computes totals by script and cross-checks `credit-card-payoff`. Link `credit-card-payoff`, `debt-payoff-planner`.
336. **Bonus allocation split** (`finance`). Inputs: gross bonus, federal withholding %, FICA %, state %, split percents (must sum to 100). Formula: net = gross x (1 - rates); bucket = net x percent. Example: $5,000, 22 + 7.65 + 0 -> net $3,517.50; 50/20/20/10 -> 1,758.75 / 703.50 / 703.50 / 351.75. Rates are user inputs; not tax advice. Link `bonus-tax`, `bonus-vs-raise`.
337. **Long-term gain wait** (`finance`). Inputs: shares, cost per share, price per share, short-term rate %, long-term rate %, days until the holding period passes (user). Formula: V = price x shares; C = cost x shares; tax now = s x (V - C); tax later at the same price = l x (V - C); saving = difference; break-even value P*n = (V(1 - s) + C(s - l)) / (1 - l), i.e. the price at which waiting nets the same as selling now; drop % = 1 - P*n / V. Example: 100 shares, $50 cost, $100 price, 32% and 15% -> tax now $1,600; later $750; saves $850; break-even $90.00 a share, a 10.0% drop (check: 9,000 - 0.15 x 4,000 = 8,400 = 10,000 - 1,600). Second: 40 shares, $30, $55, 24% and 15% -> $240 vs $150, saves $90; break-even $52.35, a 4.81% drop. Say the holding-period rule, rates and any NIIT are the user's to check; market risk while waiting is the point of the break-even; not tax advice. Link `capital-gains-tax`, `gains-harvest-headroom`, `tax-loss-harvesting`.
338. **Self-employed health insurance deduction** (`finance`). Inputs: premiums paid for the year, net self-employment profit, self-employment tax paid (user; half is subtracted), marginal rate %. Formula: limit = max(0, profit - SE tax / 2); deduction = min(premiums, limit); unused = premiums - deduction; tax saved = deduction x rate. Example: profit $70,000, SE tax $9,891, premiums $7,200 -> limit $65,054.50; deduction $7,200; at 22% saves $1,584.00. Second: profit $6,000, SE tax $848, premiums $9,600 -> limit $5,576; deduction $5,576; unused $4,024; at 12% saves $669.12. Say eligibility (for example being eligible for an employer plan) and the premium credit interaction are not computed; not tax advice. Link `self-employment-tax`, `marketplace-subsidy`, `write-off-value`.
339. **Unemployment benefit runway** (`finance`). Inputs: weekly benefit, % withheld for tax (user), benefit weeks, savings, monthly expenses. Formula: net monthly = weekly x (1 - tax) x 52 / 12; gap = expenses - net monthly; benefit months = weeks x 12 / 52; if gap x benefit months > savings, savings run out at savings / gap months, else leftover = savings - gap x benefit months and total runway = benefit months + leftover / expenses. Example: $450, 10%, 26 weeks, $4,000, $2,800 -> net $1,755; gap $1,045; savings run out at month 3.83. Second: $600, 15%, 26 weeks, $8,000, $2,500 -> net $2,210; gap $290; 6 months use $1,740; leftover $6,260 lasts 2.50 more months; runway 8.50 months. Say benefit amounts, duration and eligibility are set by the state and the user enters their own; no state formula is built in. Link `savings-runway`, `severance-pay`, `emergency-fund`.
340. **Holiday shift worth** (`finance`). Inputs: hours, base hourly rate, pay multiplier (user), tax % on the extra pay, extra costs for the day (childcare, travel). Formula: gross = hours x rate x multiplier; net = gross x (1 - tax) - costs; net per hour = net / hours; compare with regular after-tax hourly = rate x (1 - tax). Example: 8 h, $20, 1.5x, 15%, $40 costs -> gross $240; $204 after tax; $164.00 net; $20.50 an hour vs $17.00 regular. Second: 10 h, $18, 2.0x, 22%, $60 -> $360; $280.80; $220.80; $22.08 vs $14.04. Say the employer policy sets the multiplier and whether a paid holiday is separate; time with family is not priced. Link `overtime-pay`, `hours-of-work-cost`, `real-hourly-wage`.
341. **Scholarship hourly value** (`other`). Inputs: award, chance of winning % (user guess), hours per application, applications you will send, optional hourly wage to compare. Formula: expected value = award x p; per hour = EV / hours; applications for one expected win = 1 / p; hours for that = hours / p; chance of at least one win = 1 - (1 - p)^n. Example: $2,000, 5%, 6 h, 10 applications -> EV $100.00; $16.67 an hour; 20 applications and 120 hours per expected win; 40.13% chance of at least one in 10. Second: $500, 2%, 4 h -> $10.00; $2.50 an hour; 50 applications and 200 hours; 18.29% for 10. Say odds are guesses, treated as independent and not guaranteed; effort on a strong essay can be reused. Link `college-net-price`, `student-work-hours-gap`, `hours-of-work-cost`.
342. **College application budget** (`other`). Inputs: schools, average application fee, fee waivers, score reports sent and fee each, transcript fee per school, other costs. Formula: fees = (schools - waivers) x fee; scores = sent x fee each; transcripts = schools x fee; total = sum + other; per school = total / schools. Example: 8 schools, $60, 2 waived, 6 reports at $13, $5 transcripts -> $360 + $78 + $40 = $478.00; $59.75 a school. Second: 5 schools, $75, 0 waived, 5 reports at $12, $0 transcripts -> $375 + $60 = $435.00; $87.00 each. Say every fee is the user's and waiver eligibility is set by each school. Link `college-net-price`, `textbook-cost`, `semester-refund-budget`.
343. **Bodyweight strength ratio** (`sports`). Inputs: lift weight, bodyweight, target multiple (user), weekly gain you expect, unit lb or kg. Formula: ratio = lift / bodyweight; target lift = multiple x bodyweight; gap = max(0, target - lift); weeks = gap / weekly gain. Example: 185 lb bench, 175 lb body, 1.25x, 2.5 lb a week -> ratio 1.06x; target 218.75; gap 33.75; 13.5 weeks. Second: 225 squat, 160 body, 1.5x -> 1.41x; target 240; gap 15; 6.0 weeks at 2.5. Say no strength standard is built in, gains slow with training age and the plan is not coaching. Link `one-rep-max`, `plate-calculator`, `progressive-overload`.
344. **Personal heater cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: for options A and B, name, watts, hours a day, duty cycle %; price per kWh; days. Formula: kWh a day = watts x hours x duty / 1000; cost a day = kWh x price; season = x days; difference = B - A. Example: blanket 100 W, 8 h, 100% -> 0.8 kWh; heater 1,500 W, 4 h, 60% -> 3.6 kWh; $0.16 -> $0.128 vs $0.576 a day; 90 days $11.52 vs $51.84; blanket saves $40.32. Second: 60 W, 10 h, 50% (0.3 kWh) vs 750 W, 6 h, 100% (4.5 kWh), $0.20, 60 days -> $3.60 vs $54.00; saves $50.40. Say watts come from the label, thermostat cycling varies and whole-house savings from a lower setpoint are in `thermostat-setback`. Link `thermostat-setback`, `heating-cost`, `electricity-cost`.
345. **Snow shoveling calories** (`health`). Inputs: bodyweight, unit lb or kg, minutes shoveled, MET (user; heavy shoveling is often quoted around 6, check the Compendium), treat calories (default 250). Formula: kg = lb x 0.45359237; kcal = MET x kg x hours; minutes to burn the treat = treat / (MET x kg / 60). Example: 180 lb (81.6466 kg), 60 min, MET 6 -> 489.9 kcal; 250 kcal treat takes 30.6 min. Second: 150 lb (68.0389 kg), 45 min, MET 5 -> 255.2 kcal. Say MET is a population average and heart-health caution for shoveling is a medical question, not computed. Link `calories-burned`, `holiday-weight-burn-off`, `snow-removal-payback`.
346. **Draft sealing payback** (`everyday`). Inputs: yearly heating cost, percent saved (user guess), cost of materials, hours of DIY work (optional), value of an hour (optional, default 0), years to project. Formula: saved a year = cost x pct; payback months = (materials + hours x value) / saved x 12; net after N years = saved x N - upfront. Example: $1,800, 10%, $60 -> $180 a year; payback 4 months; 10-year net $1,740. Second: $1,200, 5%, $90 -> $60 a year; payback 18 months; 5-year net $210. Say real savings depend on the house and are usually estimated by an energy audit. Link `heating-cost`, `thermostat-setback`, `home-energy-credit-payback`.
347. **Hot tub cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: kWh a day (user, from a meter or the spec sheet), price per kWh, chemicals and filters per month, soaks per month, months. Formula: power a month = kWh x price x 30; monthly = power + chemicals; per soak = monthly / soaks; total = monthly x months. Example: 6 kWh, $0.16, $25, 20 soaks, 12 months -> power $28.80; monthly $53.80; per soak $2.69; year $645.60. Second: 4 kWh, $0.20, $15, 8 soaks, 12 months -> $24.00; $39.00; $4.88 a soak; $468.00. Say cold weather and cover quality change kWh. Link `electricity-cost`.
348. **Holiday turkey size** (`food`). Inputs: adults, children, child portion (user, default 0.5 adult), pounds per adult (user, default 1.5 with leftovers, 1 without), pounds thawed per day in the fridge (user, default 4). Formula: portions = adults + children x child portion; pounds = portions x lb per adult; thaw days = ceil(pounds / lb per day). Example: 8 adults, 4 children at 0.5, 1.5 lb -> 10 portions; 15 lb; thaw 4 days (15 / 4 = 3.75). Second: 5 adults, 0 children, 1.25 lb -> 6.25 lb; thaw 2 days. Say thaw guidance should be checked against USDA before it is quoted. Link `turkey-cooking-time`, `party-food-quantity`.
349. **Sugary drink swap** (`health`). Inputs: drinks a week, kcal each, price each, replacement price each (default 0), weeks (default 52), kcal per pound (user, default 3,500). Formula: kcal saved = drinks x kcal x weeks; pounds = kcal saved / kcal per lb; dollars = drinks x (price - replacement) x weeks. Example: 7, 150 kcal, $1.50, $0.25, 52 -> 54,600 kcal; 15.6 lb; $455.00. Second: 3, 140 kcal, $2.00, $0, 52 -> 21,840 kcal; 6.24 lb; $312.00. Say 3,500 kcal per pound is a rough rule and weight change is not guaranteed. Link `food-burn-off`, `latte-factor`, `dry-january`.
350. **Ski day cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: lift ticket, rental, lesson, travel and parking, food, hours on snow, days, runs a day (optional). Formula: per day = sum of costs; trip = per day x days; per hour = per day / hours; per run = per day / runs. Example: $120 + $50 + $0 + $40 + $25 = $235 a day; 4 days $940; 5 h -> $47.00 an hour; 12 runs -> $19.58. Second: $90 + $0 + $80 + $30 + $20 = $220; 3 days $660; 4 h -> $55.00 an hour. Say a season pass is compared in `season-pass-break-even`. Link `trip-budget`, `season-pass-break-even`.
351. **Dehumidifier cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: watts, hours a day, duty cycle %, price per kWh, days, pints removed a day (user). Formula: kWh a day = watts x hours x duty / 1000; cost a day = kWh x price; season = x days; per pint = cost a day / pints. Example: 500 W, 12 h, 80%, $0.16, 90 days, 30 pints -> 4.8 kWh; $0.768 a day; $69.12; $0.0256 a pint. Second: 300 W, 10 h, 100%, $0.20, 60 days -> 3 kWh; $0.60; $36.00. Say pints a day depend on humidity and the label rating is for lab conditions. Link `electricity-cost`, `humidifier-runtime`.
352. **Faucet drip cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: mode (drips a minute, or gallons a minute), drips a minute, mL per drip (user, default 0.05), gallons a minute, hours a night, nights, water and sewer price per 1,000 gal (user). Formula: drip mode gal a day = drips x mL x 1,440 / 3,785.41; trickle mode gal a night = gpm x 60 x hours; cost = gal / 1000 x price. Example: 60 drips, 0.05 mL, 24 h, 30 days, $10 -> 1.141 gal a day; 34.2 gal; $0.34. Second: 0.25 gpm, 8 h, 5 nights, $10 -> 120 gal a night; 600 gal; $6.00. Say whether to drip is a plumbing and local-advice question, and a burst-pipe cost is not computed. Link `shower-cost`, `water-heater-cost`.
353. **Aquarium water change** (`everyday`). Inputs: length, width, height (in), substrate and decor displacement % (user, default 0), change % (user, default 25). Formula: gallons = L x W x H / 231 x (1 - displacement); change gal = gallons x change %; liters = gal x 3.78541. Example: 48 x 13 x 21 in, 0%, 25% -> 56.73 gal; change 14.18 gal. Second: 20 x 10 x 12, 0%, 30% -> 10.39 gal; change 3.12 gal. Say fish-safe change size depends on species and water tests. Link `pet-lifetime-cost`.
354. **Diaper cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: diapers a day, price each, months, cloth upfront cost, cloth laundry cost a month. Formula: disposable = diapers x 30.4375 x months x price; monthly = diapers x 30.4375 x price; cloth break-even months = upfront / (monthly - laundry), shown only if monthly > laundry. Example: 8 a day, $0.30, 12 months -> 2,922 diapers; $876.60; monthly $73.05; cloth $400 and $15 laundry -> 6.89 months. Second: 6, $0.25, 24 months -> 4,383; $1,095.75. Say diapers a day fall with age and prices are user inputs. Link `childcare-vs-work`.
355. **Shooting efficiency** (`sports`). Inputs: field goals made, 3-pointers made, field goal attempts, free throws made, free throw attempts. Formula: points = 2 x (FG - 3P) + 3 x 3P + FT; eFG% = (FG + 0.5 x 3P) / FGA; TS% = points / (2 x (FGA + 0.44 x FTA)). Example: FG 8, 3P 3, FGA 15, FT 4, FTA 4 -> 23 pts; eFG 63.33%; TS 68.62%. Second: FG 10, 3P 0, FGA 20, FT 5, FTA 6 -> 25 pts; eFG 50.00%; TS 55.21%. Say the 0.44 free throw weight is a convention. Link `baseball-stats`.
356. **Bike gear speed** (`sports`). Inputs: chainring teeth, cog teeth, cadence rpm, wheel circumference (m, user, default 2.096), unit. Formula: ratio = chainring / cog; m per min = rpm x ratio x circumference; km/h = m per min x 0.06; mph = km/h / 1.609344. Example: 50/17, 90 rpm, 2.096 m -> ratio 2.941; 554.82 m/min; 33.29 km/h; 20.69 mph. Second: 39/11, 80 rpm, 2.1 m -> 3.545; 595.64 m/min; 35.74 km/h. Say tyre circumference varies by tyre and pressure. Link `cycling-power-to-weight`.
357. **School lunch cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: buy price per lunch, packed cost per lunch, school days (user, default 180), children. Formula: buy total = price x days x children; pack total = cost x days x children; savings = buy - pack. Example: $4.25, $2.10, 180 days, 1 -> $765.00; $378.00; saves $387.00. Second: $3.50, $1.50, 175 days -> $612.50; $262.50; saves $350.00. Say free and reduced lunch eligibility is not modelled. Link `recipe-cost`, `meal-kit-vs-groceries`.
358. **Average speed over two legs** (`math`). Inputs: distance and speed for leg 1 and leg 2. Formula: time = d1/s1 + d2/s2; average = (d1 + d2) / time. Example: 60 mi at 30 mph, 60 mi at 60 mph -> 3 h; 40 mph (not 45). Second: 100 km at 80, 50 km at 50 -> 2.25 h; 66.67 km/h. Say the plain average of speeds is wrong unless the times are equal. Link `distance-rate-time`.
359. **Cat litter cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: litter boxes, full changes a month, lb per full change, top-off lb a week, bag price, bag lb. Formula: lb a month = boxes x changes x lb + top-off x 4.345; cost = lb x price / bag lb; year = x 12; bags = lb / bag lb. Example: 1 box, 2 changes, 10 lb, 1 lb top-off, $20, 40 lb -> 24.345 lb; $12.17 a month; $146.07 a year. Second: 2 boxes, 4 changes, 8 lb, 0 top-off, $16, 40 lb -> 64 lb; $25.60; $307.20. Say box hygiene advice is out of scope. Link `pet-food-duration`.
360. **3D print cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: grams used, spool price per kg, print hours, printer watts, price per kWh, failure rate %. Formula: material = g / 1000 x price; energy = watts x hours / 1000 x kWh price; cost = (material + energy) / (1 - failure rate). Example: 120 g, $20/kg, 5 h, 120 W, $0.16, 10% -> $2.40 + $0.096 = $2.496; $2.77 per good part. Second: 50 g, $25/kg, 2 h, 100 W, $0.20, 0% -> $1.25 + $0.04 = $1.29. Say printer wear and time are not included. Link `electricity-cost`.
361. **Dry pasta portions** (`food`). Inputs: people, dry grams per person (default 85, labelled typical, editable), pack size g, pack price. Formula: total g = people x g; packs to buy = ceil(total / pack); cost used = total / pack x price; per serving = cost used / people. Example: 6 people, 85 g, 500 g pack, $2.00 -> 510 g; 2 packs; $2.04 used; $0.34 per serving. Second: 4 people, 100 g, 454 g box, $1.80 -> 400 g; 1 box; $1.59; $0.40. Say cooked weight and sauce are out of scope. Link `recipe-cost`.
362. **Sweat rate** (`health`). Inputs: weight before kg, weight after kg, fluid drunk L, urine L, minutes. Formula: sweat L = (before - after) + drunk - urine (1 kg of loss is about 1 L); rate L/h = sweat / (minutes / 60); body mass lost % = (before - after) / before x 100. Example: 70.0 -> 69.0 kg, 0.5 L drunk, 0 urine, 60 min -> 1.5 L; 1.5 L/h; 1.43%. Second: 80 -> 78.4, 0.3 drunk, 0.1 urine, 90 min -> 1.8 L; 1.2 L/h; 2.0%. Say plainly it is an estimate, not medical advice. Link `water-intake`.
363. **Elo rating change** (`sports`). Inputs: your rating, opponent rating, K factor (default 32, labelled editable), result (win 1, draw 0.5, loss 0). Formula: E = 1 / (1 + 10^((opp - you) / 400)); new = you + K x (S - E). Example: 1500 vs 1700, K 32, win -> E 0.2402; change +24.3; 1524.3 (opponent loses 24.3 if same K). Second: 1800 vs 1500, K 32, loss -> E 0.8490; change -27.2; 1772.8. Third: 1600 vs 1600, K 20, draw -> 0. Say federations use their own K and rules.
364. **Geometric and harmonic mean** (`math`). Inputs: list of positive numbers; mode toggle for growth factors (enter percent returns, convert to 1 + r). Formula: AM = sum / n; GM = (product)^(1/n); HM = n / sum(1/x). Example: 2, 8 -> AM 5, GM 4, HM 3.2. Second: 1, 3, 9 -> GM 3, AM 4.333, HM 2.0769. Growth mode: +10%, -20%, +30% -> product 1.144, GM 1.0458, so 4.58% a year versus a 6.67% arithmetic average. Reject zero or negatives in GM and HM with a plain message. Link `average-speed-two-legs`, `mean-median-mode`.
365. **Oven temperature** (`conversion`). Inputs: temperature, unit (F, C, gas mark), fan oven toggle with reduction (default 20 C, labelled a common rule, check the oven manual). Formula: C = (F - 32) x 5/9; F = C x 9/5 + 32; gas mark g (1 to 9) F = 275 + 25 x (g - 1); nearest gas mark = round((F - 275) / 25 + 1), only shown when 1 to 9. Example: 350 F -> 176.7 C; gas mark 4; fan 156.7 C. Second: gas mark 6 -> 400 F -> 204.4 C; fan 184.4 C. Third: 180 C -> 356 F -> gas mark 4.24, shown as 4. Link `temperature-converter`.
366. **Rafter length** (`construction`). Inputs: building span ft, pitch as rise per 12 in run, horizontal overhang ft. Formula: run = span / 2; rise = run x pitch / 12; length = sqrt(run^2 + rise^2); overhang along slope = overhang x sqrt(1 + (pitch / 12)^2); total = length + that. Example: 12 ft span, 6/12, 1 ft overhang -> run 6, rise 3, 6.708 ft + 1.118 = 7.826 ft. Second: 24 ft, 8/12, 1.5 ft -> run 12, rise 8, 14.422 ft + 1.803 = 16.225 ft. Say ridge board, birdsmouth cut and local code are out of scope. Link `roofing`.
367. **Video bitrate to file size** (`developer`). Inputs: video kbps, audio kbps, duration; reverse mode: target size MB and duration. Formula: MB = (video + audio) kbps x seconds / 8 / 1000 (decimal); MiB = bytes / 1,048,576; reverse: Mbps = MB x 8 / seconds. Example: 8000 + 128 kbps, 10 min -> 4,876,800 kbit -> 609.6 MB (581.4 MiB). Second: 2500 kbps, 90 min -> 1,687.5 MB. Reverse: 25 MB over 120 s -> 1.667 Mbps total. Say container overhead is ignored. Link `download-time`.
368. **SMS segments** (`text`). Inputs: character count, encoding (GSM-7 or Unicode, user selected; do not claim to detect it), price per segment. Formula: single message limit 160 (GSM-7) or 70 (Unicode); if longer, segments = ceil(chars / 153) or ceil(chars / 67); cost = segments x price. Example: 300 GSM-7 chars -> 2 segments; at $0.01 -> $0.02. 161 chars -> 2; 160 -> 1. Unicode 150 chars -> 3. Verify limits against 3GPP TS 23.040 and say carriers may differ. Link `character-counter`.
369. **Swimming pool volume** (`construction`). Inputs: shape (rectangle or round), length and width or diameter (ft), average depth (ft), water price per 1,000 gal (optional). Formula: rectangle ft3 = L x W x depth; round = pi x (d/2)^2 x depth; gallons = ft3 x 7.48052; liters = gal x 3.78541. Example: 20 x 10 x 5 -> 1,000 ft3 -> 7,480.5 gal; at $5 per 1,000 gal -> $37.40. Round 15 ft diameter x 4 ft -> 706.86 ft3 -> 5,287.7 gal. Say sloped floors need an average depth. Link `concrete`.
370. **Snow depth to water and roof load** (`everyday`). Inputs: snow depth (in), snow-to-liquid ratio (default 10, user editable), roof area (ft2, optional). Formula: water in = depth / ratio; load lb/ft2 = water in x 5.2 (62.4 lb/ft3 / 12); total lb = load x area. Example: 12 in at 10:1 -> 1.2 in water -> 6.24 lb/ft2 -> 6,240 lb on 1,000 ft2. 6 in at 15:1 -> 0.4 in. State it is an estimate, not a structural check. Link `snow-removal-payback`.
371. **Wall heat loss** (`construction`). Inputs: wall area (ft2), R-value now, R-value after, indoor-outdoor gap (F), hours per day. Formula: BTU/h = area x gap / R; kWh = BTU/h x hours / 3412.14. Example: 100 ft2, R-13, 40 F -> 307.7 BTU/h (4,000 / 13); R-21 -> 190.5; saving 117.2 BTU/h. 307.7 BTU/h for 24 h -> 7,384.6 BTU -> 2.164 kWh. Say it is steady-state and ignores air leaks and studs. Link `heating-cost`.
372. **Decibel addition** (`math`). Inputs: list of source levels in dB. Formula: total = 10 x log10(sum of 10^(L/10)). Example: 60 + 60 -> 63.01 dB; 70 + 60 -> 70.41 dB (10 x log10(1.1e7)); three 80 dB -> 84.77 dB (80 + 10 x log10 3). Say it assumes uncorrelated sources at one point. Link `log`.
373. **Aspect ratio and resize** (`developer`). Inputs: width, height, new width or new height. Formula: ratio = w/g : h/g with g = gcd(w, h); new height = new width x h / w. Example: 1920 x 1080 -> g = 120 -> 16:9; new width 1280 -> 720. 1080 x 1350 -> g = 270 -> 4:5. 1000 x 750 -> 4:3; width 640 -> 480. Round to whole pixels and say so. Link `ratio`.
374. **Screen pixel density** (`developer`). Inputs: horizontal px, vertical px, diagonal (in). Formula: PPI = sqrt(w^2 + h^2) / diagonal. Example: 2560 x 1440 at 27 in -> sqrt(8,627,200) = 2,937.2 -> 108.79 PPI. 1920 x 1080 at 24 in -> sqrt(4,852,800) = 2,202.9 -> 91.79 PPI. Also show dot pitch = 25.4 / PPI mm (108.79 -> 0.233 mm). Link `ratio`.
375. **Density, mass and volume** (`math`). Inputs: any two of density, mass, volume with unit selectors. Formula: density = mass / volume; mass = density x volume; volume = mass / density. Example: 500 g in 250 cm3 -> 2 g/cm3. 0.92 g/cm3 x 500 mL -> 460 g. Below 1 g/cm3 floats in fresh water (0.92 does). Link `cooking-converter`.
376. **Lease mileage overage** (`finance`). Inputs: lease months, annual allowance, miles driven so far, months elapsed, fee per mile, optional prepaid-mile price. Formula: allowance total = annual x months / 12; projected miles = driven / elapsed x months; overage = max(0, projected - allowance); cost = overage x fee. Example: 36 mo, 12,000/yr -> 36,000; 20,000 mi in 18 mo -> 40,000 projected -> 4,000 over x $0.25 = $1,000. At $0.10 prepaid -> $400, saves $600. Fees come from the user's contract. Link `car-lease-vs-buy`.
377. **Rule of 78 payoff rebate** (`finance`). Inputs: total finance charge, term months n, payments made k. Formula: sum of digits S = n(n+1)/2; interest earned to date = F x (n + (n-1) + ... + (n-k+1)) / S; rebate = F - earned. Example: F $600, n 12, k 3 -> S 78, earned = 600 x 33/78 = $253.85, rebate $346.15 (remaining digits 45, 600 x 45/78 = 346.15). Also show the straight-line rebate 600 x 9/12 = $450 for contrast. Contract and state rules vary; say so.
378. **Insurance premium refund on cancel** (`finance`). Inputs: premium paid, policy days (default 365), days in force, short-rate penalty % of unearned (default 0). Formula: unearned = premium x (term - used)/term; refund = unearned x (1 - penalty). Example: $1,200, 365 days, cancel at day 100 -> unearned 1,200 x 265/365 = $871.23; at 10% penalty -> $784.11. Link `prorated-salary`.
379. **Excess baggage fee check** (`everyday`). Inputs: weight limit per bag, fee per overweight bag, up to 4 bag weights, unit lb or kg. Formula: bags over = count(w > limit); fee = bags over x fee; pooled test: if sum(w) <= limit x bags, a repack avoids all fees. Example: limit 50, bags 52, 44, 50 -> 1 over, fee $100; total 146 <= 150, so move 2 lb from bag 1 and pay $0. If bags are 52, 48, 61 -> total 161 > 150, 11 lb must come out, fee cannot be avoided. Airline limits and fees are the user's input.
380. **Dew point and window condensation** (`everyday`). Inputs: air temp, relative humidity %, optional window surface temp; C or F toggle. Formula (Magnus, a = 17.62, b = 243.12 C; verify constants): g = ln(RH/100) + a T/(b + T); Td = b g/(a - g). Example: 20 C, 50% -> g 0.6462, Td 9.26 C; 0 C, 80% -> Td -3.04 C. Condensation if surface temp <= Td. Not a mould diagnosis.
381. **Moving average** (`statistics`). Inputs: list of numbers, window size w. Formula: simple moving average of each w consecutive values (output length n - w + 1). Example: 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, w 3 -> 5, 7, 9. Weight example: 82.0, 81.6, 81.9, 81.2, 80.9, w 3 -> 81.83, 81.57, 81.33. Chart: raw line vs average line.
382. **Skill practice hours planner** (`everyday`). Inputs: goal hours, minutes per session, sessions per week, start date. Formula: weeks = goal x 60 / (minutes x sessions); finish date = start + weeks x 7. Example: 100 h, 20 min, 5 per week -> 6,000 / 100 = 60 weeks (420 days). At 40 min -> 30 weeks. Show 10,000 hours as a joke preset (10,000 h at 1 h/day, 7 days a week = 10,000/365 = 27.4 years).
383. **Balloon loan payment** (`finance`). Inputs: principal, annual rate, amortization months, balloon month. Formula: payment = P r / (1 - (1+r)^-n) with r monthly; balloon = P(1+r)^m - pmt x ((1+r)^m - 1)/r. Example: $10,000, 12% (1%/mo), n 24, m 12 -> payment $470.73, balloon $5,298.16, total paid by month 12 = $5,648.82. Link `mortgage`, `interest-only-loan`.
384. **Coefficient of variation** (`statistics`). Inputs: list of numbers, population or sample. Formula: CV = SD / mean x 100%. Example: 2, 4, 4, 4, 5, 5, 7, 9 -> mean 5, population SD 2 -> CV 40%; sample SD sqrt(32/7) = 2.138 -> 42.76%. Undefined when mean is 0. Link `standard-deviation`.
385. **Mean arterial pressure** (`health`). Inputs: systolic, diastolic (mmHg). Formula: MAP = (SBP + 2 x DBP) / 3; pulse pressure = SBP - DBP. Example: 120/80 -> MAP 93.3, pulse pressure 40. Second: 140/90 -> MAP 106.7, pulse pressure 50. Reject diastolic >= systolic. Not medical advice; cite a standard physiology source. Link `bmi`.
386. **Calories from label macros** (`food`). Inputs: carbs g, protein g, fat g, alcohol g (optional), label kcal (optional). Formula: kcal = 4 x carbs + 4 x protein + 9 x fat + 7 x alcohol; share of each = its kcal / total. Example: 20 g carbs, 10 g protein, 5 g fat -> 80 + 40 + 45 = 165 kcal (48.5%, 24.2%, 27.3%); add 10 g alcohol -> 235 kcal. Second: label 150 kcal with 25 / 5 / 6 g -> 100 + 20 + 54 = 174 kcal, 24 kcal (16%) above the label. Say labels round and fibre rules differ. Link `macro`.
387. **Pythagorean win expectation** (`sports`). Inputs: runs or points scored, allowed, games, actual wins (optional), exponent (default 2, labelled editable; baseball often uses about 1.83). Formula: W% = RS^x / (RS^x + RA^x); expected wins = W% x games. Example: 800 scored, 700 allowed, x 2, 162 games -> 640,000 / 1,130,000 = 0.5664 -> 91.75 wins. Second: 650 / 750 -> 422,500 / 985,000 = 0.4289 -> 69.49 wins. With 95 actual wins in the first case: +3.25 over expectation.
388. **Stopping distance** (`everyday`). Inputs: speed (km/h or mph), reaction time s (default 1.5, editable), surface friction (dry 0.7, wet 0.4, ice 0.15, labelled typical, editable). Formula: v in m/s; reaction = v x t; braking = v^2 / (2 x mu x 9.81); total = sum. Example: 50 km/h = 13.889 m/s, 1.5 s -> reaction 20.83 m; dry braking 192.90 / 13.734 = 14.05 m -> 34.88 m; wet 192.90 / 7.848 = 24.58 m -> 45.41 m; ice 192.90 / 2.943 = 65.55 m -> 86.38 m. Say real stopping depends on tyres, brakes and slope. Link `winter-tire-cost`.
389. **Rainwater harvest** (`construction`). Inputs: roof catchment area ft2, rainfall in, collection efficiency % (default 85, editable). Formula: gallons = area x rain x 7.48052 / 12 (0.62338 gal per ft2 per inch) x efficiency. Example: 1,000 ft2, 1 in, 100% -> 623.4 gal; at 85% -> 529.9 gal. Second: 1,500 ft2, 2 in, 90% -> 1,870.1 x 0.9 = 1,683.1 gal. Say local rules on collection vary. Link `gravel`.
390. **Blood glucose converter** (`conversion`). Inputs: value, direction (mg/dL to mmol/L or back). Formula: mmol/L = mg/dL / 18.016 (glucose molar mass 180.16 g/mol / 10); mg/dL = mmol/L x 18.016. Example: 100 mg/dL -> 5.55 mmol/L; 7.0 mmol/L -> 126.1 mg/dL; 180 mg/dL -> 9.99 mmol/L. Many tools round the factor to 18; show both. Not medical advice; verify the factor against a source. Link `pressure-converter`.
391. **Binomial probability** (`statistics`). Inputs: trials n, success probability p, k. Formula: P(X = k) = C(n,k) p^k (1-p)^(n-k); mean np; SD sqrt(np(1-p)); cumulative sums. Example: n 10, p 0.5, k 3 -> 120 x 0.125 x 0.0078125 = 0.1172; P(X <= 3) = (1 + 10 + 45 + 120) / 1024 = 0.1719; P(X >= 3) = 1 - 56/1024 = 0.9453. Second: n 5, p 0.2, k 2 -> 10 x 0.04 x 0.512 = 0.2048; mean 1, SD 0.894. Link `permutation-combination`.
392. **Payday loan APR** (`finance`). Inputs: amount borrowed, fee, term days, number of rollovers (default 0). Formula: APR = fee / amount x 365 / days; fees paid = fee x (1 + rollovers). Example: $100, $15 fee, 14 days -> 0.15 x 26.071 = 391.1%. Second: $300, $45 fee, 14 days -> same 391.1%; $500, $100 fee, 30 days -> 0.2 x 12.167 = 243.3%. Third: $100 at $15 rolled over 3 times -> $60 in fees (60% of the amount). Simple-interest APR, not compounded; local limits vary. Link `loan`.
393. **Twelve days of Christmas** (`everyday`). Inputs: days (1-12, default 12), optional average price per gift ($, blank = skip). Formula: gift number i (1 = partridge, 12 = drummers) is given (n - i + 1) times, i x (n - i + 1) items in all; total = n(n+1)(n+2)/6; gifts on day d = d(d+1)/2. Example: n 12 -> 364 gifts (day 12 alone: 78); 12 x 13 x 14 / 6 = 364. With $50 each -> $18,200. n 5 -> 5 x 6 x 7 / 6 = 35. Chart: gifts per day cumulative. Share: "my true love sent me 364 gifts T_T". Link `holiday-spending-total`, `gift-budget`.
394. **Hanukkah candles** (`everyday`). Inputs: nights (1-8, default 8), menorahs (default 1), shamash candle yes/no (default yes), candles per box (default 44, editable). Formula: per menorah = nights(nights+1)/2 + (shamash ? nights : 0); total = that x menorahs; boxes = ceil(total / box). Example: 8 nights, 1 menorah -> 36 + 8 = 44 candles, 1 box. 3 menorahs -> 132, 3 boxes. 5 nights, no shamash -> 15. Say start dates vary by year; check a calendar. Link `candle-burn-time`.
395. **Heating degree days** (`everyday`). Inputs: average daily high, average daily low, days, base temperature (default 65 F, or 18 C with unit toggle), optional last year's HDD. Formula: daily HDD = max(0, base - (high + low) / 2); total = daily x days. Example: high 40, low 20, 31 days -> mean 30, 35 per day, 1,085 total; vs last year 930 -> +16.7% (1085 / 930 - 1). Another: high 60, low 50 -> mean 55 -> 10. High 80, low 60 -> mean 70 -> 0. Source: NOAA CPC / EIA definition (65 F base). Link `heating-cost`, `thermostat-setback`.
396. **Santa sleigh speed** (`other`). Inputs: households visited (default 2,000,000,000, labelled assumption), average distance between stops km (default 0.5), hours (default 32). Formula: distance = households x hop; speed km/s = distance / (hours x 3600); fraction of light = speed / 299,792.458; Mach = speed / 0.343 km/s. Example: 2e9 x 0.5 = 1e9 km; 32 h = 115,200 s -> 8,680.6 km/s = 2.9% of light speed, about Mach 25,300. Playful, say so; no seriousness claim. Link `distance-rate-time`.
397. **Roof snow load** (`construction`). Inputs: ground snow load pg psf, exposure Ce (fully exposed 0.9, partially 1.0, sheltered 1.2: verify), thermal Ct (heated 1.0, unheated 1.2: verify), importance Is (default 1.0), roof area ft2. Formula: flat-roof pf = 0.7 x Ce x Ct x Is x pg (ASCE 7-16 Eq. 7.3-1); total lb = pf x area; tons = lb / 2000. Example: pg 40, Ce 1, Ct 1, Is 1 -> 28 psf; 1,500 ft2 -> 42,000 lb = 21 tons. Not a structural design; sloped roofs and drifts need Cs and local code. Link `roofing`, `snow-water-equivalent`.
398. **Advent calendar cost** (`finance`). Inputs: price, days (default 24), items per day (default 1), optional loose retail value per item. Formula: per day = price / days; per item = price / (days x items); premium = price - items x retail; premium % = premium / (items x retail). Example: $30, 24 days -> $1.25 per day; retail $0.75 -> value $18, premium $12 (66.7%). Another: $60, 24 days, 2 items, $1 each -> $2.50 per day, $1.25 per item, value $48, premium $12 (25%). Link `cost-per-use`, `holiday-spending-total`.
399. **NFL passer rating** (`sports`). Inputs: completions C, attempts A, yards Y, touchdowns TD, interceptions INT. Formula (NFL): a = (C/A - 0.3) x 5, b = (Y/A - 3) x 0.25, c = TD/A x 20, d = 2.375 - INT/A x 25; clamp each to 0..2.375; rating = (a + b + c + d) / 6 x 100. Example: 25/40, 300 yds, 3 TD, 1 INT -> 1.625 + 1.125 + 1.5 + 1.75 = 6.0 -> 100.0. Perfect game -> 158.3. Link `baseball-stats`.
400. **Poisson probability** (`statistics`). Inputs: mean rate lambda, k. Formula: P(X = k) = e^-lambda x lambda^k / k!; cumulative sums; mean = variance = lambda. Example: lambda 3, k 2 -> 0.049787 x 4.5 = 0.2240; P(X <= 2) = 0.049787 x 8.5 = 0.4232; P(X >= 1) = 1 - 0.049787 = 0.9502. Link `binomial-probability`, `probability`.
401. **Uptime and downtime** (`developer`). Inputs: availability % (or pick 99, 99.9, 99.99, 99.999), period. Formula: downtime = (1 - availability) x period; year 8,760 h, 30-day month 43,200 min, day 86,400 s. Example: 99.9% -> 8.76 h per year, 43.2 min per 30-day month, 86.4 s per day. 99.99% -> 52.56 min per year. 99% -> 87.6 h per year. Link `download-time`.
402. **Alcohol units and calories** (`health`). Inputs: drink volume (ml, with oz toggle), ABV %, number of drinks. Formula: UK units = ml x ABV% / 1000; ethanol ml = ml x ABV% / 100; grams = ethanol ml x 0.789 (verify); kcal from alcohol = grams x 7; US standard drinks = grams / 14. Example: 750 ml at 13% -> 9.75 units, 97.5 ml ethanol, 76.9 g, 538 kcal, 5.5 US standard drinks. Second: 500 ml at 5% -> 2.5 units, 19.7 g, 138 kcal, 1.4 standard drinks. Sugar and mixers not included; say so. Not medical advice. Link `dry-january`, `bac`.
403. **Modulo and remainder** (`math`). Inputs: dividend a, divisor b (non-zero, integers, negatives allowed). Formula: floored: q = floor(a/b), r = a - b x q; truncated (as in many languages): q = trunc(a/b), r = a - b x q. Example: 17 and 5 -> q 3, r 2 (both rules). -7 and 3 -> floored q -3, r 2; truncated q -2, r -1. 1,000 and 7 -> q 142, r 6 (7 x 142 = 994). Show a clock example (hour + 50 mod 12). Link `long-division`, `gcf-lcm`.
404. **Power converter** (`conversion`). Inputs: value, unit among W, kW, mechanical hp, metric hp (PS), BTU/h, refrigeration ton. Base unit W: 1 kW = 1,000 W; 1 mechanical hp = 745.6999 W; 1 metric hp = 735.49875 W; 1 BTU/h = 0.29307107 W; 1 ton = 12,000 BTU/h (verify each against a source). Example: 1,500 W -> 5,118.2 BTU/h and 2.01 hp. 2 hp -> 1,491.4 W. 12,000 BTU/h -> 3,516.85 W (3.52 kW). Link `energy-converter`, `personal-heater-cost`.
405. **Hockey goalie stats** (`sports`). Inputs: shots against, goals against, minutes played (default 60). Formula: saves = shots - goals; SV% = saves / shots; GAA = goals x 60 / minutes; shots per 60 = shots x 60 / minutes. Example: 27 shots, 2 goals, 60 min -> 25 saves, SV% .926, GAA 2.00. Second: 34 shots, 3 goals, 125 min -> 31 saves, SV% .912 (31/34 = 0.9118), GAA 1.44 (180 / 125), 16.32 shots per 60. Reject goals > shots. Link `shooting-efficiency`.
406. **Typing speed (WPM)** (`everyday`). Inputs: characters typed (with spaces), minutes, uncorrected errors. Formula (5 characters = 1 word): gross WPM = chars / 5 / minutes; net WPM = gross - errors / minutes; accuracy = 1 - errors / (chars / 5). Example: 1,500 chars, 5 min, 8 errors -> 300 words, gross 60, net 58.4, accuracy 97.3% (292 / 300). Second: 2,400 chars, 10 min, 0 errors -> 48 WPM, 100%. Extra: time to type 1,000 words at 40 WPM = 25 min. Say conventions differ between test sites. Link `word-counter`.
407. **Wall stud count** (`construction`). Inputs: wall length (ft), spacing 16 or 24 in on centre, openings (default 0), extra studs per opening (default 2, labelled editable rule of thumb). Formula: studs = ceil(length x 12 / spacing) + 1 + openings x extra. Example: 12 ft at 16 in -> 144 / 16 = 9, +1 = 10 studs. 20 ft at 16 in -> 15 + 1 = 16. 14 ft at 16 in -> 168 / 16 = 10.5 -> 11 + 1 = 12. 12 ft at 24 in -> 6 + 1 = 7; with 1 door -> 12 at 16 in (10 + 2). Plates, corners and local code not included; say so. Link `drywall`, `board-feet`.
408. **Amdahl's law speedup** (`developer`). Inputs: parallel fraction p (0 to 1, or %), speedup of that part s (or processor count). Formula: overall speedup = 1 / ((1 - p) + p / s); limit as s grows = 1 / (1 - p). Example: p 0.8, s 4 -> 1 / (0.2 + 0.2) = 2.5x; limit 5x. p 0.5, s 10 -> 1 / 0.55 = 1.818x. p 0.95, s infinite -> 20x. Chart: speedup vs s. Link `uptime-downtime`.
409. **Customer lifetime value (LTV)** (`finance`). Inputs: monthly revenue per customer, gross margin %, monthly churn %, optional acquisition cost (CAC). Formula: lifetime months = 1 / churn; margin per month = revenue x margin; LTV = margin per month x lifetime; LTV:CAC = LTV / CAC; payback months = CAC / margin per month. Example: $20, 80%, 5% churn -> 20 months, $16 per month, LTV $320; CAC $80 -> 4.0:1, payback 5 months. Second: $10, 70%, 8% -> 12.5 months, LTV $87.50. Say it assumes constant churn and margin. Link `break-even`, `profit-margin`.
410. **Meeting cost** (`everyday`). Inputs: attendees, average hourly rate per person ($, labelled the user's own estimate), meeting length minutes, times per year (default 1, or per week with 50 working weeks; pick one control and say which). Formula: cost per meeting = attendees x rate x minutes / 60; yearly = cost x times per year; hours of people-time = attendees x minutes / 60. Example: 8 people, $50, 60 min -> $400 per meeting; weekly for 50 weeks -> $20,000, 400 person-hours. Second: 5 people, $60, 30 min -> $150; daily for 250 days -> $37,500. Third: salary to rate = salary / 2,080 ($90,000 -> $43.27). Say it ignores benefits and overhead unless the rate includes them. Link `hours-worked`, `cost-per-use`.
411. **Lottery odds** (`statistics`). Inputs: main numbers drawn k, pool n, optional bonus pool b (default 0), tickets (default 1). Formula: combos = C(n,k) x (b if b > 0 else 1); odds = 1 in combos; P with t tickets = t / combos. Example: Powerball style n 69, k 5, b 26 -> 11,238,513 x 26 = 292,201,338 (1 in 292 million). 6 of 49, no bonus -> 13,983,816. Match exactly 3 of 6 in 6/49: C(6,3) x C(43,3) / C(49,6) = 20 x 12,341 / 13,983,816 = 246,820 / 13,983,816 = 1.765% (1 in 56.66). Say real games differ; user edits the pool sizes. Link `permutation-combination`, `expected-value`.
412. **Torque to horsepower** (`conversion`). Inputs: torque (lb-ft or N m), rpm, direction (torque and rpm to power, or power and rpm to torque). Formula: hp = lb-ft x rpm / 5252.113; kW = N m x rpm x 2 pi / 60 / 1000; 1 lb-ft = 1.355818 N m; 1 hp = 0.7457 kW. Example: 400 lb-ft at 4,000 rpm -> 304.64 hp = 542.33 N m = 227.17 kW. 300 lb-ft at 5,252.113 rpm -> exactly 300 hp (torque and hp curves cross there). Reverse: 250 hp at 6,000 rpm -> 250 x 5252.113 / 6000 = 218.84 lb-ft. Verify constant 5252 against a source. Link `power-converter`.
413. **Tire size comparison** (`everyday`). Inputs: current tire code (width mm, aspect ratio %, rim in) and new tire code. Formula: diameter in = 2 x width x aspect / 100 / 25.4 + rim; true speed at indicated speed = indicated x new / old diameter. Example: 225/45R17 -> 24.97 in; 205/55R16 -> 24.88 in (-0.38%, 60 mph indicated is really 59.77 mph); 235/40R18 -> 25.40 in (+1.72%, 60 indicated is 61.03 mph). Also show width, sidewall height (225 x 0.45 = 101.25 mm). Say load index, clearance and fit are not checked. Link `winter-tire-cost`.
414. **Fibonacci and golden ratio** (`math`). Inputs: n (1 to 78). Formula: F1 = F2 = 1, F(n) = F(n-1) + F(n-2); sum F1..Fn = F(n+2) - 1; ratio F(n+1) / F(n) tends to (1 + sqrt 5) / 2 = 1.6180339887. Example: F10 = 55, sum to 10 = 143; F20 = 6,765, F30 = 832,040; 6,765 / 4,181 = 1.6180340 (F20 / F19). Cap at 78 because F79 passes 2^53. Show the first terms as a table. Link `factorial-calculator`.
415. **Linear regression** (`statistics`). Inputs: x list and y list (equal length, at least 2, x not all equal), optional x to predict. Formula: slope = Sxy / Sxx; intercept = ybar - slope x xbar; R squared = Sxy^2 / (Sxx x Syy). Example: x 1,2,3,4,5; y 2,4,5,4,5 -> xbar 3, ybar 4, Sxy 6, Sxx 10, Syy 6 -> slope 0.6, intercept 2.2, r 0.7746, R squared 0.6; predict x 6 -> 5.8. Second: x 1,2,3; y 2,4,6 -> slope 2, intercept 0, R squared 1. Reject constant x. Chart: scatter with the line. Link `correlation`.
416. **Extension cord voltage drop** (`everyday`). Inputs: wire gauge (18, 16, 14, 12, 10 AWG), cord length ft (one way), load amps, supply volts (default 120). Formula: drop V = 2 x length x amps x R / 1000 where R is ohms per 1,000 ft of copper; percent = drop / volts. Use NEC Chapter 9 Table 8 stranded copper values (18: 7.77, 16: 4.89, 14: 3.14, 12: 1.98, 10: 1.24); VERIFY each against the source before build. Example: 14 AWG, 100 ft, 12 A -> 7.536 V (6.28%); 12 AWG -> 4.752 V (3.96%). 16 AWG, 50 ft, 10 A -> 4.89 V (4.08%). Flag above 3% (common guideline, say so). Not electrical advice; check cord ratings. Link `ohms-law`, `holiday-lights`.
417. **Arc length and sector area** (`math`). Inputs: radius, angle (degrees or radians). Formula: theta rad = deg x pi / 180; arc = r x theta; sector = r^2 x theta / 2; chord = 2 r sin(theta / 2); segment = r^2 (theta - sin theta) / 2. Example: r 10, 60 deg -> arc 10.472, sector 52.360, chord 10.000, segment 9.059. Second: r 5, 2 rad -> arc 10, sector 25. Reject radius <= 0. Link `area`, `volume`.
418. **Gordon growth stock value** (`finance`). Inputs: last annual dividend D0 ($), dividend growth g (%), required return r (%), optional current price. Formula: D1 = D0 x (1 + g); value = D1 / (r - g); implied return = D1 / price + g. Example: D0 2.00, g 4%, r 9% -> D1 2.08, value $41.60; at price 40 implied return 2.08 / 40 + 0.04 = 9.2%. Second: D0 3.00, g 3%, r 8% -> D1 3.09, value $61.80. Reject r <= g with a plain message. Say it is highly sensitive to g and r; not advice. Link `dividend-payout-ratio-calculator`, `yield-on-cost`.
419. **Treasury bill yield** (`finance`). Inputs: face value (default 10,000), purchase price, days to maturity. Formula: discount yield = (F - P) / F x 360 / d; investment (bond-equivalent) yield = (F - P) / P x 365 / d (for terms of 182 days or less; verify against TreasuryDirect). Example: F 10,000, P 9,800, 182 days -> discount 0.0395604 (3.956%), investment 0.040928 (4.093%). Second: F 10,000, P 9,900, 91 days -> discount 0.01 x 360 / 91 = 3.956%, investment 0.010101 x 365 / 91 = 4.052%. Reject P >= F. Interest is exempt from state tax (say so only after checking). Link `apy-calculator`, `cd-ladder`.
420. **Resistors in series and parallel** (`construction`). Inputs: list of resistor values in ohms (2 to 8), mode series or parallel. Formula: series R = sum Ri; parallel 1 / R = sum 1 / Ri. Example: 100, 220, 330 -> series 650 ohm, parallel 56.897 ohm (1/100 + 1/220 + 1/330 = 0.0175758). Second: 1,000 and 1,000 parallel -> 500 ohm. Reject values <= 0. Link `ohms-law`.
421. **Kinetic energy** (`math`). Inputs: mass (kg or lb), speed (m/s, km/h or mph). Formula: KE = m v^2 / 2 in joules; 1 lb = 0.45359237 kg; 1 mph = 0.44704 m/s; 1 km/h = 1/3.6 m/s. Example: 1,500 kg at 25 m/s -> 468,750 J; doubling to 50 m/s -> 1,875,000 J (four times). Second: 0.145 kg at 40 m/s -> 116 J. Reject mass or speed < 0. Link `stopping-distance`.
422. **Projectile motion** (`math`). Inputs: launch speed (m/s), angle (degrees, 0 to 90), launch height (default 0). Formula for height 0 and g 9.80665: range = v^2 sin(2 theta) / g; peak = v^2 sin^2(theta) / (2 g); time = 2 v sin(theta) / g. Example: 20 m/s at 45 deg -> range 40.789 m, peak 10.197 m, time 2.884 s. Second: 20 m/s at 90 deg -> range 0, peak 20.387 m, time 4.077 s. Non-zero launch height uses the quadratic root for time; hand-check one case before build. Ignores air drag; say so. Link `pythagorean-theorem`.
423. **Exponential decay and half-life** (`math`). Inputs: starting amount N0, half-life h, elapsed time t (same unit); optional target amount to find time. Formula: N = N0 x 0.5^(t / h); decay constant = ln 2 / h; time to target = h x log2(N0 / N). Example: 100, h 5,730, t 11,460 -> 25, 75% decayed. Second: 80, h 8 days, t 20 -> 80 x 0.5^2.5 = 14.142; time from 80 to 10 = 24 days; decay constant 0.086643 per day. Reject h <= 0, N0 < 0. Link `log`, `exponent`.
424. **Ceiling fan size by room** (`construction`). Inputs: room length ft, width ft, ceiling height ft (optional, flags low ceilings). Formula: area = L x W; blade span band from area. Use a sourced band table (check ENERGY STAR or a manufacturer guide; draft: up to 75 sq ft 29-36 in, 76-144 sq ft 36-42 in, 144-225 sq ft 44 in, 225-400 sq ft 50-54 in) and label it a rule of thumb. Example: 12 x 14 ft = 168 sq ft -> 44 in band. Second: 10 x 7 = 70 sq ft -> 29-36 in. Do not build until the table is verified. Link `square-footage`.
425. **Standard error of the mean** (`statistics`). Inputs: sample standard deviation s, sample size n, optional target SE. Formula: SE = s / sqrt(n); n needed = (s / SE target)^2 rounded up. Example: s 12, n 36 -> SE 2.0; to reach SE 1 need n 144. Second: s 15, n 100 -> 1.5. Reject n < 2 or s < 0. Explain the difference from standard deviation. Link `standard-deviation`, `confidence-interval`.
426. **Fund year-end distribution tax** (`finance`). Inputs: amount invested, NAV per share, distribution per share, your tax rate on it (user, percent). Formula: shares = amount / NAV; distribution = shares x per-share; tax = distribution x rate; as percent of investment = per-share / NAV; NAV after ex-date = NAV - per-share. Example: $10,000, NAV 50, distribution 4.00, rate 15% -> 200 shares, $800 distribution, $120 tax, 8.0% of the investment, NAV 46, value after = 200 x 46 + 800 = 10,000 (no real gain). Second: $5,000, NAV 25, distribution 1.50, 24% -> 200 shares, $300, $72, 6.0%. Reject NAV <= 0 and distribution > NAV. Explain it applies to taxable accounts, not IRAs or 401(k)s, and that reinvested distributions are still taxed. Not tax advice. Link `tax-loss-harvesting`, `capital-loss-carryover`.
427. **Credit card interest-free days** (`finance`). Inputs: purchase date, statement closing day of month, grace days from close to due date (user, default 25, labelled check your card agreement). Formula: next close = first closing date on or after the purchase date; days to close = close - purchase; interest-free days = days to close + grace; due date = close + grace. Example: purchase Dec 5, close day 20, grace 25 -> close Dec 20, 15 days, 40 interest-free days, due Jan 14. Purchase Dec 21 -> close Jan 20, 30 days, 55 days, due Feb 14. Handle months shorter than the closing day by using the last day. Say it holds only when the previous full statement balance was paid. Link `credit-card-payoff`.
428. **Shift differential pay** (`finance`). Inputs: base hourly rate, differential type (dollars per hour or percent), differential amount, hours a week on the differential, regular hours a week (default 40), weeks a year (default 52). Formula: extra per hour = amount (or base x percent / 100); weekly extra = extra x differential hours; yearly extra = weekly x weeks; blended rate = (base x regular hours + weekly extra) / regular hours. Example: $25 base, +$3/h, 24 of 40 hours -> weekly extra $72, yearly $3,744, blended $26.80. Second: $20, 10%, 40 of 40 -> extra $2.00/h, $80 a week, $4,160 a year, blended $22.00. Reject differential hours > regular hours. Gross pay, before tax. Link `overtime-pay`, `salary-to-hourly`.
429. **Rep challenge total** (`health`). Inputs: reps on day 1, daily increase, number of days (default 30), optional rest-day every N days (skip in v1). Formula: last day = a + (n - 1) d; total = n / 2 x (2a + (n - 1) d); average per day = total / n. Example: start 10, +2 a day, 30 days -> last day 68, total 1,170. Second: start 5, +1, 30 days -> last day 34, total 585. Reject n < 1 or non-integer reps. Add a gentle note to build up and stop if painful; no medical claims. Link `step-goal-ramp`, `workout-volume`.
430. **Generator size from appliance watts** (`construction`). Inputs: rows of appliance name, running watts, starting watts (equal to running if no surge), plus headroom percent (user, default 20). Formula: running total = sum of running; extra surge = largest (starting - running) across rows; minimum = running total + extra surge; recommended = minimum x (1 + headroom). Example: fridge 700/2,200, furnace blower 600/1,500, lights 300/300 -> running 1,600, extra surge max(1,500, 900, 0) = 1,500, minimum 3,100 W, recommended 3,720 W. Second: space heater 1,500/1,500 and lights 300/300 -> 1,800, surge 0, minimum 1,800, recommended 2,160. Warn: wattages come from nameplates, never run a generator indoors (carbon monoxide), use a transfer switch fitted by an electrician. Link `fuel-tank-runtime`, `battery-runtime`.
431. **Half your age plus seven** (`everyday`). Inputs: your age (14 to 120). Formula: youngest = age / 2 + 7; oldest = (age - 7) x 2. Example: 30 -> 22 and 46. 40 -> 27 and 66. 18 -> 16 and 22. Reject age below 14 (the rule breaks down). Copy: a folk rule of thumb, not a law or advice. Keep the tone light for the share card. Link `age`.
432. **Jet lag recovery days** (`health`). Inputs: time zones crossed (1 to 12), direction (east or west), recovery days per zone (user, default 1 east and 0.5 west, labelled a rough rule of thumb, editable). Formula: days = zones x factor, rounded to 0.5. Example: 6 zones east at 1.0 -> 6 days; 3 zones west at 0.5 -> 1.5 days. Needs a source check before shipping or must be worded as a rough guide with no claim of precision. No medical advice. Link `time-zone-converter`, `sleep`.
433. **Pomodoro study planner** (`everyday`). Inputs: number of focus sessions N (default 8), focus minutes (default 25), short break (default 5), long break (default 15), sessions before a long break (default 4). Formula: gaps = N - 1; long breaks = floor(gaps / 4); short = gaps - long; total = N x focus + short x 5 + long x 15. Example: N 8 -> focus 200, 6 short + 1 long = 45, total 245 min (4 h 5 min). N 4 -> focus 100, 3 short = 15, total 115 min. Reject N < 1. Link `practice-hours-goal`, `weighted-grade`.
434. **Shrinkflation true price** (`finance`). Inputs: old size, old price, new size, new price (same unit for both sizes; optional unit label). Formula: old unit price = old price / old size; new unit price = new price / new size; effective increase = new unit / old unit - 1; size cut = 1 - new size / old size. Example: 16 oz at $4.00 -> 14.5 oz at $4.00: unit $0.25 -> $0.27586 per oz, effective increase 10.345%, size cut 9.375%. Second: 12 ct $5.00 -> 10 ct $5.00: $0.41667 -> $0.50 each, +20.0%. Third: 16 oz $4.00 -> 14 oz $4.20: $0.25 -> $0.30, +20.0% (a 5% sticker rise hides a 20% real rise). Reject any size or price <= 0. Neutral copy: it reports the maths, not blame. Link `unit-price`, `price-hike-impact`, `inflation`.
435. **Holiday savings plan** (`finance`). Inputs: holiday budget target, already saved, weeks until the date you need it, deposit rhythm (weekly or every two weeks). Formula: still needed = target - saved; weekly = needed / weeks; biweekly = needed / ceil(weeks / 2). Example: $1,200, $0 saved, 48 weeks -> $25.00 a week or $50.00 every two weeks (24 deposits). Second: $900 target, $150 saved, 30 weeks -> $25.00 a week, $50.00 biweekly (15 deposits). Third: $1,000, $0, 20 weeks -> $50.00 a week. Reject weeks < 1; if saved >= target say it is already covered. Ignores interest; say so. Link `gift-budget`, `savings-challenge`, `holiday-spending-total`.
436. **Kids allowance planner** (`finance`). Inputs: child age, dollars per year of age per week (default 1.00, labelled a popular folk rule, not an expert standard), weeks (default 52), save / spend / give split percents (default 50 / 30 / 20, must total 100). Formula: weekly = age x rate; yearly = weekly x weeks; each jar = weekly x percent. Example: age 9 at $1.00 -> $9.00 a week, $468.00 a year; jars $4.50 / $2.70 / $1.80 a week. Second: age 12 at $1.50 -> $18.00 a week, $936.00 a year. Third: age 6 at $0.50 -> $3.00 a week, $156.00 a year. Reject age < 1 or split not totalling 100. Link `savings-challenge`, `daily-spending-allowance`.
437. **Break-even win rate** (`finance`). Inputs: average win ($), average loss ($), optional your win rate (%). Formula: payoff ratio R = win / loss; break-even win rate = 1 / (1 + R) = loss / (win + loss); expected value per trade = p x win - (1 - p) x loss. Example: win $200, loss $100 -> R 2.0, break-even 33.33%; at 40%: EV = 80 - 60 = +$20 per trade. Second: win $100, loss $100 -> 50%; at 45% EV = 45 - 55 = -$10. Third: win $150, loss $300 -> R 0.5, break-even 66.67%; at 70% EV = 105 - 90 = +$15. Reject win or loss <= 0. Ignores fees and slippage; not advice, past results do not predict future ones. Differentiate from `position-size-calculator` (reward-to-risk output only) and `expected-value`. Link both.
438. **Percentage points vs percent change** (`math`). Inputs: old rate (%), new rate (%). Formula: change in points = new - old; relative change = (new - old) / old x 100. Example: 6.5% -> 7.0%: +0.5 percentage points, +7.692% relative. Second: 4% -> 3%: -1.0 points, -25.0%. Third: 20% -> 25%: +5.0 points, +25.0%. Reject old = 0 for the relative figure (show points only, with a message). Plain-English line like "rates rose half a point, which is 7.7 percent higher, not 0.5 percent". Differentiate from `percentage-change` (single plain values, no points framing). Link `percentage-change`, `percent-difference`.
439. **Layover connection check** (`everyday`). Inputs: scheduled arrival time, arrival delay (min), next departure time, minimum connection time (min, user-set, default 45, labelled check the airline and airport rule), same-day only in v1. Formula: actual arrival = scheduled + delay; time to connect = departure - actual arrival; buffer = time to connect - minimum; delay you can absorb = (departure - scheduled arrival) - minimum. Example: arrive 14:10, +25 min, depart 15:20, minimum 45 -> actual 14:35, 45 min to connect, buffer 0, absorb 25 min (tight). Second: 10:00, 0 delay, 11:30, minimum 60 -> 90 min, buffer 30, absorb 30. Third: 17:00, +50, 17:45, minimum 40 -> arrive 17:50, after departure, buffer -45 relative to minimum and the flight is gone (-5 min). Flag buffer < 0 as likely missed. Not travel advice; gates, immigration and bags vary. Link `airport-leave-time`, `time-zone-converter`.
440. **Toilet paper value** (`everyday`). Inputs: for pack A and B: price, number of rolls, sheets per roll (user reads it off the label; for "mega" or "double" rolls enter the sheet count printed, not the marketing claim). Formula: sheets = rolls x sheets per roll; cost per 100 sheets = price / sheets x 100; saving = 1 - cheaper / dearer. Example: A $24.00, 24 rolls x 200 = 4,800 sheets -> $0.50 per 100; B $18.00, 12 rolls x 400 = 4,800 sheets -> $0.375 per 100; B is 25.0% cheaper. Second: A $15.00 for 12 x 250 = 3,000 sheets -> $0.50 per 100. Reject any value <= 0. Sheet size and ply differ between brands; say so. Differentiate from `unit-price` (generic per-unit); add per-roll-equivalent note. Link `unit-price`, `diaper-cost`.
441. **Cold brew concentrate dilution** (`everyday`). Inputs: final drink volume (ml or oz), dilution (parts water per 1 part concentrate, default 1), optional coffee-to-water brew ratio for making the concentrate (default 1:5 by weight, labelled a common starting point, editable) and batch water. Formula: concentrate = final / (1 + d); water to add = final - concentrate; grounds for a batch = batch water / ratio number. Example: 500 ml at 1:1 -> 250 ml concentrate + 250 ml water. Second: 600 ml at 2 parts water -> 200 ml concentrate + 400 ml water. Third: 500 ml at 1.5 parts -> 200 ml + 300 ml; batch of 1,000 ml water at 1:5 -> 200 g grounds. Reject d < 0. Concentrate yield is lower than water used because grounds absorb water; say so. Differentiate from `coffee-ratio` (hot brew ratio). Link `coffee-ratio`.
